The accepted conditional lift remains available\nas fact04219554e41d9750; the seed existence problem is still OPEN.\n\n\n# Appendix B: all additional max2 scalar tests, reproduced and audited\n\n# Exact survivor of the complete current scalar necessary-test system\n\nStatus: self-contained joint proof DRAFT, not a verifier-accepted fact.\nThis file establishes a limitation of a specified collection of necessary\nconditions. It does NOT supply nonnegative multipliers or a full PSD seed.\nNo optimizer, numerical parameter search, grid, verifier, or installation\nwas used. The two tiny exact rational calculations expand the fixed\npolynomial identities printed below; they do not search for a schedule.\n\n## 1. Exact schedule and variable-capacity family\n\nFor 0<=t<=1/100 define\n\n h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4),\n a=h0-1=2/5, b=h1-1=5/4, c=h2-1=8/25+t, d=h3-1=3/4,\n C=1+sum h=193/25+t,\n P=2+a=12/5, Q=3+a+b=93/20,\n D=2+d=11/4, H=3+c+d=407/100+t.\n\nAll previous max2 scalar tests, and the complete C82 triangle family,\nare STRICTLY satisfied throughout 0<=t<=1/100. In addition, every\nquadratic-curvature test and every C83 cut-repair test is STRICTLY\nsatisfied for every 0<k<=1 throughout 0<=t<=10^(-12).\n\nThus there is an exact survivor at total sum168/25 and a concrete\ninterval of survivors with strictly larger sums. In particular an\nattempt to prove emptiness from these scalar tests cannot succeed.\nThis is not a restriction or a reduction of the original feasible set\nonto a fixed-capacity slice; it is a constructed family inside the\nnecessary-test region.\n\nThe original full interface remains twenty Lambda_ij>=0, i!=j in0..4,\nLambda_ii=0, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i,\nL^T e=(-2h0,-2h1,-2h2,-2h3,2sum h)^T, and\n\n diag(h0(2-h0),...,h3(2-h3),-s(s+2)) + (LT+T^T L^T)/2 >=0,\n s=sum h.\n\nNo Lambda or matrix remainder is claimed in this file. Accepted\n04219554e41d9750 gives the conditional asymptotic implication only\nif an actual full certificate is subsequently constructed.\n\n## 2. Compact box, every cut, and the elementary nonlinear tests\n\nOn our interval, 193/25<=C<=773/100<87/10, 8/25<=c<=33/100,\nand all four shifts are positive. The endpoint bounds a,d<77/97 and\nmiddle bounds b,c<1552559/1081453 hold: b=5/4 gives\n4*1552559-5*1081453=802971>0, and c< b. Thus the strict step box\nfrom the earlier joint draft is satisfied.\n\nThe four cut inequalities, in order, are\n\n Ca <= C-1-a,\n Cb <= P H,\n Cc <= Q D,\n Cd <= C-1-d.                                           (1)\n\nThe first and fourth have margins respectively\n(3/5)C-7/5>0 and C/4-7/4>0. The second has margin\n\n PH-Cb=59/500+(23/20)t>0.                                (2)\n\nFor the third, Cc<=25509/10000<64/25, whereas QD=1023/80>12.\nThese bounds follow by multiplying the upper endpoints of C and c.\nEvery prefix/reversed-prefix cut recurrence is merely a rearrangement\nof (1), so no extra cut has been dropped. The concavity-derived endpoint\nsum condition follows from the two middle cuts: if E=4+a+d, adding\nb<=P(C-P)/(C+P), c<=D(C-D)/(C+D), and using the negative second\nderivative of z(C-z)/(C+z), gives E>=2C(C-1)/(3C+1).\nThe pointwise endpoint and middle cut bounds follow directly from (1).\n\nThe actual unit-curvature product test, suffix product test, and\nchronological clipped-tail test are, respectively,\n\n C a b c d<=1,   C c d<=Q,\n C |bc-(1+d)min(bc,1)|<=P.                              (3)\n\nHere ab=1/2, bc<=33/80<1, and Cc<64/25. Consequently their left\nsides are less than\n\n (3/8)(64/25)=24/25<1,\n (3/4)(64/25)=48/25<Q,\n (15/16)(64/25)=12/5=P.\n\nAll comparisons are strict because 25509<25600.\nAt t=0 the exact full product is579/625 and the exact clipped\nleft side is579/250, whose gap from P is21/250.\n\nThe scalar mixed first-prefix test has u=7/5 and eta=b/H. Its expression\nis\n\n u^2+u-1+u(2-u^2)eta-a C^2 eta^2\n   =59/25+(7/125)eta-(2/5)(C eta)^2.\n\nBy (1), C eta<P=12/5. Therefore this expression exceeds7/125>0.\nIts domain a eta<1 also follows since a eta<ab/4=1/8.\n\nFor the two unit-curvature cut repairs, the base energy is\nV0=(1+prefix sum)^2-C^2 eta^2>0 by (1). The auxiliary terminal\nvalue q is q=1/(2H) at cut1, and q=-c/(2D) at cut2. Hence\n\n C|q|=C/(2H)<1 at cut1, since 2H-C=C-73/10>0,\n C|q|=Cc/(2D)<(64/25)/(11/2)<1 at cut2.\n\nThe auxiliary energy1-C^2 q^2 is strictly positive. Thus every repair\nexpression beta V0+eta(1+eta)(1-C^2 q^2) is positive for beta>=0,\nincluding the beta=0 case. No denominator with an unproved sign is used.\n\n## 3. All three adjacent triangles and the first backward lift\n\nThe adjacent-triangle tests, with the selected consecutive shifts\nB,E, preceding capacity P0 and following capacity D0, read\n\n B E [1+B(P0+C)/P0^2] <= D0.                            (4)\n\nFor the first pair, its left side is\nab[1+a(1+C)] < (1/2)[1+(2/5)*9]=23/10<H.\nFor the middle pair, bc<=33/80, P+C<11, and P^2>5. Therefore its\nleft side is less than\n\n (33/80)[1+(5/4)*11/5]=99/64<11/4=D.\n\nFor the last pair, cd<=99/400, Q+C<13 and Q^2>21. Its left side\nis less than\n\n (99/400)[1+(33/100)*13/21]\n <(99/400)(5/4)=99/320<1.\n\nThe strengthened middle triangle has the additional nonnegative\nparameter max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0). In this family\n\n a^2+2a-1=-1/25,\n\nso this parameter equals zero and the strengthened test is exactly\nthe already strict middle inequality. This is an actual branch check,\nnot deletion of an active max term.\n\n## 4. Both last-pair lifts and the earliest-normal guard\n\nDefine the last-triangle quantities\n\n eta=c/D,\n K=c^2 d+(c+d+2)(1-cd)=c+d+2-cd(d+2),\n r=c^2 d/K,\n Hpre=2+a+b=73/20, Apre=1+a+b=53/20,\n Z=(c+r)/(1+d),\n qlate=(1+(1-b)eta)/b,\n qearly=[eta+(Apre eta-1)Z]/Apre,\n Jpre=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 max(qlate,qearly).\n\nThe required inequalities are C^2 r<=min(Jpre,Q^2) and\n\n C^2 max(eta^2,r)<=Jquad,\n Jquad=[(1-b eta)Q^2+(1+eta)/(a^2 b)]/(1+b).             (5)\n\nAll denominators are positive: eta<3/25, cd<1/4, c+d+2>3, so\nK>3-(1/4)(11/4)=37/16>2. Also\n\n r < ((33/100)^2*(3/4))/2 <1/20,\n eta^2<9/625<1/20, C^2<64.\n\nThus every left side in (5) and C^2 r is less than16/5.\nOn the right, qlate>(1-3/100)/(5/4)=97/125>3/4,\nHpre>7/2,Hpre<4, and eta<3/25. Therefore\n\n Jpre >=1-2Hpre eta+Hpre^2 qlate\n       >1-24/25+147/16>9.\n\nAlso Q^2>21. For Jquad use b eta<3/20, a^2 b=1/5 and1+b=9/4:\n\n Jquad >[(17/20)*21+5]/(9/4)=457/45>10.\n\nThese bounds already retain the full max in Jpre. In fact the earliest\nnormal is inactive here for a proved reason: Apre eta<(53/20)(3/25)<1.\nSince Z>0, the explicit formula gives\nqearly<eta/Apre<eta<3/25<3/4<qlate. No shortcut based only on\ncd<1 or b eta<1 has been made.\nThe other max is also correctly determined: d=3/4>sqrt(2)-1, so\nr>eta^2. This follows from\n\nd(d+2)^2-K=(c+d+2)(d(d+2)-1)>0.\n\nAt t=0, K=241/100,r=192/6025,eta=32/275,\nqlate=1068/1375, and Jquad=529303/49500.\n\n## 5. The entire C82 interior-edge triangle family survives\n\nFor clarity, the interface is rederived here in this family's notation.\nTake U>0,z>0, with\n\n U>=b,\n z^2 <= (U+1)(a U-1),\n z^2 >= c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2.                              (6)\n\nUse vertices p=(U,0), q=(-1,z), w=(-bc/D,0), and the convex\nfunction F(x)=max_{v in conv(p,q,w)}(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).\nIts gradient is projection onto that triangle. The original jets are\n\n x0=((1+a)U-1,z), g0=(U,0),\n x1=(-1,z), g1=(-1,z),\n x2=(b,-bz), g2=(b,0),\n x3=(-bc,-bz), g3=(-bc/D,0),\n x4=(-bc/D,-bz), g4=(-bc/D,0).\n\nThe initial normal comparison with q is z^2-(U+1)(aU-1)<=0;\nthe other initial comparison is nonpositive since aU-1>=0.\nAt1 the normal is0. At2 its normal is(0,-bz), and U>=b places\ng2 on the base. At3 the comparison with q is\nb[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0, and the base comparison is nonpositive.\nAt4 the normal is again vertical downward. These checks include every\ntriangle vertex and the optimum0 in its base, hence all original\ninterpolation pairs. The four GD identities follow by subtraction.\nSaturated values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 telescope, giving energy\n\n E(U,z)=((1+a)U-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2(bc/D)^2.           (7)\n\nThe complete parameter-eliminated C82 test is the infimum of (7)\nover (6), including zero-height limits. We bound the whole domain,\nso no unproved formula for that infimum is needed.\nSince a=2/5, (6) forces U>=5/2. Also 1-b^2=-9/16<0, so\nE(U,z)>=q(U), where\n\n q(U)=((7/5)U-1)^2-(9/16)(U+1)((2/5)U-1)-C^2(bc/D)^2.\n\nIts quadratic coefficient is347/200>0 and\nq'(5/2)=(7/5)(71/16)>0. Thus q is strictly increasing for U>=5/2.\nUsing Cc<64/25 and b/D=5/11 gives\n\n E(U,z)>=q(5/2)=25/4-(5 Cc/11)^2\n              >25/4-25/16=75/16>0.\n\nThis includes all branches, boundary limits and the exact infimum.\nIt proves survival of C82, not sufficiency of any scalar test.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "finding_ids": ["b1592acfdb474277", "399341aaa6b09bdb", "19f1d27c0eb8a588"], "artifact": "workers/max3/research/c87_complete_joint_survivor_obstruction.md", "target_claim": "The entire C82/C83 curvature family plus the prior scalar/triangle/backward tests excludes all four-step words at sum>=168/25.", "status": "refuted_in_complete_joint_draft", "scope": "An exact interval survives the old necessary tests but is excluded by a new actual legal function; full global seed feasibility remains open.", "new_model_evaluations": 0, "new_word_proposals": 0, "new_fact_submit": 0, "new_polynomial_replays": 0}, "glossary": {"h_t": "In this finding, the explicit already-shared family h(t)=(7/5,9/4,33/25+t,7/4) for0<=t<=1/100.", "Vfull": "Exact primitive energy of the full scalar quadratic at the given curvature.", "Dext": "Explicit nonnegative energy difference coefficient when the quadratic prefix is extended through a step with curvature times step at most1.", "A3": "Normalized three-step prefix energy sum used to prove the full quadratic sign on the all-negative-factor interval."}}

Definitions and the exact core being decided\n\nWrite\n\n    h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d), C=5+a+b+c+d,\n    P=2+a, Q=3+a+b, D=2+d, H=3+c+d,\n    k=max((1+a)(a^2+2a-1)/a,0), R=P^2-k,\n    eta_mid=b/H, J=R-k*eta_mid.\n\nThe precise endpoint core is the following set of algebraic inequalities:\n\n    193/25 <= C < 87/10,\n    0<a,d<77/97, 0<b,c<1552559/1081453,\n    bc<1,\n    Ca<=C-1-a, Cb<=P*H, Cc<=Q*D, Cd<=C-1-d,\n    ab[1+a(C+1)]<=H,\n    bc[R+b(P+C)]<=D*J,\n    C^2*eta_mid^2<=J,\n    Ccd<=Q, Cabcd<=1, Cbcd<=P,\n    Cbd<=P(2+c),\n    cd[1+c(Q+C)/Q^2]<=1,\n    C^2*b^2*d^2<=R(2+c)^2-k*b*(2+c).                 (1)\n\nHere Cbcd denotes C*b*c*d, and similarly for other concatenated factors.\nOn bc<1 these are exactly the complete lower-clipping inequalities,\nall four cuts, the stated first and middle triangles, mixed first cut,\nsuffix and unit-quadratic tests, last triangle, and lifted return.\nNo claim of sufficiency for the full primitive certificate is made.\n\nFor comparison with the endpoint notation, put\n\n    Ksum=C-5-a-b=c+d, y=Cb/P, m=max(1,aP),\n    Bprod=min(1/(m*y),Q/C), r_end=(C-1)/(C+1),\n    A=b*(R+b*(P+C))/J,\n    Lcut=(C*Ksum-2Q)/(C+Q),\n    Ltri=(A*Ksum-2)/(A+1).\n\nThe exact admissible endpoint interval has\n\n    d>=max(0,Ksum-1/b,Lcut,Ltri),\n    d<=min(Ksum,r_end,Ulift),\n    0<d<Ksum, d>Ksum-1/b, d(Ksum-d)<=Bprod.            (2)\n\nThe last triangle is additionally retained. No endpoint belonging only\nto the closure of one of the three strict inequalities is admitted.\nWhen k=0, as will be proved on our survivor region, the actual stronger\nreturn endpoint is exactly\n\n    Ulift=(Ksum+2)/(1+y).                              (3)\n\nThus using (3) below is a proved inactive-lift branch, not deletion of\nan active term. The original lifted inequality in (1) is checked too.\n\n## 2. A four-dimensional region of strict core survivors\n\nLet B_surv be the following explicit region in the four shifts:\n\n    |a-2/5|<=1/1000, |b-4/5|<=1/1000,\n    |c-6/5|<=1/1000, |d-8/25|<=1/1000,\n    C=5+a+b+c+d>=193/25.                              (4)\n\nIt contains the exact point\n\n    (a,b,c,d)=(2/5,4/5,6/5,8/25),\n    h=(7/5,9/5,11/5,33/25), C=193/25.                (5)\n\nIt also contains a rectangular region with all C,a,b,d independently\nfree: take\n\n    193/25 <= C <= 193/25+1/4000,\n    |a-2/5|<=1/4000, |b-4/5|<=1/4000,\n    |d-8/25|<=1/4000,\n    c=C-5-a-b-d.                                     (6)\n\nIndeed the induced c differs from 6/5 by at most 1/1000. This is not a\nfixed-capacity reduction; capacities strictly above the threshold and\na nonempty open four-dimensional set occur.\n\nThroughout (4),\n\n    399/1000<=a<=401/1000,\n    799/1000<=b<=801/1000,\n    1199/1000<=c<=1201/1000,\n    319/1000<=d<=321/1000,\n    193/25<=C<=1931/250<31/4<8.                       (7)\n\nThe compact-domain bounds in (1) hold strictly. In particular a,d<1/2,\nb<1, and c<5/4<1552559/1081453, since\n4*1552559-5*1081453=802971>0. Also 1/2<77/97.\nFurther,\n\n    a^2+2a-1 <= (1401/1000)^2-2 <0,\n    aP <= (401/1000)(2401/1000)=962801/1000000<1.\n\nConsequently k=0, R=J=P^2, and m=1 throughout (4).\n\nEvery individual core inequality is checked next.\n\n### 2.1. The cuts and their independent first-cut conditions\n\nFor either u=a or u=d, u<1/2 and C>3 give\n\n    (C+1)u < (C+1)/2 < C-1.\n\nThis proves both endpoint cuts strictly. Since C<8, b<1, P>2 and H>4,\nthe second cut has Cb<8<PH. The third cut follows from\n\n    Cc < (31/4)(121/100)=3751/400,\n    QD > (419/100)(231/100)=96789/10000,\n    96789-25*3751=3014>0.\n\nSince k=0 and eta_mid=b/H, the mixed inequality C^2*eta_mid^2<J is\nthe square of the strict second cut. The first adjacent triangle obeys\n\n    ab[1+a(C+1)] < (1/2)[1+(1/2)*9]=11/4<H.\n\nThe independent endpoint condition a<r_end is the first endpoint cut.\n\n### 2.2. The middle triangle, including its lift\n\nWith k=0 the lifted middle triangle is exactly\n\n    bc[1+b(P+C)/P^2]<=D.\n\nThe bounds in (7) give\n\n    bc<=962001/1000000,\n    b(P+C)<=8110125/1000000,\n    P^2>=5755201/1000000,\n    D>=2319/1000.\n\nTherefore its left side is at most\n\n    (962001/1000000)*(13865326/5755201)<2319/1000.\n\nThe exact integer difference after clearing positive denominators is\n\n    2319000*5755201 - 962001*13865326\n        =7853641674>0.                                (8)\n\nThis proves strict survival uniformly, rather than relying on a sampled\nvalue of the middle inequality.\n\n### 2.3. Products, both return tests, and the last triangle\n\nThe bc boundary is strict because bc<=962001/1000000<1.\nUsing bc<963/1000, d<=321/1000 and C<31/4 gives\n\n    Cbcd < (31*963*321)/4000000\n          =9582813/4000000\n          <2399/1000<=P.                              (9)\n\nThe last strict comparison has numerator difference 13187>0.\nBecause aP<1, (9) also gives Cabcd<aP<1. The suffix inequality follows\nfrom C<8, c<5/4, d<1/3 and Q>4:\n\n    Ccd<10/3<4<Q.\n\nFor the return inequality, C<8, b<1, d<1/2 give Cbd<4, whereas\nP>2 and c>1 give P(2+c)>6. Since k=0 this proves the stronger lifted\nreturn in (1) strictly as well, and therefore d<Ulift.\n\nFinally Q>4 and C<8 give (Q+C)/Q^2<3/4. Thus\n\n    cd[1+c(Q+C)/Q^2]\n       <(5/12)[1+(5/4)(3/4)]=155/192<1.               (10)\n\nThis proves every inequality in (1), all strictly except the allowed\ncapacity boundary C=193/25.\n\n### 2.4. Both endpoint components and all their open boundaries\n\nFor the projected values C,a,b arising in (4),\n\n    Ksum>151/100, Ksum<153/100,\n    Bprod<=P/(Cb)<2/5.\n\nFor the second inequality use P<=2401/1000 and\nCb> (193/25)(799/1000); these imply P/(Cb)<2/5 by direct multiplication.\nConsequently Delta=Ksum^2-4Bprod>9/4-8/5=13/20>0. Both product roots\n\n    d_minus=(Ksum-sqrt(Delta))/2,\n    d_plus=(Ksum+sqrt(Delta))/2\n\nare therefore real and distinct. Since sqrt(Delta)>4/5, d_plus>23/20.\nEvery admissible endpoint has d<=r_end<1, so the WHOLE upper product\ncomponent is empty for these projected values. Our actual d<1/3 lies\nbelow Ksum/2 and satisfies d(Ksum-d)<Bprod by (9) and the suffix test.\nIt therefore lies strictly below d_minus, in the surviving lower\ncomponent. Its positivity, c>0 and strict bc<1 already checked in\n(7)--(9) give every open boundary in (2), including d>Ksum-1/b.\nThe strict third cut and middle triangle give d>Lcut and d>Ltri.\n\nThe last-triangle polynomial does not introduce a hidden second\nbranch on this particular projected region. Writing v=(Q+C)/Q^2<3/4,\nfor every 0<=d<=Ksum and c=Ksum-d one has\n\n    d*c*(1+v*c)<=Ksum^2/4 + (3/4)*(4*Ksum^3/27)\n               <(153/100)^2/4+(153/100)^3/9\n               =491589/500000<1.                    (11)\n\nThe product maxima used here follow from\ndc<=Ksum^2/4 and d*c^2<=4*Ksum^3/27; the latter follows by\ndifferentiating c^2(Ksum-c) on [0,Ksum]. Thus both (15) and (16) of\nthe preceding endpoint draft are actually decided on this region:\nthe upper product component is empty, the lower one has genuine strict\nsurvivors, and the last-triangle exclusion is inactive. No closure\npoint or inadmissible endpoint was substituted for a survivor.\n\n\n\nFull-interface warning: complete joint draft d3abee1b17da52da includes the actual full-pair rejection proof, not an assumption of coefficient infeasibility. At the central point its two legal witnesses have exact primitive energies -798908/525625 and -79345189/7148500. {"id": "1a5dead163a86858", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T09:38:28.986194+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "NEW sharp boundary of the short-block proof: an exact ORDINARY three-unit-step full certificate has canonical unsigned reverse lambda_rev,02=-48/168373<0, targeting the NONTERMINAL query2. This sharpens the previously stated n>=4 supplied-array example; the word is self-reversing and has an ordinary alternate certificate, so full-cone reversal is NOT refuted.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED exact counterexample to an AUTOMATIC MAP SIGN CLAIM, not to existential reversal. For h of sum s, C=1+s, Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 and V_h=2sum h_i(f_i-f_n)+sum h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sC||g_n||^2. The physical GD recursion is x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i. The grounded matrix is K=L+diag(hbar)-hbar e_n^T+diag(w), where L is the row Laplacian of lambda, hbar=(h,0), w=(h,1), and e_n is the terminal unit vector. The canonical unsigned reverse has lambda_rev,ij=-4C^2(Delta K^(-1)Delta)_ij. All definitions and coefficient arithmetic are included below; Section4 of the attached local joint artifact supplies the congruence proof, while the sign refutation itself follows solely from the displayed original certificate and exact linear-system solution.\n\n## 9. The automatic nonterminal-sign argument stops at three steps\n\nThere is an exact ordinary THREE-step certificate whose unsigned reverse\nhas a negative NONTERMINAL-targeting coefficient. This is a boundary of\nthe particular proof in Section5, not a counterexample to cone reversal:\nthe word below is its own reversal and has other ordinary certificates.\nThe previously shared obstruction37622e6cdcb606e6 was stated for n>=4;\nits local perturbation already yields the following n=3 example, whose\nfull identity and every needed integer multiplication are checked here.\n\nTake h=(1,1,1), C=4, indices0,...,3. The only nonzero multipliers are\n\n lambda01=29/15, lambda10=58/15,\n lambda12=469/60, lambda21=469/40,\n lambda23=717/40, lambda32=239/10,\n lambda02=1/30, lambda20=1/10,\n lambda13=1/40, lambda31=1/20.\n\nEvery multiplier is positive; lambda03=lambda30=0. The out/in sums at\nnodes0,1,2,3 are respectively\n\n (59/30,119/30), (281/24,329/24),\n (119/4,127/4), (479/20,359/20).\n\nThus the value flow is exactly(-2,-2,-2,6). The full identity is\n\n V_h+sum lambda_ij Q_ij\n   =(59/60)||g0-g1||^2+(1/60)||g0-g2||^2\n     +(929/240)||g1-g2||^2+(1/80)||g1-g3||^2\n     +(143/16)||g2-g3||^2.                            (15)\n\nFor a direct coefficient check, a unit-step adjacent forward residual\nhas quadratic part -||gi||^2/2-<gi,g_(i+1)>+||g_(i+1)||^2/2;\nits reverse has (||gi||^2+||g_(i+1)||^2)/2.\nA distance-two forward residual has quadratic part\n-||gi||^2/2-<gi,g_(i+1)>-<gi,g_(i+2)>+||g_(i+2)||^2/2;\nits reverse has (||gi||^2+||g_(i+2)||^2)/2+<g_(i+2),g_(i+1)>.\nThe five nonzero cross coefficients of the left side in(15) are\n-59/30,-1/30,-929/120,-1/40,-143/8, exactly those on its right.\nThe four diagonal coefficients on each side are\n1,73/15,513/40,179/20. For example the nonterminal diagonal at i is\n1+sum_(j<i)lambda_ij, and the terminal diagonal is -15+sum_j lambda_3j.\nThis checks the identity on all four independent formal gradients.\n\nHere w=e, so the grounded K from Section4 is explicitly\n\n K=[[119/30,-29/15,-1/30,-1],\n    [-58/15,329/24,-469/60,-41/40],\n    [-1/10,-469/40,127/4,-757/40],\n    [0,-1/20,-239/10,499/20]].\n\nIts row sums are1 and off-diagonals are nonpositive, so it is invertible\nby the same strict diagonal-dominance argument. Define\n\n z=(-169089,1434,-3,0)^T/673492.\n\nThen Kz=e1-e0. To check each coordinate, the numerator row products,\nusing respectively denominators30,120,40,20, are\n\n -119*169089-58*1434+3=-30*673492,\n 464*169089+1645*1434+938*3=120*673492,\n 4*169089-469*1434-1270*3=0,\n -1434+2*239*3=0.\n\nFor three steps use Delta_ij=1_(j=3-i)-1_(j=2-i), with absent indices\nzero. Row0 of Delta is(e3-e2)^T and column2 is e1-e0. Therefore\n\n (Delta K^(-1)Delta)_02=(e3-e2)^T z=3/673492>0.\n\nThe unsigned-reversal coefficient is consequently\n\n lambda_rev,02=-4C^2*(3/673492)=-48/168373<0.            (16)\n\nIndex2 is NONTERMINAL because the reversed terminal is3. The full\ncongruence and value cancellation still hold: the derivation in Section4\nuses only K e=w, K^T e=C e_n, K(T_h e)=-Cw+2C^2 e_n, and\nDelta T_rev=T_h^T Delta. These identities have identical direct\nrow checks in dimension4 (row i<n is row n-i minus row n-i-1;\nrow n is row0). Thus(16) is a coefficient-sign failure, not a loss\nof flow or PSD.\n\nThis establishes the exact limit of the automatic sign statement:\nat most two positive steps allow the preceding reduction to only\nterminal defects; already three steps admit a bad nonterminal output.\nIt does not show that every certificate of this word has that defect.\nIndeed the unit word has the separate full ordinary identity\n\n V_h+sum_(k=0)^2[2(k+1)^2 Q_(k,k+1)\n                    +2(k+1)(k+2)Q_(k+1,k)]\n       =sum_(k=0)^2(k+1)^2||g_k-g_(k+1)||^2.\n\nHence its reversed full cone is nonempty. No legal negative-energy\nwitness against its reversal can exist if these identities are correct.\nThe general four-step existential reversal problem remains precisely\nbeyond this automatic-map-plus-terminal-repair route.\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "finding_ids": ["e59fe188876aa98d", "37622e6cdcb606e6"], "artifact": "research/full_primitive_reversal_short_blocks.md#section-9"}, "glossary": {"lambda_rev": "The signed array produced by the stated inverse-difference reversal map.", "det2": "The positive leading2x2 grounded determinant in the two-step sign proof; not required for this three-step counterexample."}}
{"id": "79c081ed1cfa9d52", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T10:47:36.330535+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max30 exact anchor counterexample: subtracting the new31 terminal residual makes sample31 residual-zero but does NOT restore equality with the earlier anchor27. {"id": "0a7652c1f2280e24", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T10:47:53.925465+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Draft counterexample to generic value rigidity: saturated projection-function samples need NOT have forced values. For EVERY positive-excess four-step word, sufficiently nonboundary C82 triangles admit a strictly smaller initial value at exactly the same queried positions and gradients, still with all30 original/optimum pairs and a real closest minimizer0. This does not imply improvement over the fully optimized C82 family.", "evidence": "Target claim: saturated values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 are always rigid once actual queried positions and gradients and the zero optimum are fixed. Status: refuted by the following joint construction, UNVERIFIED. Use arbitrary a,b,c,d>0 in Section6 below. Choose v>max(b,1/a,R), where R=[1-a+sqrt((1+a)^2+4a ell)]/(2a), and choose z^2=(ell+Htri(v))/2. Then t=a v-1>0, v>b, ell<z^2<Htri(v), so BOTH arguments of Dprice in(23) are strictly positive. Equations(22)--(25) give two legal choices of values on identical physical positions and gradients; the second decreases only f0 by Dprice>0. The explicit conjugate function described below realizes the changed values and preserves closest minimizer0 and actual GD. Every assumption holds, while the claimed rigidity fails. The construction uses symbolic parameters only, with no word evaluation, sweep, or solver. Its impact is to block extension of the unit-curvature/C83 rigidity result to arbitrary projection geometries; optimized-family novelty still requires the minimization and comparisons in the extended joint draft.\n\n## Actual interpolant and its minimizers\n The optimized finite values give one static function and real minima\n\nThis section also explains why(5), without a fixed optimum vertex, is\nnot merely a formal jet result. For any legal replacement values f'_i\non the finite samples, set\n\n    y_i=x_i-g_i, beta_i=||g_i||^2/2-f'_i,\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+max_(i in I){<y_i,v>+beta_i},\n    F(x)=sup_v{<x,v>-H(v)}.                              (8)\n\nThis is the construction in accepted1252b8823bb5a290, written out here\nto identify its minimizers. H is finite, continuous, 1-strongly convex,\nand has quadratic growth. The supremum has one maximizer v(x). Strong\nsubgradient inequalities give\n\n    <x-x',v(x)-v(x')>>=||v(x)-v(x')||^2,\n\nand the maximizing inequalities give\n\n    0<=F(x')-F(x)-<v(x),x'-x>\n      <=<v(x')-v(x),x'-x><=||x'-x||^2.\n\nThus F is everywhere finite, convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with\ngradient v(x). The all-pair inequalities say that at v=g_i the i-th\nquadratic in H is active and has gradient x_i. Consequently v(x_i)=g_i\nand F(x_i)=f'_i. This is ONE fixed F used for all steps.\n\nLet beta_max=max_i beta_i, and A={i:beta_i=beta_max}. The exact minimizer\nset of F is\n\n    argmin F=conv{y_i:i in A},   min F=-beta_max.          (9)\n\nIndeed gradient F(x)=0 is equivalent to x being a subgradient of H at0.\nThe subgradients there are exactly the convex hull of the active affine\nslopes y_i. To check the reverse inclusion without an unproved formula,\nif x is outside that convex hull, a separating vector u has\n<x,u> greater than every <y_i,u> for i in A. For sufficiently small\npositive t, inactive affine pieces remain inactive at tu, and\nH(tu)-H(0)=t max_(i in A)<y_i,u>+t^2||u||^2/2,\ncontradicting the subgradient inequality for x. The forward inclusion\nfollows by convexly combining the active supporting inequalities.\n\nIn particular a real minimizer always exists, even if none was fixed in\nthe graph. The true initial distance is exactly\n\n    dist(x_0,conv{y_i:i in A}).                           (10)\n\nNo arbitrary radius or changed function is inserted into the GD run.\nThe specific families below either preserve the optimum0 or identify\nthe closest minimizer explicitly.\n\n\n\n## Complete triangle and all pairs\n C82 triangle: exact nonzero price and a real interpolant\n\nHere keep canonical h=(1+a,1+b,1+c,1+d) with a,b,c,d>0, and let\n\n    C=5+a+b+c+d, D=2+d, eta=bc/D,\n    ell=max{0,c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2},\n    t=a v-1, Htri(v)=(v+1)(a v-1).\n\nUse parameters v>=b and z>=0 with ell<=z^2<=Htri(v). For nondegenerate\ntriangles take z>0; the boundary z=0 is legal by the same normal checks.\nThese inequalities imply t>=0. Define\n\n    X_0=((1+a)v-1,z), G_0=(v,0),\n    X_1=G_1=(-1,z),\n    X_2=(b,-bz),     G_2=(b,0),\n    X_3=(-bc,-bz),   G_3=(-eta,0),\n    X_4=(-eta,-bz),  G_4=(-eta,0),\n    F_i=<X_i,G_i>-||G_i||^2/2.                         (22)\n\nEvery GD equation has the original four step lengths. For completeness,\nK=conv{G_0,G_1,G_3} contains0. At target0 the residual normal is(t,z).\nIts comparisons with G_1,G_2,G_3 and0 are, respectively,\n\n    z^2-(v+1)t, (b-v)t, -(eta+v)t, -v t,\n\nall nonpositive. At target1 the normal vanishes. At target2 the normal\nis(0,-bz), so all horizontal-gradient sources give zero and source1\ngives -bz^2. At target3 the normal is(-(1+d)eta,-bz). Its source1\nresidual is b*[c(1+d)(D-bc)/D^2-z^2]<=0; its horizontal-gradient\nsources and optimum source are nonpositive. At target4 the normal is\n(0,-bz), again giving zero for horizontal-gradient sources and -bz^2\nfor source1. Every residual into the optimum is zero. Thus all finite\nand optimum pairs hold, and(16) gives one actual base function.\n\nThe finite zero-cost arcs4->1,4->2,4->3 show d_1=d_2=d_3=d_4=0.\nThe least cost into0 is\n\n    Dprice=min{(v-b)t, (v+1)t-z^2}.                      (23)\n\nThe other incoming costs, (v+eta)t and v t from the optimum, are no\nsmaller than(v-b)t. Every path into0 has a final incoming arc, while\neach minimizing source1 or2 is reached at cost zero. Therefore\n\n    d_0=d_0^*=Dprice, all other d_i=d_i^*=0,\n    Pflow=2(1+a)Dprice.                                 (24)\n\nThe complete fixed-geometry value optimum is attained by\n\n    F'_0=F_0-Dprice, F'_i=F_i for1<=i<=4, F'_star=0.      (25)\n\nAll incoming residuals at0 increase by Dprice and stay nonpositive\nby(23); all outgoing residuals from0 decrease. All other pairs are\nunchanged. This is a direct check of all thirty ordered pairs including\nthe optimum, independently confirming the path calculation.\n\nFor the actual function use(8) with these values and the optimum.\nIts affine constants beta_i are\n\n    beta_0=-v t+Dprice<=-b t,\n    beta_1=beta_2=beta_4=beta_star=0,\n    beta_3=-(1+d)eta^2<0.\n\nWhen t>0, the active normals in(9) are0 and(0,-bz). Therefore its\nminimizer set is the segment joining these two points, and0 is the\nclosest minimizer to X_0, whose second coordinate z is nonnegative.\nIf t=0, z=0 and the initial normal also vanishes, with the same\nclosest minimizer0. Thus the true squared initial distance is\n\n    ((1+a)v-1)^2+z^2.\n\nThe base telescoping energy and the exact optimized energy are\n\n    Ebase(v,z)=((1+a)v-1)^2+(1-b^2)z^2-C^2 eta^2,\n    Eprice(v,z)=Ebase(v,z)-2(1+a)Dprice.                 (26)\n\nIn particular a generic claim that saturated projection values are\nalways rigid is FALSE. Formula(23) gives a strictly positive price\nwhenever v>b, t>0, and z^2<(v+1)t. This is a statement about a fixed\nactual geometry, not yet about improvement over an optimized family.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "related_findings": ["e9ef65394000dcb9"], "target_claim": "Generic rigidity of saturated projection values", "status": "refuted_as_joint_draft_only", "numerical_evaluations": 0, "fact_submit": 0}, "glossary": {"Dprice": "Strictly positive permitted decrease of the initial C82 value, as fully defined in the proof.", "Htri": "(v+1)(a v-1), the upper normal bound in the C82 construction."}}

{"id": "cc3e09ac422a046c", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T11:00:59.826938+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C93 scope attack: even the exact all-curvature resolvent screen does NOT upper-bound changing-region static ridge dynamics by global Huber. A fixed noncommuting quadratic-plus-one-ridge function on h=(1,4,1,4,1) has two actual sign changes and true payoff9/20<V<1/2. It beats K5=9/52 but loses to the accepted scalar multi-cut73/48; this is a scope example, not new lower-bound strength.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED self-contained scope counterexample, proved jointly in ee38a1e336bfa5a2. This is not a new competitive lower-bound result.\n\nLet h=(1,4,1,4,1), S=11, and P(t)=(1-t)^3(1-4t)^2. For 0<=t<=1/4, P(t)^2<=exp(-22t)<=1/(1+22t). For 1/4<=t<=1, put z=1-t. The function z^3(3-4z)^2 on[0,3/4] has derivative z^2(3-4z)(9-20z) and maximum6561/50000<1/7. Hence(1+22t)P(t)^2<=23/49<1. Thus the full polynomial condition used in C93 holds on this SAME word.\n\nDefine phi(0)=0, phi'(x)=max(-3/4,min(x,1/2)). Fix ANY0<epsilon<=10^-6, put s=epsilon^2, and fix one static function F(x,y)=(epsilon*x+y)^2/4+(1-s)phi(x). Start at(5/4,0). Its outer Hessian A=(1/2)[s,epsilon;epsilon,1] is PSD; its core Hessian C=A+(1-s)e1e1^T is PSD and I-C=(1/2)(epsilon,-1)(epsilon,-1)^T is PSD. The derivative is continuous at both boundaries, and along any segment the gradient increment is a matrix between A and C times the displacement. Thus F is convex and globally1-smooth. Every summand is nonnegative, phi vanishes only at0, and F has unique minimizer(0,0); true initial squared distance is25/16. A and C do not commute when epsilon>0.\n\nAt epsilon0 the exact first coordinates on the physical word are(5/4,3/4,-5/4,-1/2,3/2,1); second coordinates vanish. Gradients have first coordinates(1/2,1/2,-3/4,-1/2,1/2,1/2). The gates are positive outer,positive outer,negative outer,core,positive outer,positive outer. Every coordinate is at least1/4 inside its stated region.\n\nFor every baseline sample z_i^0, ||z_i^0||<=3/2 and ||gradF_epsilon(z_i^0)-gradF_0(z_i^0)||<=3epsilon. This follows from ||A_epsilon-A_0||<=epsilon and |phi'|<=3/4. If E_i is the actual perturbation in position, global1-smoothness gives E_(i+1)<=(1+h_i)E_i+3epsilon h_i. Therefore E_i<=3epsilon(product_(t<i)(1+h_t)-1)<=597epsilon<1/1000, because the full product is2*5*2*5*2=200. Every claimed gate persists on the actual static-function trajectory. Its first position and gradient coordinates each change sign TWICE, with signs+,+,-,-,+,+; the actual queried Hessians at indices2,3,4 are A,C,A.\n\nThe first two actual gradients are g0=(1/2+s/8,5epsilon/8) and g1=((1-s)(1/2+s/16),epsilon(1-s)/16). Their determinant is -epsilon(1-s)(9+s)/32, so the gradients are not collinear. Their dot product is positive, while <g1,g2><0: each gradient perturbation from the baseline is at most600epsilon, and the change from the baseline dot product-3/8 is at most(5/4)600epsilon+(600epsilon)^2<1/100. Hence the normalized correlations are nonconstant.\n\nEvery original ordered interpolation residual Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 is nonpositive, including optimum and terminal in both directions. Indeed J(z)=F(z)-<g_i,z> has global minimum at x_i, and J(x_j-gradJ(x_j))<=J(x_j)-||gradJ(x_j)||^2/2 gives exactly Q_ij<=0. All samples are from the same F; there is no online Hessian selection.\n\nAt the baseline terminal(1,0), F_epsilon=3/8-s/8 and gradF_epsilon=(1/2,epsilon/2), of norm<1. Convexity/smoothness and the position error give |F_epsilon(x5)-3/8|<1/100. Thus the true normalized payoff V=(32/25)F_epsilon(x5) satisfies9/20<V<1/2. In particular V>1/23 although P(t)^2<=(1+22t)^-1, refuting the assertion that the C93 spectral screen alone bounds arbitrary changing-region trajectories by global Huber.\n\nOn this SAME word, accepted b98bb807d514e80d gives K5=max(1/23,9/52,3/49)=9/52<9/20. But accepted1fca3e5dd555cb4a supplies the stronger scalar multi-cut witness73/48: checkpoints4,4, ordinary masses1,1,1, m0=1/2,m1=3/4,O1=1/2,z_term=7/4,e_term=1/4,q=7/12. The feasibility inequality is7/4>=2*(1/2), and the payoff is2*(1/4)+(7/4)^2/3=73/48>1/2. Therefore this coupled example is DOMINATED by a known scalar witness. It validates a mechanism and the necessity of the queried-region premise; it supplies no new lower-bound strength, no original-H_m gain, and no arbitrary-h theorem.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "The fixed-queried-region hypothesis can be removed from the C93 resolvent-to-Huber theorem.", "status": "refuted", "joint_proof": "ee38a1e336bfa5a2", "plan_id": "max3_C93_static_ridge_curvature_budget", "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d", "1fca3e5dd555cb4a"], "new_numerical_evaluations": 0, "new_fact_submit_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"F_epsilon": "Fixed function(epsilon*x+y)^2/4+(1-epsilon^2)phi(x),phi'(x)=max(-3/4,min(x,1/2)).", "E_i": "Euclidean position difference of perturbed and unperturbed actual GD iterates in this symbolic scope test.", "P_h": "Endpoint polynomial of the fixed physical word h.", "V_ridge": "Actual doubled objective gap divided by true squared initial distance25/16 in this example."}}

{"id": "625bd7bcb7c95e26", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T11:25:30.261115+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C94 scope attacks: deleting the background floor from the new changing-gate obstruction is false, while interpreting its independent endpoint-contraction envelope as a static adversary is also false. Neither attack settles the weak-background class.", "evidence": "Use the original H_m parameters from the joint draft ab23a6e703982fef, m>=128, beta=1-1/m, c=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m), a=beta/c, ell=1+a and M_m=1+sum H_m. {"id": "1cf3f3da242a776d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T12:17:30.439996+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C95 scope correction: an actual entry into all asymmetric Huber cores does NOT by itself guarantee remaining in the cores on original H_m, even with zero background and exact total core Hessian I.", "evidence": "Analytical proof-text example, no forward case run: in dimension one use A=0, alpha=1, derivative phi'(s)=max(-1,min(s,4)), and x=4. This is one static convex 1-smooth function with unique minimizer0 and total core Hessian1. For the original enclosing-m parameters m>=6, a=(m-1)/((m+1)(1+sqrt2))>=5/(7(1+sqrt2))>1/4 (the last inequality follows from sqrt2<10/7<13/7). The first step of every nonempty H_k is 1+a, so from x=4 the actual next point is -4a<-1, outside the negative core. Thus the implication 'all-core entry => all-core suffix' is false. The precise sufficient condition under total core Hessian I is x and -a*x both in the core intersection. For any prefix length t of H_k, the exact core multiplier is (-1)^t beta^t c^{-s_2(t)}. All positive multipliers lie in[0,1] and every negative multiplier in[-a,0], so convexity of the core intersection proves that strengthened condition suffices. The complete derivation is being included in one joint UNVERIFIED C95 draft; no new fact is asserted.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"plan_id": "max3_C95_weak_background_forward", "target_claim": "Unqualified asymmetric-core absorption", "status": "refuted_at_draft_level", "new_numerical_cases": 0, "new_fact_submit": 0, "impact": "Use the two-point reflection condition; do not discard asymmetric radius costs."}, "glossary": {"C95_core": "Intersection of all closed scalar ridge core strips for the one static C95 function.", "s_2": "Number of ones in the finite binary expansion of a nonnegative integer; s_2(0)=0."}}

{"id": "a18eedfbe5d827cf", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T12:44:51.566369+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C96 refutes unconditional eventual common-core capture for singular-background, nonsaturated-core Huber-ridge sums: for every original H_m,m>=6, an explicit static 2D two-ridge function changes queried gates, has noncommuting visited Hessians and unique minimizer0, but NEVER enters the common core and keeps both ridges strictly outer through the terminal point. This is a scope counterexample, not a stronger lower witness.", "evidence": "Take epsilon=1/12 and eta=0 in the explicit family below. The entire construction and direct gate proof are reproduced here; no unverified predecessor is used as a premise. Formal verification remains disabled.\n\n## 1. Static model, physical word, and parameters\n\nFix an integer m>=6. Put lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda*(1+1/m),\nbeta=1-1/m, a=beta/c, and ell=1+a. Define H_0=() and\n\n    H_(k+1)=(H_k,1+a*c^k,H_k), 0<=k<m.\n\nEvery subword uses these SAME enclosing-m parameters. Let\n\n    j=2^(m-1)-1, n=2^m-1, P=sum H_(m-1),\n    r=a*c^(m-1), b=1+r, d=1/(P+1), sigma=r*d/(2P+1),\n    Kcut=r^2*d^2/(2P+1),\n    p=12/13, q=5/13, rho=q^2/p^2=25/144,\n    kappa=1/ell, J=1+rho*ell, w=1/(2p^2)=169/288.\n\nThe symbol Kcut is the accepted central single-cut value on this exact word;\nit is not the entire worst-case functional W_n and need not equal the Huber\nvalue. Choose dimensionless constants\n\n    0<epsilon<=1/4, |eta|<=1/4.\n\nFor positive Rminus,Rplus define phi(0)=0 and\nphi'(s)=clip(s,[-Rminus,Rplus]). Explicitly phi(s)=s^2/2 in the core,\nphi(s)=Rplus*s-Rplus^2/2 on the positive outer interval, and\nphi(s)=-Rminus*s-Rminus^2/2 on the negative outer interval.\n\nUse u_+=(p,q), u_-=(p,-q), positive radii\n\n    Rplus_+=p*d*(1+epsilon), Rplus_-=p*d*(1-epsilon),\n    Rminus_+=p*sigma*(1+eta), Rminus_-=p*sigma*(1-eta),\n\nand the corresponding two functions phi_+,phi_-. Define ONE static function\n\n    F(x,y)=kappa*y^2/2+w*phi_+(p*x+q*y)+w*phi_-(p*x-q*y).\n\nRun actual GD from X_0=(1,0) using L=1 and exactly the word H_m. There is\nno objective switch, stepsize adaptation, or local curvature rescaling.\n\n## 2. Global legality and the actual minimizer\n\nThe useful uniform parameter bounds are\n\n    2/7<a<sqrt(2)-1<1/2, 9/7<ell<3/2,\n    0<kappa<7/9, 0<rho<1/5, 1<J<13/10,\n    P>31, r>9, b>10, b/r<10/9.                     (C96.1)\n\nIndeed a=(m-1)/((m+1)*lambda)>=5/(7lambda)>2/7 because\nlambda<5/2; also a<1/lambda=sqrt(2)-1. There are at least 31 steps in\nH_(m-1), all greater than one. Finally c>2 gives\nr>(2/7)*2^5=64/7>9. The remaining inequalities follow by substitution.\n\nFor any two scalar arguments, the difference of a clip is theta times\nthe difference of those arguments, where 0<=theta<=1. Thus the difference\nof gradients at X,Y equals D(X-Y), where\n\n    D=diag(0,kappa)+w*theta_+*u_+*u_+^T+w*theta_-*u_-*u_-^T,\n    0<=D<=C=diag(1,kappa+rho)<=diag(1,137/144)<=I.\n\nThe Euclidean norm of D is at most one, proving global 1-smoothness.\nEach summand is convex, proving convexity of F. Every summand is\nnonnegative. Since the two nonparallel ridge vectors span R^2 and each\nphi vanishes only at zero, argmin F={0} and min F=0. Consequently the\ntrue initial squared distance is exactly one and the payoff is V=2F(X_n).\n\nThe quadratic background is singular, so it has no positive curvature\nfloor on the full active plane. The total core matrix C is not I. These\nproperties follow from exact algebraic inequalities, not rounded clipping.\n\n## 3. Exact prefix and its central queried gate\n\nWrite X_i=(x_i,y_i). Put\n\n    delta=epsilon*p*d, zeta=eta*p*sigma,\n    y_plus=-epsilon*(q/p)*d*ell,\n    y_minus=eta*(q/p)*sigma*ell,\n    Lcut=epsilon*d*J/2.\n\nAs long as both ridges are in the positive outer intervals, the gradient is\n\n    grad F=(d,kappa*y+delta*q/p^2).\n\nThe FIRST physical step is ell=1/kappa. It sends y_0=0 exactly to y_plus,\nthe stationary value of this affine scalar recursion. Every subsequent\nstep preserves y_plus until a ridge changes gate, irrespective of its size.\nThe proposed prefix is therefore\n\n    x_i=1-d*sum_(t<i) h_t,\n    y_0=0, y_i=y_plus for 1<=i<=j.                 (C96.2)\n\nHere is an induction validating every proposed gate, rather than assuming\nthe affine formula remains applicable. At i=0 both projections are p, while\nRplus_+<=5p/(4(P+1))<p. For 1<=i<j, the remaining prefix mass includes its\nlast step ell, so x_i-d>=ell*d. Hence\n\n    p*x_i+q*y_plus-Rplus_+\n       >=p*d*(ell-epsilon*J)>0.\n\nThe other projection is even larger than p*x_i, and Rplus_-<p*d. This\nproves that both ridges remain strictly positive outer before index j.\nThus (C96.2) follows from the ACTUAL recursion, and x_j=d.\n\nAt index j the first ridge projection is\n\n    p*d+q*y_plus=p*d*(1-epsilon*(J-1)).\n\nIt is positive because epsilon*(J-1)<3/40, and strictly less than\nRplus_+. It therefore lies strictly in that ridge's core. The second\nprojection is p*d*(1+epsilon*(J-1))>Rplus_-, so the second ridge is still\npositive outer. Direct differentiation now gives\n\n    grad F(X_j)=(d-Lcut,-(q/p)*Lcut),\n    x_(j+1)=-r*d+b*Lcut,\n    y_(j+1)=epsilon*(q/p)*d*(b*J/2-ell).          (C96.3)\n\nThe last y-coordinate is positive by b>10 and ell<3/2. In particular the\ncentral gradient is noncollinear with the stationary precentral gradient\n(d,0). The actual gate Hessians diag(0,kappa) and\ndiag(0,kappa)+w*u_+*u_+^T do not commute: their off-diagonal commutator\nhas nonzero magnitude kappa*w*p*q.\n\n## 4. Both ridges stay negative outer throughout the full suffix\n\nFirst verify the point immediately after the central step. By (C96.1),\n\n    x_(j+1)+(q/p)*abs(y_(j+1))\n      <=d*(-r+b*epsilon*J*(1+rho)/2)\n      <d*(-r+39*b/200)\n      <-47*r*d/60.\n\nSince 2P+1>63 and |eta|<=1/4, this is strictly below\n\n    -sigma*(1+|eta|) >= -5*r*d/(4*(2P+1)).\n\nMore explicitly, 47/60>5/(4*(2P+1)), so the last strict comparison has\nthe required orientation. Both ridge projections are therefore below\ntheir own negative thresholds at X_(j+1).\n\nOn the negative outer intersection the full gradient is\n\n    grad F=(-sigma,kappa*y-q*zeta/p^2).\n\nThe FIRST step of the right H_(m-1) block is again ell. It sets y exactly\nto y_minus. Subsequent steps preserve that value as long as the gates\nstay negative outer, while x increases monotonically. The proposed terminal\npoint is\n\n    x_n=-sigma*(P+1)+b*Lcut, y_n=y_minus.         (C96.4)\n\nTo validate all the proposed post-first-step suffix gates it suffices to\ncheck the largest proposed x, namely x_n. The inequality\n\n    x_n+(q/p)*abs(y_minus)<-sigma*(1+|eta|)\n\nis equivalent to\n\n    P-|eta|*J > (b/r)*(2P+1)*epsilon*J/2.        (C96.5)\n\nThe left side exceeds P-13/40. The right side is less than\n13*(2P+1)/72. Their difference exceeds\n(23/36)*P-13/40-13/72>0, because P>31. This proves (C96.5).\nInduction now verifies the complete actual suffix, including its unqueried\nterminal point. It does not replace physical steps by a Hessian-dependent\nschedule. Every one of the slanted ridges remains strictly negative outer\nat every postcentral query and at the terminal point.\n\nThis trajectory NEVER visits the common core intersection. In particular,\nit refutes an unconditional extension of eventual common-core capture to\nall singular-background, nonsaturated-core models. Its poor objective\ncannot be attributed to entering the common quadratic region.\n\n## 5. Exact objective and a whole two-parameter family obstruction\n\nWhen both ridges are negative outer, expanding the defining static function\ngives\n\n    F(x,y)=-sigma*x-(q*zeta/p^2)*y\n           -(sigma^2+zeta^2/p^2)/2+kappa*y^2/2.\n\nNo function-value offsets are deleted. At y=y_minus=q*zeta/(p^2*kappa),\nthe transverse linear and quadratic terms combine to\n-q^2*zeta^2/(2p^4*kappa). Substituting (C96.4) yields\n\n    V=2F(X_n)\n     =Kcut-2*sigma*b*Lcut-zeta^2*J/p^2\n     =Kcut*(1-epsilon*b*J/r-eta^2*J/(2P+1)).    (C96.6)\n\nThis is the main joint statement: for EVERY m>=6 and EVERY allowed\nepsilon,eta the single static two-ridge function above is globally legal,\nhas the stated actual minimizer and queried itinerary, and obeys (C96.6).\nThe proof is entirely symbolic and independent of finite trajectory runs.\n\nThe payoff is strictly smaller than Kcut because epsilon>0. V is positive,\nsince the terminal point is strictly in the negative outer region and is\nnot the unique minimizer. As an algebraic check, epsilon*b*J/r<13/36\nand eta^2*J/(2P+1)<13/10080, so the displayed bracket is positive as well.\n\nAccepted b98bb807d514e80d gives Kcut<=K_n(H_m), and accepted\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a gives K_n(H_m)<=F_ray_n(H_m)<=W_n(H_m). Consequently\nthis entire explicit changing-region family fails to improve even the\naccepted central single cut, for all horizons in its stated domain. A\nnegative tilt eta cannot repair the loss: it incurs the exact additional\nquadratic price in (C96.6). This is not an upper bound on unrestricted W_n.\n\nThe same conclusion prices a free initial transverse displacement as long\nas the initial point (1,y0) lies in both positive outer regions. Its first\nstep sends y0 to the same y_plus, so all later states and terminal values\nare unchanged. Its TRUE initial squared distance is 1+y0^2; its normalized\npayoff is the expression in (C96.6) divided by 1+y0^2. A large transverse\ninitial coordinate cannot be silently removed from the denominator.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"joint_finding": "2d8525da4bc3b684", "target_claim": "Every actual singular-background, nonsaturated-core static ridge trajectory on H_m eventually enters the common core.", "status": "refuted in the displayed joint unverified proof", "assumptions_satisfied": ["two-dimensional static centered sum with two ridges", "global convexity and 1-smoothness", "singular quadratic background", "nonsaturated positive definite core matrix", "actual original physical H_m with enclosing-m parameters", "unique minimizer and true radius1"], "failed_conclusion": "No iterate enters the intersection of both core strips; all postcentral queries and terminal are strictly outer.", "impact": "A universal weak-background closure cannot silently extend the conditional C95 common-core capture mechanism.", "new_fact_submit": 0}, "glossary": {"C96_F": "The explicit static planar function defined in the reproduced proof.", "Kcut": "Accepted central single-cut value on the same H_m."}}

The original full array and calibrated root are reproduced in joint 1f8676462d9ee6b5; no unverified part of that draft is needed for the following direct computation and actual witness.\n\n## 8. Exact diagnostic: the D hypothesis cannot simply be deleted\n\nThis diagnostic concerns the SMALL input list used in conclusionI,\nnot the complete older system. It audits a previously proposed\nbelow-threshold example and explicitly supplies its older C82 rejection.\n\nLet alpha be the unique positive root\n\n                         alpha^2*(11+2*alpha)=1.\n\nThe left side increases on positive arguments. Its value at1/3 is\n35/27>1; at47/160 it is2047743/2048000<1. Hence\n\n                    47/160<alpha<1/3<sqrt(2)-1.\n\nTake a=alpha,b=c=v=1. Then C=7+alpha<22/3<193/25 and\n\n R=(3/2)*(1-alpha^2*(11+2*alpha))=0,\n Q=1, U=(2+alpha)^2,\n D=12+4*alpha>0, eps=-D<0.\n\nThe two old cut energies are positive:\n\n 16*F2=15+50*alpha+15*alpha^2>0,\n 4*F3=15+18*alpha+3*alpha^2>0.\n\nThe old terminal cut also holds because v=1 and C>1. Therefore\nremoving D<=0 from the implication using just these listed inputs\nwould be false. This is not a threshold word or a complete-old-system\nsurvivor.\n\nHere is its explicit OLD triangle rejection. Let r82>1/alpha be\nthe positive solution of\n\n                     (r82+1)*(alpha*r82-1)=1/4.\n\nIts existence and uniqueness follow from strict increase of this\nquadratic for r>=1/alpha. Put z82=1/2 and\n\n K=conv{(r82,0),(-1,z82),(-1/2,0)},\n F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nCompleting the square gives the unique maximizer p_x=P_K(x).\nAdding the two projection normal inequalities gives\n\n ||p_x-p_y||^2<=<p_x-p_y,x-y>, hence ||p_x-p_y||<=||x-y||.\n\nThe maximizing formula also gives\n\n 0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<p_x,d>\n       <=<p_(x+d)-p_x,d><=||d||^2.\n\nThus F_K is differentiable with gradient p_x and is1-smooth.\nIt is convex as a maximum of affine functions, and0 is a minimizer:\nthe feasible vector g=0 gives F_K>=0, while F_K(0)=0.\nThe actual GD data are\n\n x0=((1+alpha)*r82-1,z82), g0=(r82,0),\n x1=(-1,z82),             g1=(-1,z82),\n x2=(1,-z82),             g2=(1,0),\n x3=(-1,-z82),            g3=(-1/2,0),\n x4=(-1/2,-z82),          g4=(-1/2,0).\n\nAll four updates have the stated word. At index0, pairing its normal\n(alpha*r82-1,z82) with the direction to(-1,z82) gives zero by the\nroot equation, and the direction to(-1/2,0) gives a negative product.\nAt index1 the normal is zero. At indices2 and4 the normal is vertical\ndownward: its products with horizontal base directions are zero and\nwith the upper vertex are negative. At index3 the normal is\n(-1/2,-z82); its product with the direction to the upper vertex is\n1/4-z82^2=0 and with the other base direction is negative. These\ncheck every vertex, hence every original ordered interpolation row.\n\nThe saturated values give primitive energy\n\n                ((1+alpha)*r82-1)^2-C^2/4.           (25)\n\nPut rbd=(9+alpha)/(2*(1+alpha)), so that\n(1+alpha)*rbd-1=C/2. Direct expansion and the defining equation\nfor alpha give\n\n (rbd+1)*(alpha*rbd-1)-1/4\n       =(29*alpha^2+138*alpha-43)/(8*(1+alpha)^2)>0.\n\nFor the strict sign, the numerator increases for positive alpha and\nat47/160 equals1021/25600>0. Also alpha*rbd-1>0:\nits numerator alpha^2+7*alpha-2 is already3649/25600>0 at47/160.\nThus rbd>1/alpha and strict monotonicity gives r82<rbd. Since\n(1+alpha)*r82-1>0, (25) is strictly negative. This is one member\nof the older C82 triangle family and rejects the diagnostic word.\nIt cannot be reused as an independence witness against the complete\nolder system.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["1f8676462d9ee6b5", "d4fb8f8001bbc66f"], "fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750", "1252b8823bb5a290"], "target_claim": "Removing D<=0 from the implication using the SMALL list R,Q,U,F2,F3 and terminal cut", "status": "refuted for that smaller-input implication; no refutation of the complete-old-system implication", "work_round": 36}, "glossary": {"alpha": "Unique positive root alpha^2*(11+2*alpha)=1, bounded47/160<alpha<1/3.", "r82": "Positive root (r82+1)*(alpha*r82-1)=1/4.", "rbd": "(9+alpha)/(2*(1+alpha)), at which the initial horizontal coordinate is C/2.", "F_K": "Smooth convex function max over g in the stated triangle K of <x,g>-||g||^2/2."}}
{"id": "d3a15bef4ed8dc6b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T15:10:55.399167+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C100 exact local-only failure and repaired legal strong-projection family (UNVERIFIED): real dense data on h=(4,7/5), with t0=2, positive Markov law and positive affine reserve AND optimum slack, satisfy all adjacent/optimum rows for0<r<=5/6 but full interpolation iff0<r<=1/6. Real dense data: strong projection, all rows, exact radius\n\nThe excluded class is not an implicit t<=1 class. Here is a complete\nthree-dimensional family, useful also for testing the remote row.\nTake h=(4,7/5), a=2/5, kval=6/5, eta=5/4, and a real parameter r>0.\nIn the standard orthonormal coordinates e_0,e_1,e_2 put\n\n    g_0=e_0, g_1=-2e_0+e_1, g_2=sqrt(r)e_2,\n    X_0=(5/4)e_0+(3/4)e_1+(5/4)sqrt(r)e_2,\n    X_1=X_0-4g_0, X_2=X_1-(7/5)g_1,\n    q_0=1, q_1=5, q_2=r, f_i=(7/10)q_i.\n\nThe support eta is5/4 at all three points; delta_i=q_i/5 and\nz_i=q_i/20 are strictly positive. The queried projection t_0=2 exceeds1.\nThe full normalized directions have rho_0=-2/sqrt(5), rho_1=0;\ntheir distance-two product is exact. The sign law is the positive law\n\n    P(s_0,s_1,s_2)=(1/8)[1-(2/sqrt(5))s_0s_1].\n\nThe initial coefficients are all nonzero. Every original non-optimum\ndirected residual is as follows:\n\n    Q_01=-9/5,             Q_10=-1/5,\n    Q_12=-1-r/5,           Q_21=(6r-5)/5,\n    Q_02=-r/5,             Q_20=(6r-1)/5.           (35)\n\nThe two optimum orientations are Q_(star,i)=-q_i/5 and\nQ_(i,star)=-q_i/20. Hence the full data are legal exactly when0<r<=1/6;\nthey pass every adjacent and optimum row for0<r<=5/6. At r=1/2 only\nthe remote reverse Q_20=2/5 is violated. This is an explicit counterexample\nto replacing full legality by these local rows, even with a positive\njoint law, dense initial coordinates and positive reserves and slacks.\n\nFor0<r<=1/6 define the actual function from the accepted construction:\n\n    H(v)=||v||^2/2+\n          max{0, max_(i=0,1,2)(<X_i-g_i,v>-delta_i)},\n    F(x)=sup_v(<x,v>-H(v)).                         (36)\n\nAll rows in(35) and all optimum rows ensure interpolation by accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290. Thus (36) is ONE convex1-smooth function realizing both\nGD queries and the displayed actual terminal. As every delta_i>0, the\nzero affine branch uniquely dominates in a neighborhood of v=0; hence\nH equals||v||^2/2 there and its subgradient at0 is the singleton{0}.\nThe conjugate maximizer condition then implies argmin F={0}. Its true\ninitial radius and payoff, including every coordinate, are exactly\n\n    R_init^2=(34+25r)/16,\n    V=112r/(170+125r).                             (37)\n\nAt r=1/10 this is112/1825. Every residual in(35) is strictly negative.\nFor this same word the accepted scalar functional is K_2=45/19:\nits global term is5/59, the first cut is45/19 and the second cut is4/625.\nIn particular112/1825<5/59, so K, ray and the augmented lens all dominate\nthis displayed witness. It is a legal strong-projection example, not a\nbenchmark improvement. C59 concerns a different word and is inapplicable.\n\nFor completeness the constant-ratio family on every actual H_m is also\nnonempty with all initial innovations nonzero. Put k=1+a/2,\ne=k-1=a/2, eta=1+3a/4, r0=e/(2k), and in R^(n+1) set\n\n    q_i=r0^i, g_i=sqrt(q_i)e_i,\n    X_0=eta sum_(i=0)^n sqrt(q_i)e_i,\n    X_i=X_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t,\n    f_i=(k-1/2)q_i.\n\nHere all rho are zero, eta_i=eta, delta_i=e q_i, z_i=(eta-k)q_i>0.\nFor i<j, orthogonality gives\n\n    Q_ij=(e-d_i)q_i-e q_j<0,\n    Q_ji=k q_j-e q_i<=-(e/2)q_i<0.\n\nEvery optimum row also holds strictly. Formula(36), with all indices,\ngives a static function with unique minimizer0, exact radius\neta^2 sum_(i=0)^n r0^i, and payoff\n(2k-1)r0^n/[eta^2 sum_(i=0)^n r0^i]. These small coefficients demonstrate\nnonemptiness only. Their density is not claimed to supply useful gain;\nindeed the proved obstruction and the accepted orthogonal result exclude it.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["39115c0842ba32a7"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290", "b98bb807d514e80d", "1fca3e5dd555cb4a", "dfde2fa326c908bf"]}, "glossary": {"r": "Positive parameter giving terminal gradient squared norm in the explicit three-dimensional h=(4,7/5) family.", "kval": "Constant value/gradient ratio6/5 in that finite family.", "eta": "Included-origin support ratio5/4 in that finite family."}}
{"id": "cffb74c24281726d", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T15:18:25.541987+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max37 exact retained-jet counterexample to freely repeating the new triangular core-prepend rule: its legal (2,2,1) witness cannot accept another preceding step2 whose initial point equals its gradient. The required old directed interpolation row has residual4>0. This excludes only that prepend restriction, not all possible preceding jets or functions.", "evidence": "Work in R^2 at smoothness1. Put e=(1,0), p37=(1,2sqrt3), q37=(-2,-sqrt3), C=conv{p37,q37,e}, and F(x)=max_{v in C}(<x,v>-||v||^2/2). Completing the square shows its maximizer is the projection onto C. {"id": "de3e64fcb8aa9b8b", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T18:27:33.592449+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max2 NEW exact scoped failure, UNVERIFIED: complete original H4 and H5 child kernels have negative inherited high-mode directions uniformly m>=128; H5 keeps endpoint gradients zero and positions equal. These directions violate original Q and do NOT refute the complete H_m residual kernel.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED self-contained formal counterexample; no claim of verifier acceptance.\n\nFix m>=128, epsilon=1/m, lambda=1+sqrt(2), c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=(1-epsilon)/c, d_j=1+a*c^j, H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,d_j,H_j). The original queried word is H_m with its LAST d0 removed. Every H_K used below is its actual initial prefix with the SAME enclosing epsilon. Write S_K=sum H_K. For any nonempty physical word with prefix masses s_i and common vectors g_i, define\nJ=sum_i[2s_(i+1)h_i<g_(i+1),g_i>+s_(i+1)||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2].\nThis is the exact chronological quadratic being tested, with all physical steps of the child included.\n\nParameter bounds: 12/5<c<39/16 and a>127/312>2/5. The word recursion gives S_K+1=A*c^K+(1-A)*2^K, where A=a/(c-2) lies strictly between0 and1 because c(c-2)>1. Thus S_K/d_K<1/a<5/2. Also d_(k+1)>2d_k for k>=2: its difference is (c-2)a*c^k-1, and at k2 the quantity before minus1 is (1-epsilon)c(c-2)>(1-epsilon)(1+6epsilon)>1. Finally\nd0=sqrt2-2epsilon/[lambda(1+epsilon)]<sqrt2,\nd1=2-epsilon<2,\nd2=2+sqrt2-lambda epsilon^2<2+sqrt2,\nd3=1+lambda^2(1+epsilon-epsilon^2-epsilon^3)>4+2sqrt2.\nHence S3=4d0+2d1+d2<6+5sqrt2.\n\nExpanding J in the temporal gradients gives a tridiagonal matrix: at an interior query i its diagonal is 2s_i+2h_i-h_i^2 and its off-diagonal with i+1 is s_(i+1)(h_i-1). Thus a pulse of amplitude eta_k at i_k=2^k-1, zero at every other query, has COMPLETE child energy kappa_k*eta_k^2 where kappa_k=2S_k+2d_k-d_k^2. The preceding step contributes S_k*eta_k^2; the killing step contributes [S_k+2d_k-d_k^2]*eta_k^2. All other terms vanish, so this is not an isolated-step test.\nAt k3, decreasing 2d-d^2 for d>1 gives\nkappa3<2(6+5sqrt2)+2(4+2sqrt2)-(4+2sqrt2)^2=-(4+2sqrt2).\nFor k>=4, d_k>=d4>2d3>21/2, and kappa_k<d_k(7-d_k)<-d_k^2/3.\nDifferent i_k are separated by at least8, so their cross terms vanish exactly.\n\nThese complete temporal pulses belong to the ACTUAL high-mode gradient quotient on the child. For K>=4 and K+2^K<=m set N=2^K-1, R=2^K, P_i(z)=product_(t<i)(1-h_t z) on H_K, z_r=1/d_(K+r), and V_(i,r)=P_i(z_r), 0<=i,r<=N. All chosen modes j=K,...,K+R-1 survive the child. The leading coefficient of P_i is (-1)^i product_(t<i)h_t. Thus\nDdet=det V=[product_i product_(t<i)h_t]*product_(r<s)(z_r-z_s)>0.\nThe sign from leading coefficients cancels the reversed-node Vandermonde sign. For ANY prescribed vector A of ALL N+1 query gradients, set\nw_(K+r)=Ddet^(-1) sum_i (-1)^(i+r)det(V with row i and column r deleted)*A_i,\nv=0 and all other w_j=0.\nCofactor expansion proves sum_j P_i(1/d_j)w_j=A_i at every query. The determinants are exact finite signed permutation sums, not an executed solve. All vectors can be real scalars, with a rank-one PSD common Gram.\n\nFor every m>=128, H4 therefore admits the complete transcript g7=1, all other16-total query gradients zero, using modes4..19. Its endpoint gradients are zero and its full energy is less than -(4+2sqrt2).\nFor every m>=128, H5 admits the complete transcript g7=d4,g15=-d3, every other query0 through31 zero, using modes5..36. It has\ng0=g31=0, sum_(i<31)h_i g_i=d3*d4-d4*d3=0, x31=x0,\nJ_H5=kappa3*d4^2+kappa4*d3^2<-(4+2sqrt2)d4^2-d4^2*d3^2/3<0.\nThus endpoint gradients and positions cannot alone make every complete child nonnegative.\n\nMore generally the pulses at i_k for3<=k<K span a K-3-dimensional negative subspace. Imposing sum_k d_k eta_k=0 leaves a K-4-dimensional negative subspace with zero endpoint gradients and zero displacement. Any added boundary correction (Lw)^T C(Lw), with rank L<=r<K-4 and arbitrary symmetric C, vanishes on a nonzero member of this subspace by rank-nullity; it cannot repair that member. L may depend on the word. For any fixed r choose K>=r+5 and m>=max(128,K+2^K), so a uniformly bounded number of such linear features cannot close all actual children. This does not rule out growing covariance, nonlinear mechanisms, cross-block compensation, or added original-Q information.\n\nALL-Q AUDIT: at the earliest nonzero pulse i, all prior gradients are zero, so GD has x_i=x_(i-1)=x0 but g_i!=g_(i-1)=0. For every value array, the original normalized residuals Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2 satisfy\nQ_(i,i-1)+Q_(i-1,i)=||g_i-g_(i-1)||^2>0.\nThis contradicts necessary smooth-convex interpolation (accepted1252b8823bb5a290). In H5 the pair(6,7) has sum d4^2. The construction is explicitly ILLEGAL, even if x0 is chosen to have norm1.\n\nFULL-WORD SCOPE: after H_K these high modes remain alive. Their full original suffix contribution is not zero or bounded above here. Negative J_HK does NOT imply negative J on H_m minus its last d0. These are exact formal counterexamples to the stated LOCAL PSD/linear-boundary rules only, not actual hard functions or minimax disproofs. The complete full-covariance identity, state dimension m+1, final constant-column correction and explicit local payment bound are jointly derived in19f0eaeb0e7c3518; no such payment is assumed in the counterexample above.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "Every complete original ruler child has PSD signed kernel on inherited surviving modes, or can be made so by endpoints plus a uniformly bounded number of linear boundary features.", "status": "refuted_at_joint_draft_level", "candidate_counterexample": "H5 all32 queries: g7=d4,g15=-d3,others0; high modes5..36 specified by exact cofactors; original common Gram rank1.", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Original chronological child and same enclosing epsilon", "Every physical step and query of complete child included", "Original high reciprocal temporal modes, real common PSD Gram", "Zero endpoint gradients and displacement for H5", "No computation or changed physical horizon claimed for whole target"], "failed_conclusion": "Nonnegative complete child signed energy and bounded-feature local repair", "impact": "Require a growing full covariance or explicit cross-block payment; this is not a theorem excluding all probabilistic methods.", "finding_ids": ["19f0eaeb0e7c3518", "38cdb279a46a597e"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "resources": {"numerical": 0, "formal": 0}, "round": 34}, "glossary": {"J": "Entire signed chronological energy defined in the evidence, including every child step.", "S_K": "Physical sum of H_K with the fixed enclosing epsilon.", "V": "Full 2^K-by-2^K reciprocal-mode evaluation matrix on all child queries.", "Ddet": "Its strictly positive determinant, explicitly factored in the evidence.", "eta_k": "Pulse amplitude at query2^k-1.", "kappa_k": "Complete pulse coefficient 2S_k+2d_k-d_k^2.", "L": "Any linear boundary-feature map in the scoped rank obstruction.", "C": "Arbitrary real symmetric quadratic coefficient on those features."}}

{"id": "6511976491c8d824", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T19:26:53.274698+00:00", "author": "max3", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "C106 UNVERIFIED actual original-H_m counterexample to an assumed zero minimum or lossless H_c>=0 reduction in the signed-terminal-cost extension. The legal static example has mu>0,H_c<0 and a necessary extra radius term M^2v^2; its exact original-word payoff is <707/1280 times COMPLETE K=F_ray, so it is NOT an improving adversary.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED counterexample construction within the complete joint proof3387cf58f27ea36b; no fact or improving adversary is asserted.\n\nTarget claim: every legal signed-terminal-cost extension of the assigned original obtuse family has minimum zero, or can be reduced without loss to canonical H>=0 / the radius without an upper-projection term.\nStatus: refuted by exact actual-original-word data below.\nAssumptions retained: every original physical query; static Moreau envelope; alpha_o,beta_o,kappa_o>=0; p=(1,0),q=(-u,-v),r=(-w,ell); closest-minimizer normalization; all directed and optimum rows.\nChanged hypothesis: final affine cost tau is arbitrary real, rather than0.\n\nNotation for the construction: h=(block_0,1+A,block_1,1+E,block_2) in original order; T=1+sum block_0,M=1+sum block_1,N=1+sum block_2,sigma=2N-1. Canonical J(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa,<r,y>} and F(x)=min_y[J(y)+||x-y||^2/2], X_0=(T+delta,-H), delta=A-Mu-alpha,H=Mv+beta. Set a_q=u^2+v^2,a_r=w^2+ell^2,c=uw-v ell,eta=w alpha-ell beta,R_q=u alpha+v beta-kappa,G=R_q-eta,z=(Eu-alpha,Ev-beta). Slopes p,q,r are uniquely exposed. For costs k_i assigned0,kappa,0 the exact row identity is Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j>-k_i+k_j<=0. Include the minimum with slope and cost0 to obtain both optimum orientations. Coercivity/strict convexity of the proximal objective and monotonicity of the affine-max subdifferential prove F is finite convex differentiable1-smooth. At kappa=0 the weights proportional to u ell+v w,ell,v are all positive and have zero weighted slope, forcing unique minimum0.\n\nThe original function is J_o(y)=max{0,<p,y>,<q,y>-kappa_o,<r,y>-tau} with its Moreau envelope F_o. Translation and value shift in the construction are exact globally.\n\nFor any appended nonnegative mass zeta the exact holding interval is the minimum of L_0/a_r,L_p/(a_r+w),L_q/(a_r-c) when a_r>c (otherwise the last term is infinity), with L_0=<r,z>-N a_r,L_p=<r-p,z>-N(a_r+w),L_q=<r-q,z>+kappa-N(a_r-c). The normalized payoff decreases by2 zeta a_r/R^2.\n\n## 5. Actual original-word counterexample to deleting the upper branch\n\nTake the first and last level0 checkpoints of ORIGINAL H_m, m>=128.\nKeep the SAME enclosing parameters: T=N=1, A=E=a_m<1/2 and\nM=M_m-2-2A>=1, where M_m=1+sum H_m. More generally the following\nformulas work under A/(M+E+N)<1/2. Put\n\n    Q=M+E+N, t=E/(2N), u=A/(4Q), w=u/2,\n    alpha=(A-Mu)/[1+t u/(4(1+t))],\n    delta=t u alpha/[4(1+t)],\n    v=sqrt(u alpha/[4M(1+t)]), ell=t v,\n    beta=-2Mv, kappa=0.                              (D18)\n\nAll parameters used as magnitudes are positive. Because u<1/8,\nMu<=A/4 and the denominator defining alpha is less than3/2,\nalpha>A/2. Thus u(E+N/2)<=A/4<alpha. The budget identity\ndelta+Mu+alpha=A holds. Here H=-Mv<0 and d_up=Mv>0.\n\nEvery support test is now checked explicitly. The prefix q inequality\nholds since vH=-Mv^2<0. The p/r prefix margin is\n(1+w)delta+ell H=w delta>0, because delta=t Mv^2.\nAt the middle end,\n\n    R_q=eta=u alpha(1+2t)/[2(1+t)]>0, G=0,\n    a_q-c=u^2/2+(1+t)v^2>0.\n\nAt ANY tail position 1<=k<=N its r/q margin is\n\n    E(a_q-c)-k(a_r-c)\n      =u^2(E/2+k/4)+(1+t)(E-kt)v^2>0,\n\nbecause E-kt>=E/2. Its ending r/zero margin is\n\n    L_0=eta-(u^2/2)(E+N/2)+t(E-Nt)v^2\n       >=(u/2)[alpha-u(E+N/2)]>0.\n\nAt every tail proximal point its first coordinate is\nEu-alpha+k w<=u(E+N/2)-alpha<0. Thus the r/zero test also gives\nr/p activity. Prefix p/zero and middle q/zero and q/p follow from\ndelta>0, R_q>0 and alpha>0. This proves ALL (D2) tests, both original\nphysical row orientations and all optimum rows via (D7). The closest\nminimum is uniquely0, and\n\n    R^2=(T+delta)^2+M^2v^2,\n    P=2eta-2E(u^2/2-t v^2)-sigma(u^2/4+t^2v^2).       (D19)\n\nThe holding law is EXACTLY (D9), with all three displayed margins.\nThis is one static function throughout, not an iteratively reassigned\noracle or an unpriced gradient sequence.\n\nIt can be written inside the ORIGINAL nonnegative-residual signed-cost\nfamily (D1), with POSITIVE actual minimum. Set\n\n    mu_ex=alpha/2, y_ex=-(1+u)mu_ex/v,\n    alpha_o=alpha-mu_ex=alpha/2,\n    beta_o=beta-y_ex\n          =alpha[1+u-u/(1+t)]/(2v)>0,\n    kappa_o=0, delta_o=delta+mu_ex,\n    tau=-mu_ex[(1+w)+t(1+u)]<0. The estimates are derived here; no prior draft is a premise.\nEquation labels in this appendix carry the prefix B.\n\n\nFix the ORIGINAL enclosing m>=128, abbreviate a=a_m, c_r=c_m and\nrho=c_r-2, and put a_k=a c_r^k for the level-k checkpoint excess.\nThe exact mass recurrence and its solution are\n\n    M_0=1, M_(k+1)=2M_k+a c_r^k,\n    M_k=[a/(c_r-2)]c_r^k+[1-a/(c_r-2)]2^k. The remaining bounds follow\nfrom the positive two-term formula (B21). In particular the term\n(a/rho)c_r^k alone proves a_k<=rho M_k.\n\nA level-k checkpoint has an immediately adjacent H_k before and after\nit. Its complete prefix and suffix masses plus1 are therefore at least\nM_k. For every first checkpoint in any pair, A/T<=rho<=9/20<1/2.\nThus T>=2A, as required by the new full-norm comparison.\n\nFor actual checkpoint indices i<j of levels k,l, respectively, put\n\n    R_pair=AE/(TM), q_pair=c_r rho^2.\n\nIf i precedes the entire H_l immediately before j, then T>=M_k and\nM>=M_l, so R_pair<=rho^2. This includes k>=l because such a level\ncannot occur inside H_l. Otherwise i lies inside that particular H_l,\nso k<l. Let x be1 plus i's local prefix mass inside H_l, and y=M.\nIts local adjacent copies imply x,y>=M_k, and the exact identity is\nx+y=M_l-a_k. As T>=x,\n\n    TM>=xy>=M_k(M_l-a_k-M_k).\n\nWrite theta=a_k/M_k<=rho and zeta=M_l/M_k>=2+theta. The latter bound\nfollows from l>=k+1 and M_(k+1)=2M_k+a_k. Since E<=rho M_l,\n\n    R_pair<=theta rho zeta/(zeta-1-theta)\n           <=theta rho(2+theta)<=rho^2(2+rho)=q_pair.\n\nThe middle function decreases in zeta for zeta>=2+theta, and the\nlast expression increases in theta>=0. This covers every actual pair.\nIn particular q_pair<=(49/20)(9/20)^2=3969/8000<1/2.\n\nPalindromicity applies this very same pair estimate in reverse and\ngives AE/(NM)<=q_pair. The full physical word\nmass, including both checkpoints and every ordinary query, is exactly\n\n    M_m=T+M+N+A+E.\n\nMultiplying by R_pair and using the preceding bounds yields\n\n    R_pair<=min{q_pair,D_pair N/M_m},\n    D_pair=2(1+rho)q_pair+rho^2. (B24)\n\nThe rational function decreases with M_m, so the final bound follows\nfrom M_m>=16. The original tail factor2N-1 was retained throughout.\n\n### B.2. Complete SAME-word K, ray and applicable lens references\n\nFor accepted b98bb807d514e80d, EVERY single-cut term at a level-k step\nh=1+a_k has denominator\n\n    (1+P)^2[1+2(S_total-h-P)], S_total=M_m-1,\n\nwhere P is its COMPLETE physical prefix sum. Differentiation has the\nsign of2(S_total-h)-3P, so its minimum between first and last occurrence\nis at an endpoint. For the other, use that x^2/(2x-1)\nincreases for x>=1 and M_k<=M_(m-1). Thus every cut is at most\nrho^2/(2M_(m-1)-1). Since M_m<=c_r M_(m-1),\n\n    Cut_i/[1/(2M_m-1)]<=rho^2(2c_r-1)\n                         <=3159/4000<gamma104.          (B25)\n\nHere (2c_r x-1)/(2x-1)<=2c_r-1 for x>=1. If a checkpoint selection\nhas at least two indices, its first two nonterminal magnitudes are\nm_0=1/T and m_1=A/(TM), and its old offset after the first cut is\nO_1=(T-1)m_0. Its budget just after the second cut is\n\n    E m_1-O_1=m_0[R_pair T-(T-1)]<m_0,\n\nbecause R_pair<=q_pair<1. With exactly two cuts, feasibility requires\nthis budget at least N m_0>=m_0. With more cuts, the next nonterminal\nmagnitude is this budget divided by a mass at least1. A nonpositive\nbudget already violates positivity; a positive budget still gives\nm_2<m_0, violating the required repeated-ray magnitude condition.\nThus no selection with two or more checkpoints is feasible. Zero and\nsingle checkpoint choices are exactly the complete K terms. We obtain\n\n    F_ray_n(H_m)=K_n(H_m)=1/(2M_m-1). Section4 applies this ONLY in its stated domain.\n\nThe complete applicable reference from accepted dfde2fa326c908bf is\n\n    V_lens(h)=max{sup_(z in Lens_n)Gap_h(z),1/(1+2sum h)}.\n\nIt contains this SAME Huber term by definition. Thus the upper bound\nE^2 C(T,A,M)/sigma is at most gamma104 V_lens(H_m). This compares the\nfull Huber-augmented lens maximum without pretending to compute its\ncomplex component. It does not equate the universal lens with the larger\nword-dependent legal set D_h, and does not cover all nonlinear scalar\ntrajectories.\n\nC59/accepted e9a55f57461f253c remains confined to its ACTUAL H6 with\nepsilon1/6. Our m>=128 result is not a transfer of that witness to a\nchild word whose enclosing parameters changed. No new actual-H6 record\nis asserted. Accepted62a7d9329121fd75 remains the finite(2,2,1)\ncalibration; it is not used as an iteration or family-optimality premise.\n\n### B.3. The original all-split B_n, without changing the denominator\n\nFrom accepted b98bb807d514e80d, put C_n=1+S_circle(n),\nB_n=1+2S_bullet(n), C_0=B_0=1, and\n\n    C_n=max_(k+l+1=n)Omega(C_k,C_l),\n    B_n=max_(k+l+1=n)G(C_k,B_l),\n    Omega(a,d)=(a+d+sqrt((a+d)^2+4ad))/2,\n    G(a,d)=2a+d/2+sqrt(d^2+8ad)/2.\n\nWe give the sharper elementary upper estimate needed here, rather than\nusing an unverified asymptotic of the benchmark. Set p_0=log_2(lambda)>1\nand f_0(t)=(1+t)^p_0-1 for t>0. The second derivative of log f_0(t)\nwith respect to log t is\n\n    p_0 t(1+t)^(p_0-2)\n      [(1+t)^p_0-1-p_0 t]/f_0(t)^2>0.\n\nThe bracket is positive: it is0 at0 and has positive derivative for\nt>0. Convexity at log t and-log t gives\nf_0(t)f_0(1/t)>=f_0(1)^2=(lambda-1)^2=2. Consequently\n\n    Omega(a,d)<=(a^(1/p_0)+d^(1/p_0))^p_0.\n\nIndeed the proposed right side X>=max(a,d) obeys(X-a)(X-d)>=2ad,\nwhere equality defines the positive root Omega; zero arguments follow\nby continuity. Induction over ALL splits in the ORIGINAL recurrence\ntherefore gives C_n<=(n+1)^p_0. The inequality G(a,d)<=4a+d follows\nby squaring sqrt(d^2+8ad)<=d+4a. A second all-split induction gives\n\n    B_n<=1+4n^p_0.                                     (B27)\n\nFor an actual split k+l+1=n it uses (k+1)^p_0+l^p_0<=n^p_0;\nthis follows by integrating the increasing derivative of x^p_0 and\nincludes l=0. The first flow telescopes to h0=p1(1-t0+z), where z=sum_(j=2)^5 h_(j-1)D_j/D1>0. It instead forces z=(4/3)/4-1+1/3=-1/3, a contradiction.
