{"id": "83f46bf388f23c91", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T19:35:21.608906+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "The seven signed suffix/adjacent interval modes are NOT exhaustive even among ordinary full four-step certificates with M=0 and equal last two steps. A valid ConPP word forces Lambda12<0 in its unique seven-mode representation; a displayed twelve-edge skew correction recovers its actual nonnegative full array.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED; complete joint proof and expanded definitions are also in c142e479a2a094ae. Here is the standalone algebra. Let A=1+sqrt2, b>0 solve b(A+4+b)=4A, C=A+4+b, kappa=C^2/16, K=kappa-1>0, and h=(sqrt2,1+b,3/2,3/2). The full original array has only these nonzero entries: Lambda01=2,Lambda10=2A,Lambda14=Kb,Lambda21=Lambda31=3K/2,Lambda23=kappa,Lambda24=2kappa-3/2,Lambda32=7kappa/2,Lambda34=10kappa-3/2,Lambda41=K,Lambda42=kappa,Lambda43=14kappa. All are positive, others and diagonal zero. The left one-step certificate has multipliers2,2A and full remainder0. The right two-step(3/2,3/2) certificate has lambda01=1,lambda02=1/2,lambda10=7/2,lambda12=17/2,lambda20=1,lambda21=14; direct multiplication gives flow(-3,-3,6) and sym(LT)=-diag(3/4,3/4,-15), hence full remainder0. Accepted04219554e41d9750 Section11 applied with left capacity A,right capacity4,bridge excess b gives exactly the displayed five-node array and full M=0.\n\nFor the claimed restricted representation, define J^[a,j] by forward entries(i-a+1)h_i-1, backward entries1+(j-i)h_i on interval[a,j], diagonals minus row sums. Let N=sum_(a=0)^3 tau_a J^[a,4]+sum_(i=0)^2 q_i J^[i,i+1], with all seven weights real, original Lambda_ij=N_ij off diagonal. Put S_i=sum_(a<=i)tau_a, B_i=sum_(j<=i)S_j and r_i=h_i-1. Multiplication gives sym(JT) diagonal, with entry r_i^2(i-a+1)(j-i)-(i-a)(j-i+1). Matching D makes (4-i)B_i+q_i=K_i:=product_(j<=i)r_j^-2-1. Cumulative column-flow matching through cuts0 and1 gives B0=[2h0-(2-h0)K0]/(3h1+2h2+h3) and B1=[2(h0+h1)+3h0B0-(2-h1)K1]/(2h2+h3). These equations hold for every supplied seven-mode representation, without positivity of its weights. At the stated word, B0=0,K1=K, and B1=2K/3, using b^2+(A+4)b=4A. The actual entry Lambda12 is r1 K1+B0-2r1B1=-bK/3<0. Therefore no such seven-mode representation has all final multipliers nonnegative, although the full ordinary array above does.\n\nThe scope distinction is concrete: the seven-mode array forces Lambda13=Lambda14, while the displayed ordinary array has Lambda13=0<Lambda14. Section6.1 of the joint evidence gives an explicit polynomial twelve-edge correction Delta L=rho A_cyc T^-1 with A_cyc skew, A_cyc e=A_cyc p=0; it preserves ALL flow and ALL entries of M. At rho=-2K/9 it changes Lambda12 andLambda13 to0 andLambda14 to bK, and recovers every coefficient of the ordinary array. This is not a counterexample to the original target: C<193/25, since the polynomial C^2-(A+4)C-4A is positive at193/25 (use A<121/50).", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["c142e479a2a094ae"], "fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "target_claim": "Every full zero-remainder four-step primitive certificate has a nonnegative seven signed suffix/adjacent mode representation.", "status": "refuted by joint unverified construction; not a fact", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Original all20 nonnegative multipliers", "Exact original value flow", "M=0 on all five independent formal gradients", "Positive four-step word with equal last two steps"], "impact": "Must release a specific remote forward-entry equality or the prescribed mode family."}, "glossary": {"A_child": "1+sqrt2, the capacity of the left one-step child in this counterexample.", "b_bridge": "Positive root b(A_child+4+b)=4A_child.", "kappa_join": "C^2/16, the right-child scale in the accepted primitive composition.", "K_join": "kappa_join-1>0.", "J_interval_ab": "Row-sum-zero interval matrix with the offdiagonal formula in the evidence.", "B_interval_i": "Cumulative terminal-outgoing coefficients in the seven-mode representation."}}
{"id": "8e5cafa8c08e78f4", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T19:49:59.232143+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max45 actual-original-word failure: every positive-power nested dual-normal profile Ldual_i=(Ttail_i/(eta-1))^p, p>0 and eta>1, violates Q_(0,2)+Q_(2,1)+Q_(1,0)>0 on every ORIGINAL H_m,m>=4. The failure is uniform in horizon-dependent p,eta and survives arbitrary value reassignment. Nonconvex inverse-normal profiles remain open.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED, jointly derived in89aa6dc614ddac9f; no fact gate. Original word: lambda=1+sqrt2,beta=1-1/m,c=lambda(1+1/m),a=beta/c,H_0=(),H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),n=2^m-1,all children use the SAME m. The complete construction and exact failed cycle follow.\n\n## 2. An explicit nested-normal geometry and all its reverse constraints\n\nFirst take an arbitrary word with h_i>1 and eta>1. Write\n\n    tau=eta-1,\n    Ttail_i=tau+sum_(t=i)^(n-1) h_t,       0<=i<=n,\n    d_i=h_i-1.\n\nChoose finite numbers Ldual_i with\n\n    Ldual_0>=...>=Ldual_n=1.\n\nThese are inputs, not unknown feasible Gram entries. In the orthonormal\nbasis e_0,...,e_n set\n\n    rdual_n=e_n,\n    rdual_i=rdual_(i+1)+sqrt(Ldual_i-Ldual_(i+1))*e_i,\n    delta_i=Ttail_i/Ldual_i,\n    y_i=delta_i*rdual_i,\n    x_n=eta*e_n, g_n=e_n,\n    g_i=(y_i-x_(i+1))/d_i,  x_i=y_i+g_i,       i=n-1,...,0. (2)\n\nEvery vector is real. The physical update is literal:\nx_i-h_i*g_i=y_i-d_i*g_i=x_(i+1). The dependence of g_i includes\nthe full future position and the independent accumulated dual normal;\nit is not a scalar multiple of g_(i+1) plus a fresh innovation.\n\nInductively, ||rdual_i||^2=Ldual_i and\n\n    <rdual_i,g_j>=1                 for every j>=i.          (3)\n\nIndeed rdual_i differs from rdual_(i+1) by a vector orthogonal to\nthe whole suffix. Thus it pairs to1 with every already constructed\nfuture gradient, and\n\n    <rdual_i,x_(i+1)>=eta+sum_(t>i)h_t=Ttail_i-d_i.\n\nUsing delta_i*Ldual_i=Ttail_i in(2) then proves(3) also for j=i.\nIn particular <y_i,g_i>=delta_i>=0. Prescribe formal values\n\n    f_i=delta_i+||g_i||^2/2,\n    x_*=g_*=f_*=0.\n\nDirect expansion in the accepted convention gives\n\n    Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n        =<y_j,g_i-g_j>.                                   (4)\n\nThus Q_(j,i)=0 for every i<j, Q_(i,*)=0, and\nQ_(*,i)=-delta_i. The reverse rows remain necessary and have NOT\nbeen removed.\n\nThere is an exact scalar form of every remaining row. Put\n\n    Adual_i=1/Ldual_i,  Delta_i=Adual_(i+1)-Adual_i>=0,\n    Epair_(j,i)=Adual_j*(1-<rdual_j,g_i>),    i<j,\n    Epair_(j,j)=0.\n\nExpanding x_(i+1) as eta*g_n+sum_(t>i)h_t*g_t gives\n\n    d_i*Epair_(j,i)\n       =Ttail_i*(Adual_j-Adual_i)\n          -sum_(k=i+1)^(j-1) h_k*Epair_(j,k).              (5)\n\nThis retains every earlier/future pair. In particular\n\n    Epair_(2,1)=Ttail_1*Delta_1/d_1,\n    Epair_(2,0)=[Ttail_0*Delta_0\n                    +(h_0-Ttail_1/d_1)*Delta_1]/d_0,\n    Q_(0,2)=-Ttail_2*Epair_(2,0).                          (6)\n\nEquations(2)--(6) are an explicit geometric audit, not a promise that\narbitrary Ldual satisfies interpolation. They are not offered as a\nfree feasibility oracle for the main problem.\n\n## 3. The entire positive-power policy fails an actual cycle\n\nThe natural concrete policy is\n\n    Ldual_i=(Ttail_i/tau)^p,       p>0.                     (7)\n\nIt satisfies the required monotonicity, so the real-vector construction\nexists for every p>0. It also includes horizon-dependent p and eta.\nNevertheless it is NOT an interpolable trajectory on the original H_m.\n\nThe first two physical steps are\n\n    u=h_0=1+a,  v=h_1=1+beta,  d_0=a, d_1=beta.\n\nLet P_i=sum_(t<i)h_t and Ttail_0=T. As a function of P, the inverse\nnormal size in(7) is Adual(P)=tau^p*(T-P)^(-p). Its derivative is\npositive and strictly increasing on these intervals. Therefore\n\n    Delta_0/u < Delta_1/v,       Delta_1>0.                (8)\n\nMore generally the non-strict version of(8), with Delta_1>0, holds\nfor any convex increasing profile on the first two physical intervals.\n\nSubstitute Delta_0<=u*Delta_1/v and Ttail_0=Ttail_1+u into(6):\n\n    Q_(0,2) >= [Ttail_2*Delta_1/(a*beta*v)]\n                 *[(v-u*beta)*Ttail_1-u*beta*(u+v)].        (9)\n\nEvery factor outside the bracket is positive. For m>=4,\na<1/2, beta<1, u<3/2, v<2, and Ttail_1>n-1>=14. Hence\n\n    v-u*beta=1-a*beta>1/2,\n    u*beta*(u+v)<21/4,\n    (v-u*beta)*Ttail_1-u*beta*(u+v)>7/4>0.                 (10)\n\nThus Q_(0,2)>0 for every policy in(7), without any restriction on\nthe growth of p or eta with m. From(3),(4), Q_(2,1)=Q_(1,0)=0.\nConsequently\n\n                    Q_(0,2)+Q_(2,1)+Q_(1,0)>0.           (11)\n\nThe function-value terms cancel from this sum. Changing ALL sampled\nvalues, choosing a different minimum value, or moving a minimizer\ntherefore cannot make these same points and gradients smoothly\nconvexly interpolable. It is an actual three-point cyclic failure,\nnot merely an inconvenient choice of projection-conjugate heights.\n\nThe p=0 boundary has Ldual_i=1 and g_i=e_n at every sample, as follows\nby induction in(2). It is the constant-gradient Huber channel and\nhas no positive increment in(8). Nonconvex profiles of Adual, including\nprofiles with strongly unequal early increments, remain outside(9)'s\nexclusion. The claim is not that all nested-normal geometries fail.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["89aa6dc614ddac9f", "216eb3b9ac730b92"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "target_claim": "positive power policies yield interpolable nested-normal data", "status": "refuted_in_unverified_joint", "assumptions_satisfied": ["real explicit PSD vectors", "all physical updates", "all chronological forward normal ties", "optimum rows for the displayed values"], "failed_conclusion": "three-point cyclic interpolation"}, "glossary": {"Ttail_i": "eta-1 plus all physical steps from i onward.", "Ldual_i": "Squared norm of the nested dual normal.", "rdual_i": "Nested dual normal with orthogonal increments.", "Adual_i": "1/Ldual_i.", "Delta_i": "Adual_(i+1)-Adual_i.", "Epair_(j,i)": "Adual_j*(1-<rdual_j,g_i>).", "tau": "eta-1.", "eta": "Terminal radial multiplier x_n=eta*e_n.", "p": "Any positive exponent in this specific power policy.", "u": "First original physical step1+a.", "v": "Second original physical step1+beta."}}
{"id": "10d581296165c8a8", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T19:49:59.271307+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max45 ORIGINAL physical pair(3,8) refutes the zero-reserve-growth signed repair kappa_j=s+1 on every H_m,m>=4, even at eta1: Q_(3,8)=[(s+1)^2/(d_low*d_next)-1]*(eta-1+s*q_2)>0 and Q_(8,3)=0. Data are explicit and PSD, but no reassignment of values or minimizer can fix the two-cycle. Intermediate uncapped coefficients are not excluded.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED; the complete joint89aa6dc614ddac9f proves this independently of prior unverified drafts. Fix lambda=1+sqrt2,beta=1-1/m,c=lambda(1+1/m),a=beta/c and original H_0=(),H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j),n=2^m-1,with the SAME parameters in all children. The following block definitions are first stated for the legal capped family; for this counterexample keep the exact vector/physical recurrences and set kappa_j=s+1, outside the cap. The claimed legality of the capped rule is NOT imported for that changed parameter.\n\n## 4. A legal signed law on every actual H_2 block and larger bridge\n\nFor the original word let\n\n    s=sum H_2=3+2a+beta,\n    Ccap=(s+1)/2,\n    Nphase=2^(m-2)-1,\n    Lcount=2^(m-3).\n\nThere are Nphase bridges and Nphase+1 blocks in the exact decomposition\n\n    H_m=(H_2,b_0,H_2,b_1,...,b_(Nphase-1),H_2).\n\nThe bridge at physical index4j+3 is\n\n    b_j=1+d_j,\n    d_j=a*c^(2+nu_2(j+1)),       0<=j<Nphase,              (13)\n\nwhere nu_2(k) is the exponent of2 dividing the positive integer k.\nNo word has been replaced by a compressed algorithm: the individual\nqueries inside each H_2 are supplied explicitly below. Formula(13)\nuses the SAME enclosing a,c even in the smallest blocks.\n\nChoose ANY eta>=1 and ANY sequence\n\n    0<=kappa_j<=Ccap,       0<=j<Nphase.\n\nThese may depend on m and on each physical bridge separately. Put\nrhold_j=s+1-kappa_j, so rhold_j>=Ccap>=kappa_j. Use the orthonormal\nbasis e_0,...,e_(Nphase). At the end of the last block set\n\n    G_(Nphase)=e_(Nphase),\n    Y_(Nphase)=(eta-1)*e_(Nphase),\n    X_(Nphase)=eta*e_(Nphase),\n    q_(Nphase)=1, delta_(Nphase)=eta-1,\n    Znormal_(Nphase)=(eta-1)^2.\n\nConstruct backward for j=Nphase-1,...,0 by\n\n    delta_j=delta_(j+1)+rhold_j*q_(j+1),\n    v_j=sqrt((d_j+kappa_j)*delta_j)/d_j,\n    t_j=-kappa_j/d_j,\n    G_j=t_j*G_(j+1)+v_j*e_j,\n    Y_j=Y_(j+1)+rhold_j*G_(j+1)+d_j*v_j*e_j,\n    X_j=Y_j+G_j.                                         (14)\n\nHere q_j=||G_j||^2 and Znormal_j=||Y_j||^2; their completely specified\nscalar recurrences are\n\n    q_j=[kappa_j^2*q_(j+1)+(d_j+kappa_j)*delta_j]/d_j^2,\n    Znormal_j=Znormal_(j+1)+2*rhold_j*delta_(j+1)\n                  +rhold_j^2*q_(j+1)+(d_j+kappa_j)*delta_j. (15)\n\nThe quantities under the square roots are positive: q_(j+1)>0 and\nrhold_j>0 imply delta_j>0. The vectors therefore realize a PSD Gram\nmatrix in dimension Nphase+1. Direct multiplication gives\n\n    <Y_j,G_j>=delta_j,\n    <Y_j,G_(j+1)>=delta_j,\n    X_j=X_(j+1)+s*G_(j+1)+b_j*G_j.                        (16)\n\nFor example the first identity uses\nt_j*delta_j+d_j*v_j^2=delta_j. For the third, the coefficient identity\nrhold_j+t_j=s+1+b_j*t_j and d_j+1=b_j suffice.\n\nWrite H_2=(u,v,u), where u=1+a and v=1+beta. Define remaining\nblock masses a_rem,0=s, a_rem,1=u+v, a_rem,2=u, a_rem,3=0.\nFor every phase j and r=0,1,2,3 provide the ORIGINAL physical sample\n\n    x_(4j+r)=X_j+a_rem,r*G_j,\n    g_(4j+r)=G_j,\n    f_(4j+r)=delta_j+(a_rem,r+1/2)*q_j.                   (17)\n\nThe last sample4*Nphase+3 is precisely n=2^m-1. Within each block\nsubtracting its next physical step decreases a_rem by that step.\nAt its bridge, (16) gives\n\n    x_(4j+3)-b_j*G_j=X_(j+1)+s*G_(j+1)=x_(4j+4).\n\nThus every physical update is correct. Different phases have distinct\ngradients because v_j>0 gives a fresh nonzero coordinate. For strictly\npositive kappa_j the adjacent phases have negative inner products;\nwithin each physical H_2 block the correlations are positive.\n\n\nFor projection-form values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 the original residual is Q_ij=<x_j-g_j,g_i-g_j>. At all phase ends in the changed rule, <Y_j,G_k>=eta-1 for every k>=j, by descending induction. The following exact original-word failure completes the counterexample.\n\n## 9. The zero-growth signed repair fails physical pair(3,8)\n\nThe bound(26) suggests increasing kappa until rhold=0. Test precisely\n\n                 kappa_j=s+1=:kappa_full  for every j.     (34)\n\nApply the SAME explicit recurrences(14),(15) without the cap. The\nradicands are still nonnegative: delta_j=eta-1=tau for every phase,\nand v_j^2=(d_j+kappa_full)*tau/d_j^2. The physical recursions also\nstill hold. Thus this is real PSD data, including tau=0, but it is\nnot an interpolable gradient trajectory.\n\nSince rhold=0, the end normals satisfy\nY_j=Y_(j+1)+d_j*v_j*e_j. They pair to tau with EVERY future gradient.\nMeanwhile the first two bridge coefficients give\n\n    G_0=Pbad*G_2+z, z in span{e_0,e_1},\n    Pbad=kappa_full^2/(d_low*d_next)>1.                   (35)\n\nTo check the strict sign on the original parameters, for m>=4\none has a>6/25,beta>=3/4, so s+1>5. Also\nc<25/8 and d_low<5/2 imply\nd_low*d_next=c*d_low^2<625/32<25<(s+1)^2.\n\nThe physical index3 is the END of phase0, while index8 is the START\nof phase2. Its normal is Y_2+s*G_2, and its gradient is G_2. The\nnormal at3 is Y_0, with gradient G_0. The original residuals are\n\n    Q_(3,8)=(Pbad-1)*(tau+s*q_2)>0,\n    Q_(8,3)=<Y_0,G_2-G_0>=0.                             (36)\n\nThe strict positivity includes eta=1: then q_2>0 because the\nnonzero scalar backward coefficients preserve the nonzero terminal\ngradient, and the actual phase-start mass s is positive. Using only\nphase ends would miss this failure at eta=1.\n\nThe two-cycle sum in(36) is independent of values. Even freely\nreassigning the values or changing the optimum cannot repair these\nsame points and gradients. We do not claim that all other rows pass,\nnor that every choice Ccap<kappa_j<s+1 fails. Formula(36) closes the\nspecified zero-growth proposal, not that entire remaining interval.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["89aa6dc614ddac9f", "216eb3b9ac730b92"], "fact_ids": ["1252b8823bb5a290"], "target_claim": "setting all holding reserve coefficients to zero repairs the signed block payoff", "status": "refuted_in_unverified_joint", "assumptions_satisfied": ["explicit real PSD vectors", "all physical gradient-descent recursions", "nonnegative innovation squares", "arbitrary terminal eta>=1"], "failed_conclusion": "original two-cycle interpolation at physical indices3 and8"}, "glossary": {"s": "Actual original H_2 mass3+2a+beta.", "Nphase": "Number2^(m-2)-1 of actual large bridges.", "d_low": "Original level2 excess beta*c.", "d_next": "Original level3 excess beta*c^2.", "eta": "Any terminal radial multiplier at least1.", "tau": "eta-1.", "G_j": "Gradient prescribed throughout actual H_2 block j.", "Y_j": "Normal at the physical end4j+3 of block j.", "q_2": "Squared norm of G_2, strictly positive.", "Pbad": "(s+1)^2/(d_low*d_next), strictly greater than1.", "kappa_full": "The uncapped zero-growth choice s+1."}}
{"id": "7525661fa05ff48f", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T20:01:59.003444+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact scope counterexample for round44: terminal-difference-square remainders are NOT a lossless reduction, even for equal-tail words and equal remote first-row coefficients. Thus M=sum_(i=0)^3(i+1)^2(e_i-e_(i+1))(e_i-e_(i+1))^T, which is PSD on allR5 and rank4. Here e_i is an original standard coordinate column. The same argument is proved jointly in Section7 of b9ea65c8c8fdf1ec and remains unverified.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["b9ea65c8c8fdf1ec"], "fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "target_claim": "Every equal-tail full primitive word has a terminal-square remainder after re-selection.", "status": "refuted_at_joint_draft_level_not_verified", "impact": "Prevents extrapolating the round44 construction obstruction to the unrestricted equal-tail or four-step cone.", "new_numerical_evaluations": 0, "new_fact_submit_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"e_i": "The ith original standard coordinate column on the five formal gradients.", "kappa_sq": "The nonnegative coefficient of the proposed pure terminal-gradient square."}}
{"id": "d633fc3667cb97e9", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T20:02:51.959591+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Exact deletion check for the free-word capacity theorem: terminal product balance alone allows capacity above C_2. The proper-interval inequalities cannot be omitted; this is not a primitive certificate or an original-cone survivor.", "evidence": "Let r=2, t_0=1/3, t_1=(-5+2sqrt(13))/3>0, h_i=1+t_i, and A=1+h_0+h_1=(5+2sqrt(13))/3. Since lambda<17/7 and this polynomial at X=81/20 and lambda=17/7 equals247/2800>0, C_2<81/20. Thus A>C_2 despite global balance. The first singleton interval fails exactly: (1+h_0)t_0=(7/3)(1/3)=7/9<1. It also violates positive-factor prefix necessity since P_1=7/3<R_1=3. This is included in the complete joint draftc1d41aaebcae37de, whose factor and interval arguments are explicitly unverified. {"id": "d7ce8d2534b77861", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T20:23:07.983541+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max2 round38 exact UNVERIFIED counterexample to losslessness of finite SIGNED two-sequence factorization: h=(sqrt2,2,sqrt2) has an explicit complete original nonnegative array with exact flow and M=0, but NO full certificate for this same word can have one finite real factor pair. The displayed array has an explicit TWO-pair representation. This does not refute capacity<=C_r for the full M=0 cone.", "evidence": "Status: UNVERIFIED counterexample and joint direct proof, max2 round38. No formal call. The target being refuted is the assertion that every full original nonnegative primitive certificate with M=0 admits one finite REAL two-sequence factorization, or even that every word with such a full certificate admits some certificate of that form. This is not a counterexample to a capacity bound for the entire zero-remainder cone.\n\nSet w=sqrt(2), lambda=1+w, kappa=lambda^2=2lambda+1, and h=(w,2,w). There are FOUR independent original formal gradients g_0,...,g_3 and values f_0,...,f_3. Define x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)h_t g_t and Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2. Put s=sum h=2lambda, A=1+s=kappa, and V_h=2sum_(i<3)h_i(f_i-f_3)+sum_(i<3)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-sA||g_3||^2.\n\nIn original order0,1,2,3, use the COMPLETE array\nLambda=[\n[0,2,0,0],\n[2lambda,0,0,kappa-1],\n[0,(kappa-1)w,0,2kappa+(kappa-1)w],\n[0,kappa-1,2kappa*lambda,0]\n].\nAll entries are nonnegative. Their row sums minus column sums are (-2w,-4,-2w,4lambda), the exact original flow. Indeed lambda*w=lambda+1: at nodes0,1,2 the differences are 2-2lambda, 4lambda-(4lambda+4), and (8lambda+6)-(10lambda+4), respectively; the last is4lambda.\n\nFor a single step w, direct expansion gives V_(w)=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10; the mixed gradient term vanishes since (w-1)lambda=1. Accepted fact04219554e41d9750, proof Section11, supplies the following exact primitive join identity on ALL original coordinates. Two children of capacity lambda joined by bridge2 have d=1 solving d(2lambda+d)=lambda^2, and\nV_h=V_left+kappa V_right-(kappa-1)[w Q_21+Q_31+Q_13+w Q_23].\nInsert V_left=-2Q_01-2lambda Q_10 and V_right=-2Q_23-2lambda Q_32. This proves V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=0 as a polynomial identity. For T_ii=1,T_it=h_t for t<i and0 for t>i, e=ones(4), L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, and D_prim=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<3,-s(s+2)), the accepted original identity therefore gives M_prim=D_prim+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2=0 on all R^4. No gradient identification, support deletion, numerical PSD test, or trajectory-only claim is used.\n\nNow define exactly the proposed one-pair representation. Let t_i=h_i-1, t_3=0, U_-1=D_4=0, and allow arbitrary finite real U_0,U_1,U_2,D_1,D_2,D_3. For all i<j set\nmu_ij=(U_i-U_(i-1))(D_j+t_jD_(j+1)),\nmu_ji=(U_(i-1)+t_iU_i)(D_j-D_(j+1)),\nLambda_ij=2mu_ji.\nAll original entries must be nonnegative and the full identity must hold. Write Qbar_ij=-2Q_ji, so V_h=sum mu Qbar.\n\nWe prove that NO certificate for this same h can have this representation, independently of the particular displayed array. The terminal incoming sum is U_2D_3; its squared-gradient coefficient is s-2U_2D_3. Matching -sA forces U_2D_3=s(s+2)/2>0. A common change of both factor signs gives D_3>0 without altering any entry. The nonnegative entries mu_i3=(U_i-U_(i-1))D_3 force 0<=U_0<=U_1<=U_2. If U_0=0, the whole row and column at0 in mu vanish, contradicting its outgoing-minus-incoming flow d_0=h_0=w. Thus U_0>0. Since t_0=w-1>0, entries mu_j0=t_0U_0(D_j-D_(j+1)) give D_1>=D_2>=D_3>0.\n\nLet p_1=U_0D_1. The incoming sum at0 is J_0=t_0p_1. The coefficient of ||g_0||^2 in sum mu Qbar is -w+2t_0J_0. Matching -wt_0 yields\nt_0^2(2p_1+1)=1,\nhence p_1=(lambda^2-1)/2=lambda.\nThe actual flow at0 telescopes to\nd_0=U_0[(1-t_0)D_1+h_1D_2+h_2D_3]\n   =(1-t_0)lambda+U_0(2D_2+wD_3)\n   =w+U_0(2D_2+wD_3)>w.\nThis contradicts the exact required flow. Every factor-sign and zero-factor possibility has been covered, so the failure is not merely the failure of one chosen positive representation.\n\nFor the displayed array precisely TWO factor pairs suffice. Use the same t in each copy of the formula and take\nU^(1)=(1,0,0), D^(1)=(lambda,0,0),\nU^(2)=(0,1,kappa+1), D^(2)=(0,lambda,lambda).\nThe first pair has only mu^(1)_01=lambda, mu^(1)_10=1.\nThe second has only\nmu^(2)_12=lambda*w, mu^(2)_13=lambda, mu^(2)_31=lambda,\nmu^(2)_23=kappa*lambda, mu^(2)_32=lambda+kappa+1.\nEach is nonnegative, and Lambda=2(mu^(1)+mu^(2))^T gives the displayed full array because lambda*w=lambda+1. Neither component satisfies the full word's prescribed flow and diagonal matching on its own. Therefore this is not a nonnegative mixture of two full-word feasible one-pair certificates.\n\nConclusion: finite signed one-pair factorization is not lossless even for the original M=0 cone; the explicit array has exact factor-pair count2. Its capacity is the basic C_3 and it is not a strict-threshold improving seed. The hereditary interval-product inequality for arbitrary full M=0 arrays remains unproved, not refuted by this example. All statements here remain UNVERIFIED; the only established input used is accepted fact04219554e41d9750.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "finding_ids": ["ac0c1e866da0d926", "80fe44b008f89877"], "target_claim": "Every original nonnegative M=0 word admits a finite real one-pair two-sequence certificate.", "status": "refuted_in_unverified_joint_draft", "formal_gate": "closed"}, "glossary": {"w_sf38": "sqrt(2)", "kappa_sf38": "(1+sqrt(2))^2, the capacity of the explicit length-three word", "mu_sf38": "Auxiliary oriented interpolation array, Lambda=2mu^T", "U_sf38": "Finite real first factor sequence in the displayed formula", "D_sf38": "Finite real second factor sequence in the displayed formula", "factor_pair_count_sf38": "Minimum number of pairs (U,D) whose generated auxiliary arrays sum to the displayed mu; exactly2 for this explicit array"}}

For the original matrix, e=ones(r+1), T_ii=1, T_it=h_t for t<i and zero for t>i, L_flow=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, D=diag(h_i(2-h_i) for i<r,-s(s+2)), and M=D+(L_flow T+T^T L_flow^T)/2. All original gradients remain independent. Use V_h and Q_ij with exactly the definitions in accepted04219554e41d9750 Section10, reproduced in the joint report821f43aa871af2a2; the construction below invokes only that accepted fact and directly checks the child array.\n\n## 7. Exact full-array counterexample to the entrywise sign shortcut\n\nThis construction refutes only the stronger claim that every summand\nof C_R is nonpositive for a prefix interval. It DOES NOT refute heredity.\n\nLet d be the positive root of d^2+5d-4=0, put\n\n C=5+d, kappa=C^2/16, K=kappa-1,\n h=(1+d,3/2,3/2), s=4+d.\n\nThe polynomial is negative at0, positive at1 and strictly increasing\non [0,infinity), so 0<d<1. (15)\n\nEvery entry is nonnegative, including the two stated off-diagonal zeros.\nThe root equation implies dC=4 and\n\n                    K(4-d)=2(1+d).                   (16)\n\nIdentity (16) follows from the accepted join identity with child\ncapacities1 and4; it is also checked directly as follows:\n\n (C^2-16)(4-d)-32(1+d)\n = (9+10d+d^2)(4-d)-32-32d\n = 4-d-6d^2-d^3\n = -(1+d)(d^2+5d-4),\n\nwhich is zero. Thus the four row-minus-column sums in (15) are\n\n K(d-4), -3kappa+3K, -3kappa+3K,\n                            6kappa-K(d+2)\n = -2(1+d), -3, -3, 8+2d,\n\nthe exact required flow.\n\nFor the full matrix check, the two-step child (3/2,3/2) has array\n\n Lambda_child=[ [0,1,1/2], [7/2,0,17/2], [1,14,0] ].\n\nIts flow is (-3,-3,6), its L_child T_child is exactly\n\n [ -3/4, -7/4, -1/2 ]\n [  7/4, -3/4, -17/2]\n [  1/2, 17/2, 15   ],\n\nand its D_child is diag(3/4,3/4,-15). Hence its remainder is zero on\nall three independent formal gradients. The empty left child has\ncapacity1 and zero remainder. Fact04219554e41d9750, Section11, applied\nwith right capacity4 and d(1+4+d)=4, gives\n\n V_h=kappa V_child-K[(3/2)Q_10+(3/2)Q_20+Q_30\n                               +d Q_03+(3/2)Q_13+(3/2)Q_23].\n\nInserting the just-checked child identity gives exactly every entry\nof (15). Therefore its FULL original remainder M is identically zero;\nno restricted Gram test, quotient, or unidentified gradient is involved.\n\nNow take the prefix interval I=[0,2). Its local data are\n\n v_0=1, v_1=-d, p=d/2, H=5/2+d,\n S_3-S_2=3/2, mu_30=Lambda_03/2=Kd/2.\n\nThe (j,i)=(3,0) summand of C_R is\n\n                 (3Kd^2/8)(1-d/2)>0.                (17)\n\nThus even an actual full M=0 array does not give a nonpositive sign\nto each right-boundary term. The opposing (3,1) term is negative.\nTheir total is nonpositive, as required for this particular interval:\n\n (1+H)q=(7/2+d)d/2=2-3d/4>1.\n\nThis is a counterexample to an attempted proof mechanism only. For\nclarity, the entire word in (15) satisfies heredity: its singleton\nproducts are d(d+2)=4-3d>1 and5/4,5/4; the left two-step interval\nhas product2-3d/4>1; the right two-step interval has product1;\nthe full interval has product C d/4=1.\n\nNo capacity comparison or original minimax disproof is asserted.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["821f43aa871af2a2", "c4a1ca67043f9e12"], "fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "branch_id": "max2_full_m0_interval_heredity_round39", "target_claim": "Every individual term of the right-boundary sum C_R is nonpositive.", "status": "refuted stronger proof shortcut only; original interval implication unresolved"}, "glossary": {"d": "Positive root of d^2+5d-4=0.", "C": "Counterexample word capacity5+d.", "kappa": "C^2/16.", "K": "kappa-1.", "S_i": "Chronological prefix step sum sum_(t<i)h_t.", "mu_ji": "Transposed half multiplier Lambda_ij/2.", "v_i": "Prefix quadratic gradient mode, v_0=1 and v_(i+1)=-(h_i-1)v_i.", "p": "Signed mode endpoint v_k for prefix [0,k).", "C_R": "Sum_(j>=k,i<k)mu_ji(S_j-S_k)p(v_i-p), the exact right boundary sum."}}

The p40 increments obey\n\n <p40,u40>=1-eta40[mu40+(2/3)(1-nu40)]>59/100,\n <p40,u40+v40>\n =1+eta40[-4/3-mu40+(2/3)nu40]+(1-mu40)eta40^2>19/100.\n\nThe q40 increments obey\n\n <q40,u40>=-eta40[mu40+(2/3)(1-nu40)]<0,\n <q40,v40>=(1-mu40)eta40^2>0.\n\nFinally\n\n <w40,v40>=-mu40 eta40^2<0,\n <w40,u40+v40>\n   =eta40[(2/3)nu40-mu40(1+eta40)]<eta40(7/15-1/2)<0.\n\nAt p40 the normal must pair nonnegatively with u40 and u40+v40;\nat q40 it must pair nonpositively with u40 and nonnegatively with v40;\nat w40 it must pair nonpositively with v40 and u40+v40. The displayed\ninequalities establish all these conditions at all six original queries.\nThey establish one actual static trajectory, without deleting its unit\nstep or imposing equalities on any formal certificate coordinate.\n\nThe actual values are f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. For EVERY ordered pair,\nincluding both directions involving the terminal and optimum samples,\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2\n      =<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.\n\nThis follows from the complete normal checks, and0 belongs to K40.\nIn particular Q_(i,star)=0. Original Goal OPEN.\n\nThe claim tested is that strict original global cuts together with an\ninternal unit step suffice for a primitive seed at capacity99/10.\nThis exact example refutes that claim at draft level in the full\noriginal30-multiplier/six-gradient PSD cone.\n\nTake the length5 physical word\n\n h=(7/5,1,491/200,489/200,8/5), s=sum h=89/10, A=1+s=99/10.\n\nThe five quantities\n\n (1+sum_(i<j)h_i)(1+sum_(i>j)h_i)-A(h_j-1)_+\n\nare, chronologically,\n\n (227/50,18,5497/2000,367/400,59/25)>0.\n\nThus every original scalar global cut passes strictly. The\nunit-curvature product is zero. Both endpoints and the two middle\nsteps differ; the unit is at index1, not at the center.\n\nHere is the complete actual static-function witness. Set\n\n P=17/5, D=13/5, B=291/200, C=289/200, U=13/2,\n kappa=B^2 C+U(D-BC)=50344109/8000000,\n t=B^2 C/kappa=24472809/50344109,\n u=(1,0), w=(t,sqrt(t(1-t))),\n v=-(B u+D w)/(1+C), K=conv{u,v,w},\n F(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nWe have0<t<1 and D-BC=19901/40000>0. Completing the square shows\nthat grad F(x)=P_K(x), the Euclidean projection onto K. To justify\nsmoothness directly, add the two projection normal inequalities to get\n||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>. Hence P_K is1-Lipschitz.\nThe two maximizing inequalities bound\nF(x+z)-F(x)-<P_K(x),z> between0 and ||z||^2, proving differentiability.\nThus F is convex, differentiable, and1-smooth. The positive relation\nB u+(1+C)v+D w=0 and linear independence of u,w put0 in the interior\nof the nondegenerate triangle K. Consequently0 is the unique minimizer:\nF(0)=0 and, for x!=0, a small positive multiple of x lies in K and\ngives a strictly positive value in the defining maximum.\n\nDefine the actual query arrays\n\n x=( (17/5)u,2u,u,-B u,D w,w ),\n g=( u,u,u,v,w,w ).\n\nEvery identity x_(i+1)=x_i-h_i g_i holds. In particular\n-B u-(489/200)v=D w by the definition of v, and\nD w-(8/5)w=w. It remains to prove these are the actual gradients.\n\nTheir normal vectors y_i=x_i-g_i are\n\n y=( (12/5)u,u,0,-B u-v,(8/5)w,0 ).\n\nThe exact inner products are\n\n <u,w>=||w||^2=t,\n <u,v>=-(B+D t)/(1+C), <w,v>=-(B+D)t/(1+C).\n\nAt either nonzero prefix normal, comparison with w-u is nonpositive\nbecause t-1<0; comparison with v-u is nonpositive because\n-(B+D t)/(1+C)-1<0. For the middle normal n=-B u-v,\n\n <w-v,n>=(-B^2 C+kappa*t)/(1+C)^2=0,\n <u-v,n>=-BC(1-t)/(1+C)<0.\n\nThese formulas follow by expanding n=(-BC u+D w)/(1+C); the first\nt coefficient is D(B+1+C+D)-BC(1+C+D)=kappa, and the second\nformula differs by that same zero numerator. At the suffix normal,\n<u-w,w>=0 and <v-w,w>=-t[1+(B+D)/(1+C)]<0. The two remaining\nnormals are zero. This checks the entire normal cone of K at every\noriginal query, so g_i=P_K(x_i)=grad F(x_i) for all six indices.\n\nSet the actual values f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2, and add the optimum\njet x_star=g_star=f_star=0. For every original ordered pair define\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nDirect expansion gives Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>. All30 finite inequalities\nand both optimum orientations are therefore nonpositive by the cone\nchecks, with Q_(i,star)=0. Nothing about actual value assignment,\nremote interpolation, terminal interpolation, or the true minimum\nis omitted. The true squared initial distance is ||x_0||^2=289/25.\n\nThe original primitive energy is\n\n V_h=2sum_(i<5)h_i(f_i-f_5)\n       +sum_(i<5)h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2-s(s+1)||g_5||^2.\n\nThe norm telescope\n||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2=2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2\nimplies V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_5+s g_5||^2. Since x_5=g_5=w,\n\n V_h=289/25-(9801/100)(24472809/50344109)\n    =-36332042201/1006882180<0.\n\nAccepted04219554e41d9750 gives, for every full original nonnegative\nmultiplier array Lambda with the required flow,\n\n V_h+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij\n     =<M_prim,Gram(g_0,...,g_5)>,\n\nwhere M_prim is exactly the original full matrix in TASK.md.\nAll Q_ij are nonpositive while V_h<0, so the right side is negative.\nIt cannot arise from a PSD M_prim paired with this PSD Gram. {"id": "8011b6b5fba80423", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T23:05:20.153917+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max49 UNVERIFIED exact original-row counterexamples: actual core queries6,7 do NOT imply a quadratic tail; prescribed G8=X8 forces Q8,0>2. After the actual lower-face query5, continuing its affine line at6 forces Q0,6>20 even with a free new value. These are scoped retained-jet obstructions, repaired by the actual three-face law in the attached joint.", "evidence": "# Max49: three actual faces and a remote-forced return\n\nStatus: COMPLETE JOINT ANALYTICAL DRAFT, UNVERIFIED. No fact submission\nis authorized. This is the final of the two sessions allocated after\ncompleted47, with completed ceiling49 and attempted ceiling50. The\noriginal all-n/all-nonnegative-word exact-profile goal remains OPEN.\n\n## 1. Result and scope\n\nThe construction below changes gradients at the actual queries inside\nthe original H2 blocks. In one fixed planar triangle its exact sequence\nof projection locations is\n\n    original indices 0,1,2,3: the vertical edge,\n    index4: upper sloping edge,\n    index5: lower sloping edge,\n    indices6,7: quadratic core,\n    index8: vertical edge again,\n    every index from9 onward: quadratic core.\n\nThe lower-edge transition at5 happens INSIDE the second physical H2.\nThe return at8 is forced by a remote original interpolation row. In\nparticular, observing core entry at6 does not justify a quadratic tail.\nEvery gradient and value is given by finite formulas; the proof checks\nthe actual projections against the whole fixed triangle and the optimum.\n\nThe result holds uniformly for every integer m>=1000000 and every real\naspect t>=1000. These cutoffs are conservative symbolic enclosures, not\nthe outcome of a fit or a computation. The aspect may depend arbitrarily\non the entire word and on m. Every child uses the SAME enclosing-m\nparameters. There is one static function for each (m,t).\n\nThis explicit family is legal but too weak. Its true-distance payoff\nV49 obeys\n\n    0<V49<2 Qunit,\n    V49<Huber/10<=K_n/10<=F_ray_n/10,\n    V49<(sup_(z in D_h) Gap_h(z))/10,\n    0<B_n V49<10(25/48)^m ->0.\n\nAll comparisons use the same original physical word. D_h is the FULL\nschedule-dependent complex domain, not a lens or a short-cycle screen.\nThe benchmark B_n is the ORIGINAL all-split recurrence. The bounds\nconcern this family, not the unrestricted W_n(h).\n\nTwo precise forbidden continuations are also proved below. Continuing\nthe lower affine face at6 fails original row Q_(0,6), even if its new\nvalue is free but all retained values remain fixed. Continuing the\nquadratic core through8 fails Q_(8,0); the optimum rows force its value,\nso a value repair cannot save that prescribed gradient.\n\n## 2. Correctness inputs and literature use\n\nThe accepted inputs, in their stated scopes, are:\n\n* 1252b8823bb5a290: every original directed smooth-convex interpolation\n  row and both optimum directions are necessary. Its Q convention is\n  used throughout.\n* b98bb807d514e80d: full K_n and the original all-split estimate\n  B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3).\n* 1fca3e5dd555cb4a: full alternating-ray maximum F_ray_n, including\n  every feasible checkpoint set, with K_n<=F_ray_n.\n* dfde2fa326c908bf: full D_h, maximal interpolating Gap_h, and the\n  legal real parameters used in the complete complex comparison.\n\nAccepted e9a55f57461f253c is only an actual H6 result with epsilon1/6,\ndimension66 and a unique minimizer. No new-m transfer or radius claim\nis inferred from its dimension. The previously saved H5/H6 observations\nmotivate changing within-block gradients only. None is recomputed here.\n\nUnverified max48, finding ec0b4a7f59979259, identifies a useful free-aspect\ninterface, but is NOT a correctness input. Every needed formula and\nargument is reproduced here. The older constant-H2 T1/T2 laws are not\nretuned or used as premises.\n\nMatlas returned Lemma S17 of arXiv:2107.07257 and Theorem4/Lemma3 of Jai\nMoondra, Hassan Mortagy and Swati Gupta, \"Reusing Combinatorial Structure:\nFaster Iterative Projections over Submodular Base Polytopes,\"\narXiv:2106.11943v3 (2023). The exact PDF already exists as\ndownloads/2106.11943.pdf. The general-polytope definitions, Section3,\nLemma1, Theorem4 and its proof were read in the\n[arXiv HTML](https://arxiv.org/html/2106.11943v3). The useful\ndistinction is between membership in an affine face and the sign of its\nnormal residual. Here these conditions are proved directly. No external\ntheorem is a proof premise, and no submodular-polytope assumption is\nimposed on the triangle.\n\n## 3. Original word and explicit scalar definitions\n\nFix m>=1000000, t>=1000 and define\n\n    lambda=1+sqrt(2), epsilon=1/m, beta=1-epsilon,\n    c=lambda(1+epsilon), a=beta/c,\n    H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n    h=H_m, n=2^m-1, ell=1+a, b=beta c,\n    A=4+2a+beta, eta=a^2 beta, k=A/(b eta)=A/(a beta^3),\n    s=(b+1)/(k-1), D=b+s,\n    rho=(a k+1)/(k-1), e=b rho-s,\n    N=(A-1)s, C=(A-ell)s, p=ab=beta^2,\n    delta=t^(-2), omega=epsilon+delta,\n    u=N/(1+delta).\n                                                               (1)\n\nAll symbols in this draft are local aliases; none renames a project\nbenchmark or reference. In shared records the new objects carry the\nsuffix49. In particular C in(1) is a scalar, while the convex triangle\nbelow is denoted T49. The letter e in(1) is a positive edge intercept,\nnot the exponential constant, and t is an aspect, not a time index.\n\nDefine the exact further coefficients, in the following order:\n\n    x5=p-ell u,       v5=C-ell u,\n    d5=-(rho x5+v5+e),\n    E5=d5/(rho^2+delta),\n    j5=x5+rho E5,    w5=v5+delta E5,\n    x6=x5-(1+beta)j5, v6=v5-(1+beta)w5,\n    gamma=beta^2 c,\n    x8=gamma x6,     v8=gamma v6,\n    x9=x8-ell,       v9=-a v8.\n                                                               (2)\n\nThese are finite explicit rational expressions in lambda, m and t.\nTheir denominators are positive by the bounds in Section4. There is\nno unknown Gram matrix, optimization oracle, implicit trajectory solve,\nor root-selection problem in these definitions.\n\nUse the three finite vectors\n\n    U=(1,t(D+1)), V=(1,-a t(D+1)), Lvert=(-b,s t),\n    T49=conv{U,V,Lvert},\n    F49(X)=max_(g in T49) {<X,g>-||g||^2/2},\n    X0=(A,t(D+1)).\n                                                               (3)\n\nHere Lvert is a vertex label, not the smoothness constant. All vector\ninner products and distances are Euclidean. The formula specifies one\nstatic function; its actual gradients are proved below rather than\nassumed from the envelope definition.\n\n## 4. Uniform finite-parameter enclosures\n\nThe auxiliary coefficients with final subscript0 are algebraic reference\nvalues at epsilon=delta=0. They are NOT substituted for the actual word.\nPut\n\n    a0=lambda-2=1/lambda, ell0=lambda-1,\n    A0=lambda^2, k0=lambda^3,\n    s0=1/(2lambda-1), D0=lambda+s0, rho0=2s0, e0=1,\n    z0=lambda s0, T0=lambda^2 s0=1+2s0,\n    N0=1+s0, C0=(A0-ell0)s0,\n    x50=z0-ell0, v50=-z0,\n    d50=(3-lambda)s0^2, E50=(3-lambda)/4,\n    j50=-ell0/2, w50=-z0,\n    x60=v60=z0, x80=v80=T0,\n    x90=a0 s0=1/(3lambda+2), v90=-z0.\n                                                               (4)\n\nThese identities follow from lambda^2=2lambda+1. For example\n(lambda^2-2)(lambda+1)=lambda^3-1, which gives s0; then\n(2lambda-1)s0=1 gives rho0=2s0, e0=1, N0=1+s0 and T0=1+2s0.\nAlso (lambda-1)(2lambda-1)=lambda+3. This last equality gives\nx50=z0-ell0, d50=(3-lambda)s0^2, j50=-ell0/2 and x90=a0 s0\nby substituting successively into(1)--(2).\n\nSquaring rational endpoints gives\n\n    12/5<lambda<29/12<5/2,\n    6/23<s0<5/19,\n    29/46<z0<12/19,\n    35/23<T0<29/19,\n    4/37<x90<5/46,\n    1/26<d50<1/24.\n                                                               (5)\n\nFor z0, the function q/(2q-1) decreases for q>1/2, giving the\ndisplayed endpoints. For d50, the respective product bounds are\n21/529 and15/361; they lie strictly between1/26 and1/24.\n\nHere epsilon,delta<=10^(-6) and omega<=2*10^(-6). The basic differences\nare bounded as follows:\n\n    |a-a0|<epsilon, |b-lambda|<epsilon, |A-A0|<3epsilon,\n    |k-k0|<120epsilon, |s-s0|<4epsilon,\n    |D-D0|<5epsilon, |rho-rho0|<3epsilon, |e-1|<13epsilon,\n    |N-N0|<22epsilon, |C-C0|<20epsilon.\n                                                               (6)\n\nHere are details ensuring that(6) is a finite, uniform estimate.\nThe exact identities a=a0(1-epsilon)/(1+epsilon) and\nb=lambda(1-epsilon^2) prove its first bounds. Since\n|beta^3-1|<=3epsilon, one has\n\n    |a beta^3-a0|<(5/2)epsilon,  a beta^3>3/8.\n\nThe quotient difference for k is at most\n(3+15*(5/2))epsilon/(3/8)=108epsilon<120epsilon, because k0<15.\nIt also gives13<k<15. Thus k-1>12 and k0-1>12. The quotient for s\nis bounded by [1+(1/3)120]epsilon/12<4epsilon.\nUsing rho=a+(a+1)/(k-1), the difference for rho is bounded by\n\n    epsilon+epsilon/12+(3/2)*120epsilon/144<3epsilon.\n\nIn particular s<1/3 and rho<3/5. The difference for e=b rho-s\nis below [1+(5/2)*3+4]epsilon<13epsilon. For N, use A0-1<5:\nits difference is below 3epsilon/3+5*4epsilon=21epsilon.\nFor C, use A0-ell0=lambda+2<9/2:\nits difference is below4epsilon/3+(9/2)*4epsilon<20epsilon.\nThese arguments also give the useful strict bounds\n\n    2/5<a<1/2, 12/5<c<5/2, 9/10<beta<1,\n    1/4<s<1/3, 1/2<rho<3/5, 9/10<e<11/10,\n    D>5/2, 18/5<D+1<15/4, N<4/3,\n    0<A<6, b>2, A-1>4.\n                                                               (7)\n\nFor example D0>12/5+6/23>5/2, and the error5epsilon preserves\nthis bound. The sharper lower bound D0+1>12/5+6/23+1>18/5\nproves the stated lower bound on D+1. All margins in(7) exceed\nthe corresponding error in(6).\n\nSince u=N/(1+delta),\n\n    |u-N0|<22epsilon+(4/3)delta,\n    |ell u-ell0 N0|<35epsilon+2delta.\n\nIt follows from |p-1|<2epsilon and(6) that\n\n    |x5-x50|<40omega, |v5-v50|<60omega.\n                                                               (8)\n\nIn particular |x5|<1. In the d5 expression in(2), use rho0<3/5\nto get\n\n    |d5-d50|<3epsilon+(3/5)40omega+60omega+13epsilon\n             <=100omega.\n                                                               (9)\n\nConsequently\n\n    1/27<d5<1/23.\n                                                               (10)\n\nIndeed the larger error allowed in(9) is1/5000, and\n1/26-1/5000>1/27 while1/24+1/5000<1/23.\nAlso rho^2+delta>1/4 and\n|rho^2+delta-rho0^2|<4epsilon+delta. The quotient difference gives\n\n    |E5-E50|<4[100omega+(3/20)(4epsilon+delta)]<410omega.\n\nSince E50<3/20, this implies0<E5<1/6. Substitution into(2) now yields\n\n    |j5-j50|<300omega, |w5-w50|<61omega,\n    |x6-z0|<650omega, |v6-z0|<190omega.\n                                                               (11)\n\nFor the first pair, the respective bounds before rounding are\n40omega+(1/6)3epsilon+(3/5)410omega and60omega+delta/6.\nFor x6 and v6 use1+beta=2-epsilon, |j5|<3/4 and |w5|<2/3;\nthe bounds are40omega+2*300omega+(3/4)epsilon and\n60omega+2*61omega+(2/3)epsilon. They prove(11).\n\nFinally |gamma-lambda|<5epsilon, since\nbeta^2(1+epsilon)=1-epsilon-epsilon^2+epsilon^3. With(11),\n\n    |x8-T0|<1700omega, |v8-T0|<500omega,\n    |x9-x90|<1701omega, |v9+z0|<252omega.\n                                                               (12)\n\nFor x8 the bound is(2/3)5epsilon+(5/2)650omega;\nfor v8 replace650 by190. For v9 use v8<8/5 and a0<1/2.\nEquations(5),(8)--(12) prove the following margins, which will be\nused to select the actual faces:\n\n    -3/4<j5<-2/3, -2/3<w5<-3/5,\n    3/5<x6<2/3, 3/5<v6<2/3,\n    3/2<x8<8/5, 3/2<v8<8/5,\n    1/10<x9<3/25, -2/3<v9<-3/5.\n                                                               (13)\n\nFor the narrowest x9 margin, its error is at most1701/500000,\nand4/37-1701/500000>1/10, while5/46+1701/500000<3/25.\nFor x8, 35/23-1700/500000>3/2 and\n29/19+1700/500000<8/5. The other intervals follow using the\nsmaller errors in(11)--(12) and29/46<z0<12/19.\nThus(13) does not rely on a qualitative continuity assertion.\n\n## 5. The fixed function and the actual first block\n\nThe identities D+1=ks and e+rho=a(D+1) show that T49 is exactly\nthe intersection of the three halfspaces\n\n    X<=1,\n    -t X+Y<=t D,\n    -rho t X-Y<=t e.\n                                                               (14)\n\nThe upper line contains U,Lvert, the lower contains V,Lvert,\nand the vertical line contains U,V. The other vertex in each case\nis strictly on the interior side. All three inequalities are strict\nat0 because D,e>0. Thus T49 has nonempty interior containing0.\n\nFor any X the maximum in(3) exists and is unique, by compactness of\nT49 and strict concavity of the objective in g. Completing the square\nshows that its maximizer is the Euclidean projection of X. A vector\ng in T49 is that projection if and only if\n\n    <X-g,v-g><=0 for EVERY v in T49.\n                                                               (15)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating the squared distance from X along\neach segment from g to v; sufficiency follows by expanding the squared\ndistance. Adding(15) at two inputs proves\n||g(X)-g(Z)||^2<=<g(X)-g(Z),X-Z>, hence g is1-Lipschitz.\nThe two maximizing-objective inequalities give\n\n    0<=F49(X+d)-F49(X)-<g(X),d><=||d||^2.\n\nThis proves differentiability with gradient g, as well as convexity\nand global1-smoothness. Because0 is an interior point of T49, the\nsame formula in(3) is strictly positive at every nonzero X: take a\nsufficiently small positive multiple of X as its feasible g. Therefore\n\n    min F49=0, argmin F49={0},\n    R49^2:=dist(X0,argmin F49)^2=A^2+t^2(D+1)^2.\n                                                               (16)\n\nFor the first actual H2, put\n\n    (P0,P1,P2,P3)=(0,ell,ell+1+beta,A-1),\n    (T0',T1',T2',T3')=(1,-a,a beta,-eta).\n\nThe prime distinguishes these four tangent coefficients from T0 in(4).\nFor0<=i<=3 define\n\n    Xi=(A-Pi, t(D+1)Ti'),\n    Gi=(1,t(D+1)Ti'),\n    Yi=Xi-Gi=(A-1-Pi,0),\n    fi=||Gi||^2/2+(A-1-Pi).\n                                                               (17)\n\nEach Ti' lies in[-a,1], so Gi lies on UV. Each Yi is a nonnegative\nmultiple of the outward normal of the first inequality in(14).\nThus(15) holds against EVERY vertex and Gi is the actual gradient.\nThe tangent relation Ti+1'=(1-h_i)Ti' and the partial sums Pi prove\neach of the three original updates separately. Because a,beta>0 and\neta<a, all these gradients are distinct at consecutive queries.\n\nAt index3 the residual is zero. Using D+1=ks and b eta k=A gives\n\n    X3=G3=(1,-A s t/b),\n    X4=-b X3=(-b,A s t).\n                                                               (18)\n\n## 6. Upper edge4, lower edge5, and the return at8\n\nThe upper normal is n_plus=(-t,1). The difference between its\ninner product at X4 and its supporting value tD is Nt. Subtracting\nthe corresponding normal component gives exactly\n\n    G4=(-b+u,t(s+u)),\n    Y4=X4-G4=(Nt/(1+t^2)) n_plus,\n    f4=<X4,G4>-||G4||^2/2.\n                                                               (19)\n\nThis is a point in the RELATIVE INTERIOR of Lvert U. Its horizontal\nincrement u is positive and smaller than N<4/3<b+1, and it lies\non the upper supporting line. The residual in(19) has positive\ncoefficient. Hence(14)--(15) check the remote vertex V and the\noptimum as well as the two edge vertices. G4 is the actual gradient.\nThe physical step h4=ell gives\n\n    X5=(x5,t v5).\n\nIts violation of the LOWER supporting inequality is exactly t d5>0.\nThe lower normal is n_minus=(-rho t,-1). Its true projected candidate is\n\n    G5=(j5,t w5),\n    Y5=X5-G5=(-rho E5,-delta t E5)\n                 =(t d5/(1+rho^2 t^2)) n_minus,\n    f5=<X5,G5>-||G5||^2/2.\n                                                               (20)\n\nThe definitions in(2) give rho j5+w5+e=0. Also -b<j5<1 by(7),(13).\nThus G5 lies in the relative interior of Lvert V, rather than being\nan endpoint or an affine projection outside the segment. The positive\nnormal coefficient and(14) verify(15) against the REMOTE U as well.\nThis is the second genuine face transition, at an internal query of\nthe second original H2 block.\n\nThe next original step h5=1+beta gives\n\n    X6=(x6,t v6).\n\nBy(13), x6<1, v6-x6<1/15<D, and rho x6+v6+e>0. Therefore\nX6 is in the interior of T49, and G6=X6, f6=||X6||^2/2.\nThe original step h6=ell gives\n\n    X7=-a X6.\n\nFor this point its horizontal coordinate is negative, and its upper\nline expression divided by t is a(x6-v6)<1/3<D. Its lower slack,\nalso divided by t, is\n\n    e-a(rho x6+v6)>9/10-(1/2)[(3/5)(2/3)+2/3]>0.\n\nThus G7=X7 and f7=||X7||^2/2. The physical bridge at index7 is\n1+beta c^2. Consequently\n\n    X8=-(beta c^2)X7=gamma X6=(x8,t v8).\n                                                               (21)\n\nIts horizontal coordinate is greater than3/2. Its projection is\nNOT itself. Since3/2<v8<8/5<D+1 and the bottom endpoint of UV\nhas negative vertical coordinate, the exact projection is\n\n    G8=(1,t v8), Y8=(x8-1,0),\n    f8=<X8,G8>-||G8||^2/2.\n                                                               (22)\n\nIt lies in the relative interior of UV, and the positive horizontal\nresidual proves(15) for the entire triangle, including Lvert and0.\nThe next original step h8=ell now gives\n\n    X9=(x9,t v9).\n                                                               (23)\n\nIn the second H2, G4 has positive vertical coordinate, G5 negative,\nG6 positive, and G7 negative. The gradients vary at EVERY one of its\nthree actual updates. No constant-block compression has been used.\n\n## 7. Permanent core from9 with every physical query retained\n\nBoth endpoints X9 and -c X9 lie in the strict interior of T49.\nFor X9, the right and upper inequalities in(14) follow from(13),\nand its lower slack divided by t is greater than e-2/3>0.\nFor -c X9, the right inequality holds because its horizontal coordinate\nis negative. Its upper expression divided by t satisfies\n\n    c(x9-v9)<(5/2)(3/25+2/3)=59/30<D.\n\nIts lower slack is\n\n    e+c(-rho x9-v9)>e+c[3/5-(3/5)(3/25)]>0.\n\nBy convexity,\n\n    [-c X9,X9] subset interior(T49).\n                                                               (24)\n\nHere [P,Q] denotes the closed Euclidean segment, including its endpoints.\nFor a positive integer j let nu2(j) be the exponent of2 dividing j,\nand for a nonnegative integer i let s2(i) be the number of binary1\ndigits in i, with s2(0)=0. The original word recursion gives\n\n    h_i=1+beta c^(nu2(i+1)-1), 0<=i<n.\n\nIndeed the central position in each word has the stated valuation,\nand adding its power of2 to a right-child index preserves the smaller\nvaluation of that child index. Induction on the recursion proves the\nformula. Counting multiples of2^j gives\n\n    sum_(j=1)^i nu2(j)=sum_(r>=1)floor(i/2^r)=i-s2(i).\n\nThe last equality follows by writing i in binary and summing the\ncontribution 2^r-1 of each binary1 digit. Thus the EXACT prefix product\non the original physical word is\n\n    Pprod_i:=product_(j<i)(1-h_j)\n             =(-1)^i beta^i c^(-s2(i)).\n                                                               (25)\n\nFor EVERY9<=i<=n define\n\n    sigma_i=(-1)^(i-9) beta^(i-9)c^(2-s2(i)),\n    Xi=Gi=sigma_i X9, fi=||Xi||^2/2, Yi=0.\n                                                               (26)\n\nFor odd i>=9, s2(i)>=2, so0<sigma_i<=1. For even i>=10,\ns2(i)>=1, so-c<=sigma_i<0. Hence(24) proves every Xi in(26)\nlies in T49 and is its own actual projection. Equation(25) proves\nXi+1=(1-h_i)Xi for every remaining physical step. This is induction\nfrom the already established X9, not an assumed quadratic suffix.\nAll factors are nonzero, so the gradients continue to change sign at\nevery physical query, including all later original H2 blocks.\n\n## 8. Every remote and optimum interpolation row\n\nAt every finite sample use the single value\n\n    fi=<Xi,Gi>-||Gi||^2/2.\n\nThis agrees with(17),(19),(20),(22),(26) and the core values at6,7.\nPut X_star=G_star=0, f_star=0, Y_star=0. Direct expansion of the\naccepted convention gives, for every distinct pair of indices,\n\n    Q_ij:=fi-fj+<Gi,Xj-Xi>+||Gi-Gj||^2/2\n         =<Gi-Gj,Yj>.\n                                                               (27)\n\nEvery Gi is in the SAME triangle. For j=0,1,2,3 and8, Yj is a\nnonnegative right normal; for j=4 it is a positive upper normal;\nfor j=5 it is a positive lower normal. Equations(14) and(19)--(22)\ntherefore make(27) nonpositive for EVERY possible source i, including\nall remote samples and the optimum. For j=6,7 and j>=9 it is zero.\nFor j=star it is also zero. Since G_star is in the triangle, the\nreverse optimum inequalities are included too. In particular no\nincoming or outgoing terminal row, earlier remote row, or original\nquery has been omitted.\n\nTogether with Sections5--7 this supplies a full static interpolant,\nits true minimum set, all original points, gradients, values and\nnormal conditions. Dimension2 and the paid radius in(16) are separate\nquantities. Orthogonal embedding preserves the same closest distance:\nthe minimizer set in the larger space is the orthogonal complement\nof the original plane.\n\n## 9. Two exact original-row obstructions\n\n### 9.1 Continuing the lower affine face through query6\n\nRetain all actual points, gradients and values through5. Define the\nclosest point to0 on the lower supporting LINE by\n\n    q=(e t/(1+rho^2 t^2)) n_minus,\n    w=G5-q.\n\nThen q is perpendicular to w, and(20) gives\n\n    X5=(1+d5/e)q+w,\n    X6=(d5/e-beta)q-beta w.\n\nThe affine-line continuation would prescribe\n\n    G6_aff=q-beta w,\n    Y6_aff=[d5/e-(1+beta)]q.\n                                                               (28)\n\nBy(7),(10), the scalar in square brackets is negative. Using the\nnatural projection-form value at6, direct expansion(27) gives\n\n    Q_(0,6_aff)\n       =(1+beta-d5/e) e ell(D+1)t^2/(1+rho^2 t^2)>20.\n                                                               (29)\n\nTo see this exact numerator, U=G0 and\n\n    <q,U-q>=-e ell(D+1)t^2/(1+rho^2 t^2).\n\nThis follows from <n_minus,U>-e t=-ell(D+1)t, using\ne+rho=a(D+1). The candidate G6_aff lies on the same supporting\nline as q, so <q,G6_aff>=||q||^2. Substitution into(28) proves\nthe equality in(29). For its quantitative bound, d5/e<10/207<1/20,\n1+beta>19/10, ell>7/5, D+1>18/5, e>9/10, and\n1/(rho^2+delta)>5/2. Their product is greater than\n(37/20)(9/10)(7/5)(18/5)(5/2)=209790/10000>20.\n\nAllowing a free value f6 cannot repair this prescribed gradient while\nthe prior value f5 is retained. In fact\n\n    G5-G6_aff=(1+beta)w,\n    X5-X6=(1+beta)G5,\n    <G5-G6_aff,X5-X6>=||G5-G6_aff||^2.\n\nTherefore Q_(5,6_aff)+Q_(6_aff,5)=0 independently of f6. If both\nrows were nonpositive, both would be equalities and they would uniquely\nforce f6. The projection-form value used in(29) makes both equalities\nhold, since both normals are perpendicular to w. Thus it is the forced\nvalue and the original remote row(0,6) remains positive. This rejects\nonly this same-line continuation with the specified retained jets;\nit does not reject arbitrary new gradients or a new whole transcript.\n\n### 9.2 Declaring a permanent core at query6\n\nRetain the ACTUAL construction through7, including its optimum and\ninitial jet. The physical query X8 is fixed by(21). The false quadratic\ncontinuation would set G8_core=X8. The two optimum rows in accepted\n1252b8823bb5a290 force f8_core=||X8||^2/2. Since Y0=(A-1,0),\n\n    Q_(8_core,0)=(A-1)(x8-1)>(A-1)/2>2.\n                                                               (30)\n\nThus even with free new values the stated core continuation is\nimpossible for ANY smooth convex interpolant retaining these earlier\njets and optimum. The actual projection(22) repairs this row by\nreturning to the old vertical face. Both source and target in(30)\nare original physical queries, not added support points or compressed\nblock endpoints.\n\n## 10. Exact true-distance payoff\n\nSince n=2^m-1 is odd and s2(n)=m, formulas(16),(26) yield the exact\nnormalized payoff of this actual function:\n\n    V49(m,t):=2(F49(Xn)-min F49)/dist(X0,argmin F49)^2\n      =beta^(2(n-9)) c^(4-2m)\n             (x9^2+t^2 v9^2)/(A^2+t^2(D+1)^2)>0.\n                                                               (31)\n\nLet Qunit=Pprod_n^2=beta^(2n)c^(-2m). This is the scalar unit\nquadratic's exact payoff on the SAME word. Using t>=1 and(7),(13),\n\n    (x9^2+t^2 v9^2)/(A^2+t^2(D+1)^2)\n       <[(3/25)^2+(2/3)^2]/(18/5)^2\n        =2581/72900<1/25.\n\nBernoulli's inequality beta^18>=(1-18/m)>=9/10 follows by induction\nfrom (1-v)^r>=1-rv for0<=v<=1. Thus\n\n    V49/Qunit<beta^(-18)c^4/25\n              <(10/9)(5/2)^4/25=125/72<2.\n                                                               (32)\n\nThe bound by2 Qunit has a precise large-aspect limit. Fix any allowed\nm and let t tend to infinity, so delta tends to0 while the enclosing\nword stays fixed. All coefficients in(1)--(2) remain finite and have\nlimits. At4 the projected vertical coefficient tends to A s; at5\nit tends to -a A s because delta E5 tends to0. Hence successive\nvertical coefficients give\n\n    lim_(t->infinity) v6=a beta A s,\n    lim_(t->infinity) v8=beta^4 A s,\n    lim_(t->infinity) v9=-a beta^4 A s\n                       =(D+1)Pprod_9.\n\nThe last identity uses D+1=k s=A s/(a beta^3) and\nPprod_9=-beta^9/c^2. Divide numerator and denominator of the radius\nratio in(31) by t^2. Since x9 and A remain bounded, it follows that\n\n    lim_(t->infinity) V49(m,t)/Qunit(m)=1.                (32a)\n\nThus arbitrarily increasing the aspect does not improve its asymptotic\nscale, even though the same three-face pattern remains present at\nevery finite allowed t. No claim that V49<=Qunit at every finite t\nis needed or asserted.\n\nThe payoff is paid at the closest actual minimizer by(16). For any\npositive smoothness constant L_s, replace F49 by L_s F49 and use\nsteps h_i/L_s. All positions, closest distances and normalized payoffs\nstay the same. The convention is W=2R throughout.\n\n## 11. Complete same-word references and original B_n\n\nPut S=sum H_m and M=1+S. The exact multiplicities of the recursively\ndefined steps give\n\n    M=2^m+(beta/c)(c^m-2^m)/(c-2)\n       =(1-zeta)2^m+zeta c^m,\n    zeta=beta/[c(c-2)] in(0,1).\n                                                               (33)\n\nIndeed summing the unit portions gives2^m-1, and summing the excesses\ngives (beta/c)sum_(j=0)^(m-1)2^(m-1-j)c^j. The geometric sum is\nthe displayed quotient. Since c>lambda>2 and lambda(lambda-2)=1,\none has c(c-2)>1>beta. Consequently M<c^m.\n\nThe COMPLETE reference from accepted b98bb807d514e80d is\n\n    K_n(h)=max{1/(1+2S),\n      max_(0<=j<n) (h_j-1)_+^2/\n         [(1+sum_(i<j)h_i)^2(1+2sum_(i>j)h_i)]}.\n\nDefine Huber=1/(1+2S)=1/(2M-1). The complete F_ray_n of accepted\n1fca3e5dd555cb4a maximizes over ALL feasible chronological checkpoint\nsets, retaining the offsets and accumulated value areas specified\nthere; in particular its zero-cut option is Huber and its one-cut\noptions are all the displayed K terms. Thus Huber<=K_n<=F_ray_n,\nwith no checkpoint choice suppressed.\n\nUsing(32)--(33), beta<1 and c>2,\n\n    V49/Huber=V49(2M-1)<4 c^(-m)<1/10.\n                                                               (34)\n\nThe final strict inequality already follows for m>=6 because2^6>40;\nour stated domain has m>=1000000. This proves the full K/ray comparison.\nIt uses a lower bound on each complete reference, rather than replacing\nthe reference by a chosen checkpoint.\n\nFor completeness, the FULL complex reference in accepted\ndfde2fa326c908bf uses Poly_i(z)=product_(j<i)(1-h_j z), the real-plane\npoints xi_i=Poly_i(z), gradients gamma_i=z Poly_i(z), and the optimum0.\nFor every directed simple cycle C on{star,0,...,n}, it imposes\n\n    (Re(z)-|z|^2) Energy_C>=2|Im(z) Area_C|,\n    Energy_C=sum_(j->i in C)|xi_i-xi_j|^2,\n    Area_C=sum_(j->i in C)Im(conj(xi_j)xi_i).\n\nD_h is the set satisfying ALL these cycles. Gap_h(z) is the maximal\nnormalized terminal objective among interpolants of these specified\npoints and gradients with f(0)=0, using initial point1 and denominator1.\nWe compare with the entire supremum over D_h.\n\nTake the real parameter theta=1/(2M). It is in[0,1], so all cycle\ninequalities hold: their right sides vanish and their left coefficients\nare nonnegative. The explicit convex1-smooth scalar quadratic\nf_theta(x)=theta x^2/2 produces the exact specified real polynomial\npoints and gradients. It can be embedded in the real plane by making\nthe function independent of the second coordinate. Its minimum is0\nand its initial squared distance is1. Therefore the maximality in the\naccepted fact implies\n\n    Gap_h(theta)>=theta Poly_n(theta)^2.\n\nAll factors are positive and\nsum_j theta h_j=S/(2M)<1/2. Induction on the factors, using\n(1-u)(1-v)>=1-u-v for u,v>=0, proves\n\n    Poly_n(theta)>=1-theta S>1/2,\n    sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)>1/(8M)>1/(8c^m).\n                                                               (35)\n\nTogether with(32),\n\n    V49/[sup_(z in D_h)Gap_h(z)]<16 c^(-m)<1/10.\n                                                               (36)\n\nThe final inequality follows for m>=8 because2^8>160. This is a\ncomparison to the FULL unaugmented complex channel, not only its\nuniversal lens. It also dominates V49 after any Huber augmentation.\n\nFinally retain the ORIGINAL all-split recurrences. In the notation of\naccepted b98bb807d514e80d they are Cbench_0=B_0=1 and\n\n    Cbench_n=max_(0<=j<n) Omega(Cbench_j,Cbench_(n-j-1)),\n    B_n=max_(0<=j<n) Gjoin(Cbench_j,B_(n-j-1)),\n    Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n    Gjoin(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2.\n\nCbench is only a local alias for the accepted primitive mass. Using\nits proved bound B_n<=1+4n^(log_2 3)<5*3^m at n=2^m-1, and(32),\n\n    0<B_n V49<10(3/c^2)^m<10(25/48)^m ->0.\n                                                               (37)\n\nThis closes the entire stated large-aspect family uniformly, even if\nt grows arbitrarily fast with m. It is not an upper bound on W_n(h),\nand does not transfer the finite accepted H6 result to another word.\n\n## 12. Exact remaining boundaries\n\nThe first new conclusion is a complete legal three-face construction\nfor every stated(m,t), with changing gradients inside physical H2\nblocks, every ordered original row, and true minimum/radius accounting.\nIt enters the core permanently only AFTER the remote-forced return8.\nIts payoff is quantitatively below every complete reference above.\n\nThe two row obstructions(29)--(30) retain specific earlier jets and\nthe optimum. They close exactly the corresponding proposed next\ngradients, including arbitrary reassigned new values. They do not\nexclude whole-transcript redesign, different extra vertices, conjugate\nheights, a moved initial state, or a changing closest-minimum state.\n\nTogether with max48 as awareness only, the two drafts identify a\nbalanced-aspect failure and the present large-aspect failure. This\njoint draft alone does NOT close the intermediate/small aspect region,\nall triangles, all polytopes, or all changing-gradient laws. This refutes a general implication from multiplier drainage to objective decay. (ii) Fix original m>=128, beta=1-1/m,c=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m),a=beta/c and H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j). Put x0=(0,(2+a)/sqrt2). {"id": "b530822940419137", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T23:34:22.046654+00:00", "author": "max2", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "UNVERIFIED round42 exact boundary: every t>1 on h(t)=(t sqrt(2),t b,1+(sqrt(2)-1)(1+t(sqrt(2)+b))-(1-t),t sqrt(2),t b) is nonprimitive in the full original cone. The Gamma5 parameter is already in this excluded continuation.", "evidence": "# Actual quadratic obstruction to continuing the chosen five-step path\n\nStatus: UNVERIFIED joint counterexample draft. This is a scoped failure of\nthe ONE round42 physical curve, not a no-seed theorem. No fact_submit is\nauthorized or performed. All calculations needed for this obstruction are\nreproduced here; no other unverified draft is a premise.\n\nUse normalized smoothness1. Set\n\n  a=sqrt(2), lambda=1+a, rho=a-1=1/lambda,\n  b=(-a+sqrt(10+8a))/2, p=a+b.\n\nThe number b is positive and satisfies b^2+a b=2(1+a). For every real\nparameter t>1 define the actual nonnegative five-step word\n\n  C=1+t p, d=rho C-(1-t),\n  h(t)=(t a,t b,1+d,t a,t b),\n  s=sum h_i=2C+d-1, A=1+s=2C+d=a+t(lambda p+1).        (1)\n\nHere C is a child capacity, not a benchmark sequence. All local symbols\nmay be read with the suffix d42.\n\nAt t=1 put C_*=1+a+b. Direct expansion gives\n\n  (b-1)C_*=b^2+a b-1-a=lambda,\n  (a-1)(b-1)=1/C_*,\n  d_*=rho C_*, A_*=lambda C_*.\n\nIt follows that\n\n  A_* product_(i=0)^4(h_i(1)-1)\n    =lambda C_* [(a-1)(b-1)]^2 rho C_*=1.              (2)\n\nThe increasing polynomial F(u)=u^2+a u-2(1+a) has F(3-a)=7-5a<0,\nbecause a>7/5. Thus b>3-a>1. Also a>1 and d_*>0. Every step at t=1\nis greater than1. Each physical step increases strictly with t: its\nderivative is a,b,rho p+1,a,b respectively. Capacity also increases.\nConsequently, for every t>1,\n\n  A(t) product_(i=0)^4(h_i(t)-1)>1.                     (3)\n\nNow take the single static scalar function\n\n  f(x)=x^2/2, x_star=0, x_0=1.\n\nIt is convex, differentiable,1-smooth, with unique minimum0 and actual\ninitial distance1. {"id": "eebbada960b7bc3a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-09-30T23:46:28.963493+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max4 growing-axis guardrail: the projection multiplier and Euclidean radius, even together with boundary weight sigma=1, do not determine the next state. Two actual starts for the same geometric ellipsoid and original H_m agree in all three scalars but the first original step puts one in the core and leaves the other exterior. The complete joint draft also gives both full-word payoffs.", "evidence": "Fix m>=128, lambda=1+sqrt(2), beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c, Cax=c^m, D=Cax^2. In R^(m+1) let A=diag(c^(2l):0<=l<=m), E={g:g^T A g<=1}, F(x)=max_(g in E)(<x,g>-||g||^2/2). Completing the square gives grad F=P_E. For x outside E the projection is g=(I+tA)^(-1)x, with the unique positive t solving g^T A g=1: for any z in E, <x-g,z-g>=t(<g,Az>-1)<=0 proves the normal condition. For core x, g=x and t=0. Adding normal inequalities gives firm nonexpansiveness, and comparing the two defining maxima proves F convex, differentiable,1-smooth. Testing a small positive multiple of any nonzero x proves F(x)>0, so its unique minimum is0 at0. Its exact value is F(x)=||g||^2/2+t. Choose X0=(1+1/Cax)e0 and Y0=(1+1/Cax)em. Their gradients are e0 and em/Cax, respectively. Both have multiplier t0=1/Cax, Euclidean radius1+1/Cax, and sigma=sum_l d_l*g_l^2=1. The FIRST prescribed step is1+a. It gives X1=(1/Cax-a)e0 in E and t(X1)=0, whereas Y1=(1-a/Cax)em is exterior with t(Y1)=(Cax-1-a)/D>0. The inequalities use Cax>16 and3/8<a<1/2, following directly from m>=128. Thus an exact state recursion on these three scalars cannot exist for all initial vectors. These are actual first queries of the requested word, not hypothetical internal inputs. For full-word formulas define H0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a c^j,H_j), S=sum H_m and q_j=beta^(2^j-1)c^(-j). Its mass satisfies S+1=alpha c^m+(1-alpha)2^m<Cax, where alpha=a/(c-2) lies in(0,1). Every nonempty prefix product of the positive step excesses is at most a: induct on H_j, using q_j*a*c^j=a*beta^(2^j-1)<=a through each bridge. Hence every prefix product after deleting only the first step is at most1. The X trajectory therefore stays in the core after X1 and has payoff P_X=(q_m/a)^2(a-1/Cax)^2/(1+1/Cax)^2. All Y queries are exterior, since Y_i=(Cax+1-sum_(k<i)h_k)em/Cax and S<Cax-1; their gradients stay em/Cax. Its exact payoff is P_Y=[1+2(Cax-S)]/(Cax+1)^2. Both use the true radius1+1/Cax and the same static objective. This refutes only the scalar-state closure, not an H_m benchmark bound. Complete joint evidence with the all-axis recursion and all ordered Q inequalities is1843cf0b12a35803; no formal gate was authorized.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "An exact all-initial-state update can use only projection multiplier, Euclidean radius, and sigma.", "status": "refuted in joint UNVERIFIED draft", "finding_ids": ["1843cf0b12a35803"], "branch_id": "max4_round48_growing_axis", "original_target_verified": false}, "glossary": {"Cax": "c_m^m, inverse shortest ellipsoid semiaxis.", "sigma": "Sum d_l*g_l^2, equal to1 at both initial boundary projections.", "P_X": "Full original-word true-radius payoff from X0.", "P_Y": "Full original-word true-radius payoff from Y0."}}

No further full-array elaboration, seed, fact_submit, or original-target progress.", "evidence": "# Same-round correction: two further candidates fail before the threshold\n\nUNVERIFIED research note, not a fact.
