This note records those two screens;\nno long multiplier-continuation proof was attempted after either failed.\nAll new arguments are reproduced jointly below, using the original\nidentity in fact04219554e41d9750 and thresholds in4c92d3fc4eb6153e.\n\n## Actual starting certificates and the two proposed physical deformations\n\nBoth candidates start at the ordinary all-ones word of their own length r.\nThat word has a concrete original full certificate: for0<=i<r set\n\n  Lambda_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2,\n  Lambda_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2),\n\nand set every other ordered coefficient to zero. Row sum minus column\nsum is(-2,...,-2,2r). In the original matrix formula the off-diagonal\nentry(i,i+1) is-(i+1)^2; the interior diagonal is i^2+(i+1)^2, the\nfirst diagonal1 and the last diagonal r^2. Thus the full remainder is\n\n  M=sum_(i=0)^(r-1)(i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2 >=0.       (1)\n\nThis proves the start in all original coordinates. It imposes no support\nrestriction on a proposed continuation.\n\nCandidate B uses a terminal unit step to keep the full-curvature product\nzero while transferring mass into separated earlier long steps. Its\nintended coefficient change would release the terminal row and its full\nSchur complement, rather than retain the old primitive-join array:\n\n  hB(t)=(1+t/2,1+29t/20,1+13t/20,1+13t/10,1),\n  0<=t<=1, A_B(t)=6+39t/10.                            (2)\n\nThe proposed endpoint is(3/2,49/20,33/20,23/10,1), capacity99/10.\n\nCandidate C instead uses six strictly nonunit steps for t>0, a small\ninterior excess, unequal neighboring middle steps, and a free terminal\nstep. It would require simultaneous selection of the original full array,\nwithout the terminal-unit Schur specialization:\n\n  hC(t)=(1+11t/20,1+33t/20,1+t/100,\n                              1+19t/20,1+31t/20,1+3t/10),\n  0<=t<=1, A_C(t)=7+501t/100.                          (3)\n\nIts proposed endpoint is(31/20,53/20,101/100,39/20,51/20,13/10),\ncapacity1201/100. Neither endpoint is one of the eight old frozen words.\nThese are proposed physical continuations from(1), NOT assertions that\nall their intermediate words have feasible certificates.\n\nBoth endpoints pass the original unit-quadratic screen\nA product|1-h_i|<=1. For B the product is zero. For C the excesses\nare(11/20,33/20,1/100,19/20,31/20,3/10); their product is below1/25\nand A<13, giving A product<13/25<1.\n\nThey also pass every original scalar cut. At index i write\nK_i=1+sum_(j<i)h_j and D_i=1+sum_(j>i)h_j. The positive slacks\nK_i D_i-A(h_i-1) at the respective endpoints are\n\n  B: (69/20,13/25,297/20,33/100,89/10),\n  C: (7709/2000,99/1000,352399/10000,\n                               18709/1000,61/400,7107/1000).     (4)\n\nFor completeness the cut follows from the projection interval with\nconstant gradient1 before the cut and gradient-(h_i-1)/D_i after it,\ninitial point K_i and terminal point equal to that last gradient.\nThe energy identity below gives V=K_i^2-A^2(h_i-1)^2/D_i^2. Thus a\nnegative slack would be an actual scalar obstruction. Passage of(4)\nis only a necessary test. A stronger actual triangle defeats both words.\n\n## One exact static-function construction for both early screens\n\nConsider a word(H,b,m_1,...,m_k,c,ell), k=1 or2. Put\n\n  B=b-1, J=1+sum m_i, C=sum m_i+c, D=1+ell, K=1+H.\n\nThe local C here is a sum of physical steps, not the benchmark C_n.\nLet p=(1,0), choose a noncollinear q with\n\n  p.q=-u, ||q||^2=N,\n  w=-(B p+C q)/D.                                     (5)\n\nAll B,C,D in the applications are positive. Hence0 lies strictly inside\nthe triangle T=conv{p,q,w}. Define one static function for this whole word\n\n  F(x)=max_(v in T)(<x,v>-||v||^2/2).                  (6)\n\nCompleting the square gives its unique maximizing v=Proj_T(x). Projection\nnormal inequalities imply\n||Proj_T(x)-Proj_T(y)||^2<=<Proj_T(x)-Proj_T(y),x-y>, so projection is\n1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing inequalities for F(x+z) and F(x) bound\nF(x+z)-F(x)-<Proj_T(x),z> between0 and||z||^2. Therefore F is convex,\ndifferentiable and1-smooth, with gradient Proj_T. It has unique minimizer0:\nProj_T(x)=0 implies <x,v><=0 for all v in T, and0 is interior.\n\nStart x_0=Kp. The intended gradients are p,p, then q on every middle\nquery through the second long step, then w,w. The actual positions are\n\n  x_1=p, x_2=-B p,\n  x_j=-B p-(sum of preceding middle steps)q,\n  x_(r-1)=D w, x_r=w.\n\nAt the p queries the projection test reduces to p.(q-p)<=0 and\np.(w-p)<=0. At q the normal is -B p-z q, where1<=z<=J. Its two required\ndot products, tested at z=1,J because they are affine in z, are\n\n  P_z=-B(1+u)+z(u+N),\n  N_z=B^2-B(C+D+z)u+z(C+D)N.                          (7)\n\nThe second actual dot product is N_z/D. At the two w queries it suffices\nthat w.p<=||w||^2 and w.q<=||w||^2; their normals are ell w and0.\nThese tests check every triangle vertex at every actual query. They\ntherefore imply ALL ordered interpolation inequalities, including remote\nand optimum rows: with f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2,\n\n  Q_ij=<g_i-g_j,x_j-g_j><=0.                          (8)\n\nDirect GD telescoping gives the original primitive energy\n\n  V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_r+s g_r||^2=K^2-A^2||w||^2.      (9)\n\nIndeed substituting f_i into the definition of V_h leaves\nsum_i(2h_i<x_i,g_i>-h_i^2||g_i||^2)-2s<x_r,g_r>-s^2||g_r||^2,\nand the sum telescopes to ||x_0||^2-||x_r||^2.\nA negative value of(9) excludes every original full nonnegative array\nand arbitrary full PSD remainder, by fact04219554e41d9750's identity.\n\n## Candidate B: a whole terminal-unit segment is excluded below Gamma5\n\nUse99/100<=t<=1 and q=(-2/5,sqrt(2)/10), so u=2/5,N=9/50. Then\n\n  B=29t/20, J=2+13t/20, C=2+39t/20, D=2, K=2+t/2,\n  w=((80-67t)/200,-sqrt(2)(40+39t)/400).               (10)\n\nThe four quantities in(7), in order P_1,P_J,N_1,N_J, are\n\n  29/50-203t/100,\n  29/25-1653t/1000,\n  18/25-2549t/1000+1943t^2/2000,\n  36/25-231t/100+16453t^2/20000.                       (11)\n\nAll are negative on the stated interval. For the last two, use t>=99/100\nin the negative term and t^2<=1 in the positive term to bound them by\n-83201/100000 and-97/4000 respectively. The first two are already\nnegative at99/100 and decrease.\n\nThe remaining normals are also strict: w.p<7/100, while\n||w||^2>1521/20000>7/100, using40+39t>78. Furthermore\nw.q=(-360+229t)/2000<0. Finally p.q<1 and p.w<1.\nThus(6) is an actual static function for the entire physical trajectory.\n\nSince K<=5/2, A_B(t)>49/5 and ||w||^2>1521/20000, equation(9) gives\n\n  V_h<25/4-(49/5)^2(1521/20000)\n      =-526921/500000<-1.                              (12)\n\nThis excludes every t in[99/100,1], not only the endpoint. The lower\nend has capacity9861/1000, strictly below Gamma5, since\n\n  Gamma5_c107-9861/1000=29641/806000>0.\n\nBecause A_B(1)=99/10>Gamma5, every above-Gamma5 point of the proposed\ninterval(2) is already in this excluded final segment. No coefficient\ncontinuation could reach its proposed sufficient-capacity endpoint.\n\n## Candidate C: a six-step segment is excluded below Gamma6\n\nUse999/1000<=t<=1 and q=(-3/10,1/10), so u=3/10,N=1/10. Then\n\n  B=33t/20, J=3+24t/25, C=3+251t/100,\n  D=2+3t/10, K=2+11t/20,\n  w=((900-897t)/1000,-(300+251t)/1000)/D.              (13)\n\nThe four quantities in(7) are now\n\n  2/5-429t/200,\n  6/5-1761t/1000,\n  1/2-2689t/1000+26631t^2/20000,\n  3/2-2637t/1000+112611t^2/100000.                     (14)\n\nThe last two are at most-854761/1000000 and-8253/1000000 using\nt>=999/1000 and t^2<=1. The first two are negative and decreasing.\nAll intermediate q queries are covered by the affine-in-z check in(7).\n\nFor the w normals,0<w.p<1/500. The magnitude of its second coordinate\nexceeds11/46, since its numerator exceeds11/20 and D<=23/10. Thus\n||w||^2>121/2116>1/18>1/500. Also w.q<0, because w has positive first\ncoordinate and negative second coordinate. The p normals are strict.\nThis completes all original interpolation checks via(6)--(8).\n\nHere K^2<7, A_C(t)>12 and ||w||^2>1/18, so\n\n  V_h<7-12^2/18=-1.                                   (15)\n\nThe lower segment capacity is1200499/100000. It is below the accepted\nthreshold because\n\n  Gamma6_c107-2401/200=112799/32240000>0,\n  2401/200-1200499/100000=1/100000>0.\n\nSince A_C(1)=1201/100>Gamma6, every above-Gamma6 point of(3) lies in\nthe excluded segment[999/1000,1]. At t=1 the witness is especially\nsimple: w=(3,-551)/2300, x_0=(51/20,0).\n\n## Verdict under the actually consumed correction\n\nBoth new candidates have explicit actual feasible starts and proposed\nsufficient-capacity endpoints that survive the cheaper original screens.\nBoth fail a complete actual-function screen on a parameter interval\nbeginning BELOW their respective thresholds. Full multiplier integration\nwas therefore not pursued for either candidate. No interior feasibility\nclaim, new fact, or original-target progress is asserted.\n\nThe concrete difficulty exposed here is the same retained two-transition\ngeometry: redistributing mass around an exact terminal unit or a small\ninterior excess leaves the p,p,q,...,q,w,w projection trajectory legal,\nwith its terminal norm too large. {"id": "b2d39a364362f68a", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T00:34:21.391883+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max4 round49 scoped counterexample (UNVERIFIED): equal cumulative effective clocks do not determine a nonlinear Gibbs GD endpoint. This refutes a clock-sum substitution, not the assigned H_m trajectory or its target inequality.", "evidence": "For the assigned tree feature function F=log(2^-m sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)), restrict to a point with only the root coordinate nonzero and put y=sqrt(w_0)x_root. Equal numbers of leaves have root signs + and -, all descendant mean coordinates cancel, and F=log cosh y. Thus this slice is invariant and a physical step h updates y to y-(h w_0/Ltree)tanh y. Let t0=log2, a_test=5t0/3 and b_test=a_test/2. Starting at y=t0, apply physical steps a_test Ltree/w_0 and b_test Ltree/w_0. Since tanh(t0)=3/5, the order (a_test,b_test) reaches zero on its first step and stays zero. Reversing the same two steps first gives t0/2; since tanh(t0/2)=1/3, its final field is t0/2-5t0/9=-t0/18. The total effective clock is a_test+b_test in both cases, but terminal values are 0 and log cosh(t0/18)>0. Assumptions satisfied: one fixed static function, exact finite positive physical steps, all actual iterates retained, identical initial state and total clock. Failed conclusion: dependence only on cumulative clock. These test states and test words differ from the prescribed all-spine start and H_m; they do not constitute a counterexample to that target. Complete joint context and separate actual-word occupation relaxation are in finding1ad14d90628c1312.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["1ad14d90628c1312"]}, "glossary": {"a_test": "First selected normalized scalar clock 5log(2)/3 in the scoped order counterexample.", "b_test": "Second normalized clock a_test/2.", "t0": "Initial scalar test field log(2).", "w_0": "Root feature weight in the assigned tree.", "Ltree": "Sharp smoothness (1+w_0)/2 of that tree."}}
{"id": "e5289b075ce208a2", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T01:08:28.316544+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max52 UNVERIFIED inverse-map guard: the original largest H_m step on the root-only slice of the SAME static Gibbs function has at least three preimages of0 and expands sufficiently small nonzero root states. Universal injectivity and inverse norm contractivity are false; existence of a cheap selected whole-word branch is not refuted.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED component of complete joint d23006aab2b6e9be. Definitions, fixed-function legality, and root-slice invariance are reproduced jointly below; no draft is an accepted premise.\n\n## 2. Original words and original all-split benchmark\n\nFix an integer m>=128 for the rest of the document. Put\n\n lambda=1+sqrt2, beta=1-1/m, c=lambda(1+1/m), a=beta/c,\n H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+a*c^j,H_j), 0<=j<m,\n N=2^m, n=N-1, h=H_m, S=sum_(i=0)^(n-1) h_i.\n\nAll children retain these SAME enclosing-m parameters. Solving the\nrecursion for the sum, or counting the copies of each bridge, gives\n\n 1+S=N+(beta/c)(c^m-N)/(c-2)\n     =(1-zeta)N+zeta*c^m,\n zeta=beta/[c(c-2)].                                      (2.1)\n\nThe largest physical step is hmax=1+beta*c^(m-2). Since\nlambda(lambda-2)=1, one has 0<zeta<1 and S<c^m.\n\nRetain the ORIGINAL recurrences, for integers k in the indicated ranges:\n\n C_0=B_0=1,\n C_t=max_(0<=k<t) Omega(C_k,C_(t-k-1)),\n B_t=max_(0<=k<t) G(C_k,B_(t-k-1)),\n Omega(u,v)=(u+v+sqrt((u+v)^2+4uv))/2,\n G(u,v)=2u+v/2+sqrt(v^2+8uv)/2,\n U_t^ZJ=1/B_t.                                            (2.2)\n\nThere is no surrogate recurrence or new benchmark evaluation here.\nAccepted fact04219554e41d9750 gives, with the SAME B_t,\n\n B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m -> Z_1,\n B_(2^m-1)<=Z_1*lambda^m,\n 0<Z_1<=(sqrt(B_1)+3/100)^2=41209/10000<33/8.              (2.3)\n\nIndeed B_1=G(1,1)=4. The full statement and its Section7 ray proof were\nread. This accepted fact, not either unverified Gibbs draft, supplies\nthe benchmark limit and enclosure used below.\n\nSeveral elementary bounds will be useful. Write epsilon=1/m. Expanding\nc(c-2) gives\n\n c(c-2)=1+2lambda(lambda-1)epsilon+lambda^2 epsilon^2.\n\nFor m>=128, lambda<5/2 implies the last two terms total less than\n8epsilon. Hence\n\n zeta>(1-epsilon)/(1+8epsilon)>=1-9epsilon>=119/128.\n\nAlso c<5/2: for example lambda<29/12 and\n(29/12)(129/128)<5/2. Since c^m>128, (2.1) now gives\n\n             (9/10)c^m<S<c^m.                           (2.4)\n\nFor completeness, the lower bound follows from\nS/c^m>119/128-1/128=59/64>9/10.\n\nThe binomial expansion, already for m>=16, gives\n\n (1+1/m)^m >= 2+(1-1/m)/2\n                   +(1-1/m)(1-2/m)/6\n             >=2+15/32+35/256>13/5.\n\nBecause lambda^m>32, (2.1)-(2.3) imply the convenient finite screen\n\n S/lambda^m>(119/128)(13/5)-1/32=1527/640,\n B_n/(2S)<1320/1527<7/8.                                  (2.5)\n\nFinally zeta->1, (2/c)^m->0, and\nm log(1+1/m)->1 show\n\n S/c^m->1, c^m/lambda^m->exp(1),\n B_n/(2S)->Z_1/(2 exp(1))<99/128<1.                       (2.6)\n\nFor the displayed rational gap, use Z_1<33/8 and exp(1)>8/3.\nThe logarithmic limit follows, for example, by integrating\n1-u<=1/(1+u)<=1 between0 and1/m.\n\n## 3. The fixed function, sharp smoothness, and true minimum\n\nSet r_tree=2/c and\n\n Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) r_tree^j,\n w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom.\n\nFor every binary word v of length j<m use an orthonormal coordinate e_v.\nFor a leaf s=(s_1,...,s_m) define\n\n a_s=sum_(j=0)^(m-1) sqrt(w_j)(-1)^(s_(j+1))*e_(s_1...s_j),\n F_m(x)=log[N^(-1)sum_s exp(<a_s,x>)].                      (3.1)\n\nThis is exactly the fixed function in TASK.md. Each feature has norm1.\nThe feature mean is zero. Distinct coordinate rows of the feature\nmatrix are orthogonal: their supports are disjoint, or the descendant\nrow sums to zero inside a region on which the ancestor row is constant.\nEach row has positive squared norm. The matrix therefore has rank N-1.\n\nJensen gives F_m(x)>=0, with equality only when all leaf logits are\nequal. Their sum is zero; rank then implies x=0. Thus the WHOLE\nminimizer set is {0} and the minimum is0. In particular every radius\nbelow is the actual Euclidean distance to the unique minimizer.\n\nFor finite x define p_s(x)=exp(<a_s,x>)/sum_t exp(<a_t,x>) and\ng(x)=sum_s p_s(x)a_s. Differentiating the finite sum gives\n\n grad F_m=g, Hess F_m=sum_s p_s a_s a_s^T-g g^T.            (3.2)\n\nThe Hessian is a covariance, hence positive semidefinite. For a scalar\nrandom variable T in [b,d], averaging (T-b)(d-T)>=0 yields\nVar(T)<=(d-E T)(E T-b)<=(d-b)^2/4. Applying this in every unit direction\nbounds the covariance by one quarter of the squared feature diameter.\n\nIf two leaves first separate at depth j, their squared distance is\n\n                 4w_j+2sum_(ell>j)w_ell.\n\nThe quarter-distances strictly decrease with j, because their successive\ndifferences are w_j-w_(j+1)/2>0. Consequently\n\n              Ltree=(1+w_0)/2=(Zgeom+1)/(2Zgeom)            (3.3)\n\nis a global gradient Lipschitz constant. It is sharp. The logit map\nis onto the zero-mean subspace, by the rank calculation. Prescribe equal\nlogits on two leaves separating at the root and logits lower by T on\nthe others, and subtract the common mean. These are realized by a finite\nx. As T tends to infinity the covariance tends to that of the equal\ntwo-point distribution, whose variance in the feature-difference\ndirection is Ltree. No new fitting problem is hidden in this argument.\n\nThe full physical iteration throughout is\n\n             x_(i+1)=x_i-(h_i/Ltree)g(x_i).                (3.4)\n\nFor a requested smoothness L>0 take the project objective\nf=(L/Ltree)F_m. It is L-smooth and (3.4) uses exactly steps h_i/L.\nThe actual normalized score is\n\n                    V=2F_m(x_n)/(Ltree||x_0||^2).         (3.5)\n\nLegality includes every original interpolation row. To reproduce this\nwithout importing a draft, fix x and set k(u)=F_m(u)-<g(x),u>.\nConvexity makes x a minimizer of k. Integrating its Lipschitz gradient\nalong a step of length1/Ltree gives\nk(y-grad k(y)/Ltree)<=k(y)-||grad k(y)||^2/(2Ltree).\nComparison with k(x) yields\n\n F_m(y)>=F_m(x)+<g(x),y-x>\n                         +||g(y)-g(x)||^2/(2Ltree).\n\nAfter division by Ltree this is every directed normalized original\nQ_ij<=0, including both terminal orientations and all optimum rows.\nThere is no independently prescribed jet or label transition.\n\nWe will use\n\n 9/2<Zgeom<6,  1/2<Ltree<1,\n 11/4<Ltree*Zgeom<7/2,\n 4/7<kappa_m:=c/(Ltree*Zgeom)<10/11.                       (3.6)\n\nFor the upper bound, Zgeom<c/(c-2)<=lambda/(lambda-2)\n=lambda^2<6. For the lower bound, c<5/2 gives r_tree>4/5;\nthe first11 terms have sum greater than 5(1-(4/5)^11)>9/2. The last strict inequality\nis the exact integer inequality10*4^11<5^11. The other assertions\nfollow from (3.3) and2<c<5/2. In particular\n\n Zgeom->lambda^2, Ltree*Zgeom->2+sqrt2,\n                       kappa_m->1/sqrt2.                 (3.7)\n\n## 4. Mechanism1: actual symmetric inverse branches with paid norm\n\nFor a fixed depth d in {0,...,m-1} define the full vector X_d(y) by\n\n X_d(y)_v=y/sqrt(w_d) if |v|=d, and0 otherwise.\n\nExactly half the leaves have logit+y and half have logit-y, so\nF_m(X_d(y))=log cosh y. Every depth-d subtree has the same partition\nsum. Thus gradients at ancestors vanish. Below depth d, each relevant\npair of halves has equal logits and gradients vanish. At a node of\ndepth d its Gibbs subtree mass is2^(-d), and its left-minus-right\nconditional mass is tanh y. Therefore\n\n g(X_d(y))_v=2^(-d)sqrt(w_d)tanh y for |v|=d,\n g(X_d(y))_v=0 at other depths.                            (4.1)\n\nThis proves invariance at EVERY physical query for any new starting y.\nIt proves the norm identity as well:\n\n ||X_d(y)||^2=2^d y^2/w_d=Zgeom*c^d*y^2.                  (4.2)\n\nDefine q_d=1/(Ltree*Zgeom*c^d) and alpha_i=q_d h_i. The exact\nscalar physical recurrence and its original score are\n\n y_(i+1)=y_i-alpha_i tanh y_i,\n                 V=2q_d log cosh(y_n)/y_0^2.             (4.3)\n\nFor every d>=m-3, all alpha_i<1. Indeed\n\n alpha_i<=hmax/(Ltree*Zgeom*c^(m-3))\n          =(c^(3-m)+beta*c)/(Ltree*Zgeom)<1,               (4.4)\n\nbecause the numerator is less than1/4+5/2=11/4 and the denominator\nexceeds11/4 by (3.6).\n\nHere is a concrete backward construction, choosing d=m-1 and y_n=1.\nFor i=n-1,...,0 let y_i be the unique positive solution of\n\n                     y_i-alpha_i tanh y_i=y_(i+1).        (4.5)\n\nExistence and the branch are proved, not assumed: the function\ns-alpha_i tanh s has derivative at least1-alpha_i>0, maps the real\nline onto itself because its difference from s is bounded by alpha_i,\nand is zero at0. At s=y_(i+1) its value is smaller than y_(i+1),\nwhile at s=y_(i+1)+alpha_i it is larger. Thus\n\n             y_(i+1)<y_i<y_(i+1)+alpha_i.\n\nThis also makes (4.5) an explicit inverse-branch specification using a\nunique root in a displayed bounded interval. Define x_0=X_(m-1)(y_0).\nBy (4.1), its actual forward trajectory is x_i=X_(m-1)(y_i), and\n\n x_n=X_(m-1)(1), F_m(x_n)=log cosh1,\n 1<y_0<1+q_(m-1)S,\n R_init^2=Zgeom*c^(m-1)*y_0^2\n          <Zgeom*c^(m-1)*(1+q_(m-1)S)^2.                  (4.6)\n\nThis is a new initial point and its own true norm. The old all-left\nlog2 radius is not used. Every scalar state is in the SAME F_m.\n\nLet qdeep=q_(m-1). The exact score and explicit positive bound are\n\n V_sym=2qdeep log cosh1/y_0^2\n        >2qdeep log cosh1/(1+qdeep S)^2.                  (4.7)\n\nIt is useful to screen this whole stratum before further work. For\nANY nonzero initial y_0 at ANY d>=m-3, oddness allows y_0>0.\nEquation (4.4) implies 0<y_n<=...<=y_0. With t=y_n and T=q_d S,\n\n       y_0=t+sum_i alpha_i tanh y_i>=t+T tanh t.\n\nFor t>0, log cosh t<t tanh t, since their difference has derivative\nt sech^2 t>0 and is0 at0. Also (t+T tanh t)^2>=4T t tanh t.\nCombining these inequalities with (4.3) proves the ACTUAL upper bound\n\n              V<1/(2S), hence B_n V<7/8.                 (4.8)\n\nThis is an obstruction for these specified invariant strata and all\ntheir initial states, not only for a selected lower-bound certificate.\nIt says nothing about depths d<=m-4 with overstepping, simultaneous\nfields at different depths, or general starts.\n\nThe constructed branch does have the correct exponential order:\nusing qdeep*c^m=kappa_m, equations (2.6),(3.7),(4.7)-(4.8) give\n\n liminf B_n V_sym >= (Z_1/exp(1))\n                 *[sqrt2 log cosh1/(1+1/sqrt2)^2]>0,\n limsup B_n V_sym <=Z_1/(2 exp(1))<1.                     (4.9)\n\nNo limit of y_0 is asserted or needed. The early screen (4.8) stops\nthis subfamily without pursuing a sharper scalar optimum.\n\n## 8. Counterexample checks for the remaining inverse problem\n\nNeither injectivity nor norm contractivity of a long step is available,\neven on a simple slice of this SAME function. At root-only points\nx=X_0(y), the exact map is\n\n           y -> map_root(y)=y-alpha_root tanh y,\n              alpha_root=hmax/(Ltree Zgeom).\n\nHere hmax>9 and Ltree Zgeom<7/2 by (3.6), so alpha_root>2.\nSince map_root(y)/y tends to1-alpha_root<-1 as y tends to0,\nsmall nonzero actual root states satisfy |map_root(y)|>|y|.\n\nAlso map_root is odd, is negative immediately to the right of0, and\nmap_root(alpha_root)=alpha_root(1-tanh alpha_root)>0. The intermediate\nvalue theorem gives a positive gamma<alpha_root with map_root(gamma)=0.\nThus 0, gamma, and -gamma are three distinct actual preimages of0.\nAn inverse choice can have positive radius while its target is0.\nThis refutes universal uniqueness and universal inverse norm\ncontractivity. It is not a counterexample to existence of a better\nselected branch, and terminal0 is not used as a payoff witness.\n\nTwo direct attempts at the missing useful norm estimate are now\ndistinguished. The universal bounded-gradient price (5.2) gives the\ncertificate obstruction (5.3). Selecting a fully coherent exposed-leaf\nbranch avoids that logical gap, but (6.9)-(7.5) show that its ACTUAL\nprice is also too high. The three-preimage example prevents silently\nextending either conclusion to all branches.\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"finding_ids": ["d23006aab2b6e9be"], "plan_id": "8fab4ac8728aa9a6", "target_claim": "Long actual GD steps are injective or admit norm-contractive arbitrary inverse choices.", "status": "refuted", "impact": "Universal claims only. No nonexistence of a useful chosen preimage is inferred."}, "glossary": {"H_m": "Original enclosing-m diagnostic word H_0=(), H_(j+1)=(H_j,1+(beta/c)c^j,H_j).", "F_m": "Fixed finite weighted binary-tree log-sum-exp function (3.1); minimum0 at0.", "Zgeom": "Sum of (2/c)^j over depths0 through m-1.", "w_j": "Depth-j feature energy (2/c)^j/Zgeom.", "Ltree": "Sharp global smoothness constant (1+1/Zgeom)/2.", "X_d(y)": "Full vector with coordinate y/sqrt(w_d) at every node of depth d and zero at other depths.", "q_d": "Normalized scalar stiffness1/(Ltree Zgeom c^d).", "V_sym": "Actual normalized payoff of the backward depth-(m-1) branch ending at field1.", "V_leaf": "Actual normalized payoff of the full-path leaf branch constructed in Sections6-7.", "Econc": "Explicit inactive-mass bound (N-1)exp(2-t delta_min) for the new full backward branch.", "delta_min": "Minimum leaf feature gap2w_(m-1) relative to the selected leaf.", "Rleaf": "Exact finite terminal inactive exponential sum in (7.1).", "qfix": "Contraction factor4A Econc for the simultaneous backward path map, with local A=S/Ltree.", "eps_path": "Full backward-path error bound2A Econc.", "Gamma_m": "Composition of all actual normalized GD maps for the original H_m.", "Rmin_m": "Minimum original Euclidean norm over the full preimage of a nonzero terminal point under Gamma_m.", "map_root": "Actual root-only map y-alpha_root tanh y at the original largest step.", "B_n": "Original all-split benchmark denominator, never a surrogate recurrence.", "Z_1": "Accepted positive limit B_(2^m-1)/lambda^m from fact04219554e41d9750."}}
{"id": "b53b38b27498e8a0", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T01:43:31.029919+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "Refuted shortcut, NOT refuted physical inflation: canonical block virtual jets need not satisfy smooth-convex interpolation, even when h=q=[sqrt2] have full M=0 certificates and every actual child energy is zero.", "evidence": "Joint draft4f623d6a2c835679 Sections1--4 supplies all definitions; the explicit calculation is reproduced here. Let a=sqrt(2), C=1+a, d=a-1=1/C. Both input one-step words h=q=(a) have Lambda_01=2, Lambda_10=2C, diagonal0, flow(-2a,2a), and full M=0: with T=[[1,0],[a,1]], D=diag(a(2-a),-a(a+2)), the first diagonal of D+sym(LT) is a(2-a)+2(1-a)=2-a^2=0, the second is -a(a+2)+2C=0, and twice the off-diagonal is -2+2C(a-1)=0. These are original certificates under accepted04219554e41d9750 Section10.\n\nThe actual inflated word is H=(a,2,a). On the single static f(x)=x^2/2 with unique minimizer0 and x0=-C, the full actual run is (-C,1,-1,d), with g_t=x_t and f_t=x_t^2/2. The initial distance is C. For block i let y_i,e_i be its endpoint and gradient, P_i its actual displacement, dinf_i=P_i-(C-1)e_i, Dinf_0=0,Dinf_1=dinf_1. The canonical virtual jets are X_i=[y_i+(C-1)e_i+Dinf_i]/C and F_i=f(y_i)/C+(C-1)e_i^2/(2C), with gradient e_i. Here P_1=-a, dinf_1=-a-a d=-2, so the virtual jets are (X0,F0,e0)=(1,1/2,1) and (X1,F1,e1)=(-d,d^2/2,d). Their positions obey X1=X0-a e0. However Qhat_ij=F_i-F_j+e_i(X_j-X_i)+(e_i-e_j)^2/2 satisfies Qhat_01=1-d-a=-2d<0 and Qhat_10=d(d+a-1)=2d^2>0. Thus no convex1-smooth function interpolates these virtual jets.\n\nEach actual child energy is zero, since on this quadratic V_q=x_start^2-C^2*x_end^2 and Cd=1; every actual ordered Q is also zero. Consequently the positive Qhat_10 cannot be certified individually by nonnegative actual interpolation multipliers and payments from child energies, even with a subtracted PSD term: such a proposed upper representation evaluates to at most zero on this run. The template-weighted virtual expression does cancel: 2Qhat_01+2C Qhat_10=0. A viable generic argument therefore needs collective template-dependent payment, not each virtual inequality independently.\n\nThis is explicitly a NONREFUTING example for the physical lift: H=(a,2,a) is the accepted primitive binary join of q with itself, since surplus1 solves 1(2C+1)=C^2. It has a full certificate by accepted04219554e41d9750 Section11, and its actual V_H is zero on this run. No arbitrary-template impossibility or seed capacity ceiling follows.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"target_claim": "Every full primitive child acts as a smooth-convex virtual oracle for an arbitrary template, possibly with separate child-energy payments.", "status": "refuted", "assumptions_satisfied": ["Both inputs satisfy every original ordered sign and exact flow with full M=0.", "Same single static convex1-smooth function on one physical GD run.", "All actual child energies vanish."], "impact": "Invalidates only the virtual-oracle and pairwise payment proof premise; physical inflation remains unresolved.", "joint_finding": "4f623d6a2c835679", "branch_id": "max4_arbitrary_template_inflation", "fact_ids": ["04219554e41d9750"], "new_fact_submit_calls": 0}, "glossary": {"a": "sqrt(2) in this example.", "C": "1+sqrt(2), capacity of each one-step input.", "d": "sqrt(2)-1=1/C.", "dinf_i": "Displacement defect P_i-(C-1)e_i of physical block i.", "Dinf_i": "Cumulative block defect with Dinf_0=0.", "Qhat_ij": "Smooth-convex residual of the explicitly defined virtual jets."}}

Projection function, smoothness, minimum, and all original rows\n\nLet K be any nonempty compact convex subset of a Euclidean space with\n0 in K. Define the SAME finite function at every query by\n\n F_K(x)=max_(g in K){<x,g>-||g||^2/2}.\n\nCompleting the square shows that its unique maximizing vector is the\nEuclidean projection g=P_K(x). Existence follows from compactness and\nuniqueness from strict convexity of squared distance. The exact normal\ncondition is\n\n <x-g,v-g><=0 for every v in K.                     (2.1)\n\nNecessity follows by differentiating squared distance on the segment\nfrom g to v. Sufficiency follows by expanding ||x-v||^2-||x-g||^2.\nAdding (2.1) for two inputs proves\n<x-x',g-g'>=||g-g'||^2, hence ||g-g'||<=||x-x'||.\nThe maximizing formulas at x and x+d give\n\n 0<=F_K(x+d)-F_K(x)-<g,d><=<P_K(x+d)-g,d><=||d||^2.\n\nTherefore F_K is finite, convex, differentiable, and grad F_K=P_K is\nglobally1-Lipschitz. Also\n\n 2F_K(x)=||x||^2-||x-P_K(x)||^2.                    (2.2)\n\nIts minimum is0, and its WHOLE minimizer set is\n\n K^polar={x:<x,v><=0 for all v in K}.                (2.3)\n\nOne direction follows directly from the maximizing formula. In the other\ndirection, if <x,v>>0 for some v in K, a sufficiently small positive\nmultiple epsilon*v is in K and has positive objective. This proves\nthat x is not a minimizer. It also proves (2.3) without identifying the\nminimum with an arbitrarily selected point.\n\nWrite y_i=x_i-g_i, f_i=F_K(x_i), with g_i=P_K(x_i). The original\ninterpolation convention in accepted1252b8823bb5a290 is\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2.\n\nSubstitution of f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2 gives the exact identity\n\n Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j><=0.                            (2.4)\n\nThe inequality is (2.1). It holds for ALL directed pairs, however\ndistant, and with any actual minimizer sample g_*=0,f_*=0. No sampled\nvalues, remote rows, or terminal rows are fitted separately.\n\nBoth bodies below have nonempty Euclidean interior in their stated\nambient space. The explicit growing tree and exact symmetry reduction\n\nSet\n\n r_tree=2/c, Zgeom=sum_(j=0)^(m-1)r_tree^j,\n w_j=r_tree^j/Zgeom, 0<=j<m.                        (3.1)\n\nUse one orthonormal coordinate e_v for every binary word v of length\nless than m. Each ||a_s||=1 and sum_s a_s=0.\nDistinct coordinate rows of the feature matrix are orthogonal: disjoint\nsupports give zero, and every proper descendant row cancels within an\nancestor's constant-sign region. Every row has positive squared norm.\nThus the matrix has rank N-1, its kernel is exactly the constant leaf\nvector, and the N features are affinely independent. In particular0 is\nin the interior of their simplex. If two leaves first diverge at depth k,\n\n <a_s,a_t>=sum_(j<k)w_j-w_k.                       (3.3)\n\nLet s_left=0^m, write a_left=a_(s_left), and let e_root=e_empty be the\nroot coordinate. The selected initial points have coordinates only on its\nspine, and possibly one common carrier. The group of tree automorphisms\nwithin every off-spine subtree acts orthogonally, fixes these points,\nand permutes the leaf features. Projection onto either selected body\ncommutes with this group by uniqueness. Every non-spine coordinate of a\nfixed vector is zero: swapping the children of its node reverses that\ncoordinate. Averaging any convex representation over the\ngroup shows that the centered simplex's fixed section is EXACTLY\nconv{b_0,...,b_m}. With a carrier, its fixed section is the convex hull\nof0 and the corresponding carrier plus each b_k.\n\nHere is an explicit constructive active-set rule, valid for every state\nin this section, including every future state below. For a finite listed\nsimplex with vertices v_k, consider a nonempty subset I of its vertices.\nChoose k0 in I, form the edge columns E_I=(v_k-v_k0)_(k in I\\{k0}),\nand set\n\n t_I=(E_I^T E_I)^(-1)E_I^T(x-v_k0),\n g_I=v_k0+E_I t_I. The negative tail then makes all further normal values\nstrictly decrease, with the same last-leaf check. This proves(6.5).\n\nThe common active normal values also give the exact actual conjugate\nexcesses cex_1=<y_1,g_1>=w_0 sigma_3>0 and\ncex_2=<y_2,g_2>=w_0 sigma_4>0. The residuals are\nnonzero at both queries, including their retained deep components.\n\nThe third physical update in the first H2 is therefore exactly\n\n z_(3,j)=z_(2,j)-(1+a)d_(2,j).                       (6.6)\n\nFor every j>=4 it is STILL -a. Equations(6.3)-(6.6) are actual\nprojections and updates; they do not prescribe a rank-one law. In\nparticular the face changes between queries1 and2, and the full deep\nnoncore state is retained. At query3 and every subsequent query the\nall-support rule(3.5),(3.6),(5.3) applies. Two direct continuation attempts and their limits\n\nThe first attempt was to propagate a fixed exposed face, or treat a\nvisit to the quadratic core as permanent. No remote interpolation defect occurs: (2.4)\nholds, and the true projection supplies the required repair automatically.\n\nThe second attempt was to simplify the full simplex projection by\nthresholding its unconstrained leaf masses with the ordinary Euclidean\nprobability-simplex formula. (8)\n\nCompleting the square identifies its unique maximizer as the Euclidean\nprojection P_K(x). A proposed g in K is that projection exactly when\n<x-g,z-g><=0 for all z in K. Adding this inequality at two queries\nshows ||P_K(x)-P_K(y)||^2<=<P_K(x)-P_K(y),x-y>, hence the projection\nis1-Lipschitz. The two maximizing inequalities also give\n\n 0<=F_K(x+z)-F_K(x)-<P_K(x),z>\n       <=<P_K(x+z)-P_K(x),z><=||z||^2.\n\nIt follows that F_K is convex, differentiable and1-smooth, with gradient\nP_K. If0 is interior to K, it is the unique minimizer: F_K(0)=0, and\nfor x!=0 a sufficiently small positive multiple of x belongs to K and\nhas positive objective in(8).\n\nAt every actual query define y_i=x_i-g_i and\nf_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2. Direct expansion gives the complete residual\nformula, in the ORIGINAL orientation,\n\n Q_ij=<y_j,g_i-g_j>,  including i or j equal to star.    (9)\n\nChecking the normal against every vertex of K therefore checks every\ndirected finite, remote, terminal and optimum comparison. In particular\nQ_(i,star)=0; the other optimum direction is also included because0 is in K.\nThe norm telescope along actual GD is\n\n ||x_i||^2-||x_(i+1)||^2\n       =2h_i f_i+h_i(1-h_i)||g_i||^2.\n\nUsing the actual terminal value gives\n\n V_h=||x_0||^2-||x_7+s g_7||^2.                        (10)\n\nIn particular, if x_7=g_7 the second square is A^2||g_7||^2.\n\nFor reference, the original global-cut screen at index j is\n\n A(h_j-1)_+ <= P_j^+ D_j^+,\n P_j^+=1+sum_(i<j)h_i, D_j^+=1+sum_(i>j)h_i.           (11)\n\nHere these superscript-plus quantities include1, to distinguish them from\nunshifted prefix/suffix sums in other project findings. To derive(11) when\nh_j>1, set eta=(h_j-1)/D_j^+ and K=[-eta,1]. Start at x_0=P_j^+.\nAll queries through j have gradient1; query j equals1. Its step lands at\n-D_j^+ eta. Every suffix gradient is-eta, and the terminal equals-eta.\nThis uses one static function(8), with unique minimizer0 and true initial\ndistance P_j^+. Equation(10) gives V_h=(P_j^+)^2-A^2 eta^2, proving\nnecessity. At h_j<=1 the displayed screen is automatically satisfied.\n\n## 4. Departure I: a nonzero-moment path out of the balanced word\n\nWrite r2=sqrt2, u2=r2-1=1/lambda. For0<=t<=1/100 set\n\n a=r2-t, b=2+3t, c=a-1=u2-t, B=b-1=1+3t,\n h^I(t)=(a,b,a,1+lambda,a,b,a),\n P=1+2a+b=lambda^2+t,\n A=lambda^3+2t=2P+lambda.                              (12)\n\nThis is one selected symmetric physical path. Its equality of entries and\nits fixed middle step are not restrictions on any competing certificate.\nAt t=0 the actual word is obtained by three levels of the accepted balanced\nprimitive join: singleton sqrt2, three-step(sqrt2,2,sqrt2), then(12).\nIts capacity is lambda^3 and the embedded zero remainders remain zero.\n\nFor every t>0 in the displayed interval its capacity exceeds lambda^3.\nNevertheless it is excluded in the ENTIRE original cone, as shown below.\n\n### 4.1 Both cheap screens pass strictly\n\nFor the center j=3, both shifted side sums in(11) equal P, and\n\n P^2-lambda A=2(lambda^2-lambda)t+t^2>0.\n\nEvery step equal to a has a<3/2 and A>14. Its shifted side sums satisfy\nP_j^+D_j^+>=P_j^++D_j^+-1=A-a>A/2>A(a-1).\nAt either step b, the smaller side sum exceeds2 and the other exceeds10:\nlambda-t>2 and A-a-b>14-3/2-203/100>10. Thus their product exceeds20,\nwhereas A(b-1)<15*(103/100)<20. This checks every index of this word.\n\nThe absolute unit-quadratic terminal product is lambda c^4 B^2. At t=0,\nA lambda c^4 B^2=1. Its logarithmic derivative for0<=t<=1/100 is\n\n 2/A-4/c+6/B <1/7-8+6=-13/7<0,\n\nusing A>14, 0<c<1/2, B>=1. Hence A|v_7|<1 and chi>0 whenever t>0.\nIn particular a rank7 REAL equality remainder(6) exists; signs remain\nthe essential condition, rather than the unit-quadratic moment.\n\n### 4.2 One exact quadrilateral and all eight actual queries\n\nDefine\n\n R2=lambda(1-t-t^2/2)/(lambda-t)\n       =[c lambda^2+(lambda/2)(2-a^2)]/(a+1),\n U=(1,0), V=(-1/2,sqrt(R2-1/4)),\n G4=-(lambda U+V)/a,\n K=conv{U,G4,V,-B V}.                                  (13)\n\nThe two expressions for R2 agree because lambda u2=1, a+1=lambda-t,\nand2-a^2=2sqrt2*t-t^2. We have R2<=1, with equality only at t=0.\nFor a useful lower bound, since7/5<sqrt2<3/2,\n\n R2=1-[(1-u2)t+t^2/2]/(1-u2 t)\n       >1-(61/100)t >=9939/10000>1/4.                  (14)\n\nIndeed (1-u2)+t/2<3/5+1/200 and1-u2t>199/200; their ratio is less\nthan121/199<61/100. Thus U,V are independent. The positive relation\nlambda U+a G4+V=0 makes0 interior to their triangle, hence interior to K.\nFunction(8) consequently has unique minimizer0.\n\nStart at x_0=P U. The actual queries and gradients are\n\n x_i=(1+sum_(k=i)^2 h_k)U, g_i=U, 0<=i<=3,\n x_4=-lambda U,             g_4=G4,\n x_5=V,                    g_5=V,\n x_6=-B V,                 g_6=-B V,\n x_7=c B V,                g_7=c B V.                  (15)\n\nAn empty sum in the first line is zero. Every GD equality follows directly\nfrom(12)--(13); in particular -lambda U-a G4=V. Since0<cB<103/200<1,\nthe terminal belongs to the segment from0 to V and therefore to K.\nThe true initial distance is exactly P, not a surrogate radius.\n\nHere is the full normal check. For0<=i<=3, y_i is a nonnegative multiple\nof U, and\n\n <U,G4>=-(lambda-1/2)/a<0,\n <U,V>=-1/2,\n <U,-B V>=B/2<=103/200<1=<U,U>.\n\nSo every prefix normal supports all vertices. The only other nonzero\nnormal is y_4=(-c lambda U+V)/a. Its product with V-G4 is\n\n [-c lambda^2+lambda(2-a^2)<U,V>+(a+1)R2]/a^2=0.       (16)\n\nUse this equality to compare the remaining vertices:\n\n <y_4,U-G4>=<y_4,U-V>\n       =[-3c lambda/2-1/2-R2]/a<0,\n <y_4,-B V-G4>=-(B+1)<y_4,V>\n       =-(B+1)(R2+c lambda/2)/a<0.\n\nThe normal comparison with G4 itself is zero. At5,6,7 the normals are\nzero because x_i=g_i. This proves every query in(15) is a projection onto\nthe SAME static K. Formula(9) is the exact value of every one of its\noriginal interpolation residuals; all56 finite directed residuals and all\noptimum residuals have the required sign. No terminal or remote row is\nmissing. Values are explicitly f_i=<x_i,g_i>-||g_i||^2/2.\n\n### 4.3 A finite strict negative-energy bound, not just a derivative\n\nEquation(10) gives the exact energy\n\n V_(h^I(t))=P^2-A^2 c^2 B^2 R2.                         (17)\n\nFor0<t<=1/100 set X=(A/P)cB. Since A/P=lambda-u2*t/P,\n\n X=1+(3-lambda)t-3lambda t^2-(u2*t/P)cB.\n\nUse lambda<5/2, P>5, u2<1/2, cB<3/5, and t<=1/100 to obtain\n\n X>1+(1/2-3/40-3/50)t\n     =1+(73/200)t>1+(9/25)t.\n\nCombining this with(14), all factors being positive, yields\n\n X^2 R2>(1+(18/25)t)(1-(61/100)t)\n        =1+(11/100)t-(549/1250)t^2\n        >1+t/10.\n\nThe last strict inequality follows from t<=1/100, since\n11/100-549/125000=13201/125000>1/10. Therefore\n\n V_(h^I(t))<-P^2 t/10<0,  EVERY0<t<=1/100.              (18)\n\nAt t=0 the same actual construction has energy exactly zero, R2=1,\nchi=0, and the center cut is an equality. All formulas stay defined there.\nThe positive interval passes BOTH cheap screens but has a negative full\nprimitive energy. Thus no choice among all56 coefficients and no nonzero\nor higher-rank PSD remainder can certify ANY point of this interval.\n\n## 5. Departure II: an asymmetric subunit path from a full-rank remainder\n\nThe second, genuinely different physical construction is the affine path\n\n h^II(t)=(1+t/2, 1+3t/2, 1-t/20, 1+23t/10,\n           1+t/2, 1+3t/2, 1+4t/5),  0<=t<=1,\n A(t)=8+(141/20)t.                                     (19)\n\nFor t>0, the third step is subunit, all steps are positive, and the word\nis asymmetric. At t=0 it is the all-ones word. We first give its ACTUAL\nfull-rank starting certificate rather than assuming feasibility.\n\n### 5.1 The exact starting array and complete PSD remainder\n\nFor i=0,...,6 set\n\n Lambda_(i,i+1)=2(i+1)^2,\n Lambda_(i+1,i)=2(i+1)(i+2),\n\nand set every other off-diagonal entry and every diagonal to zero.\nThese specify ALL56 entries. At node0 the outflow minus inflow is-2;\nat each node i=1,...,6 it is\n2[(i+1)^2+i(i+1)-(i+1)(i+2)-i^2]=-2;\nat node7 it is112-98=14. Thus the original flow holds for h_i=1.\n\nFor a unit step the quadratic part of Q_(i+1,i) is\n(||g_i||^2+||g_(i+1)||^2)/2, and\n\n Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i)=||g_(i+1)||^2-<g_i,g_(i+1)>.\n\nAfter adding2sum_(i=0)^6(i+1)Q_(i+1,i) to V_h, the value terms cancel,\nand the quadratic is\nsum_(i=0)^6(i+1)(||g_i||^2+||g_(i+1)||^2)-56||g_7||^2.\nAdd2sum_(i=0)^6(i+1)^2[Q_(i,i+1)+Q_(i+1,i)]. The coefficient of g_0^2\nis1; for1<=i<=6 it is2i+1+2i^2=i^2+(i+1)^2; at7 it is-49+98=49.\nEvery cross coefficient is-2(i+1)^2. Hence the ENTIRE remainder is\n\n V_h+sum Lambda_ij Q_ij\n       =sum_(i=0)^6(i+1)^2||g_i-g_(i+1)||^2.             (20)\n\nIt is PSD on R8, has rank7 and kernel exactly the common-gradient line.\nThe starting support is only a concrete initialization; it is not imposed\non any later certificate in our exclusion of the path.\n\n### 5.2 Every global cut and the unit-quadratic screen along the path\n\nHere |v_7|=(207/4000)t^7, so throughout0<=t<=1,\n\n A|v_7| <= (301/20)(207/4000)=62307/80000<1.             (21)\n\nIn particular chi>0 everywhere, including the unit-step endpoint t=0.\nNo zero terminal product is incorrectly inverted.\n\nFor each superunit coordinate, the exact cut margin\nP_j^+D_j^+-A(h_j-1) is in the following table. Index2 has h_2<=1 and\nthus passes its cut without this formula.\n\n j | exact margin\n --|------------------------------------------------------\n 0 | 7+(51/20)t-(141/40)t^2\n 1 | 12+(11/10)t-(161/20)t^2\n 3 | 16+(3/5)t-(2151/200)t^2\n 4 | 15+(81/4)t+(25/4)t^2\n 5 | 12+(23/10)t-(271/40)t^2\n 6 | 7-(3/20)t-(141/25)t^2\n\nEach row except4 is concave in t and hence at least the lesser of its\ntwo endpoint values on[0,1]. The values at1 for rows0,1,3,5,6 are,\nrespectively,241/40,101/20,1169/200,301/40,121/100, all positive;\ntheir values at0 are positive as displayed. Row4 is at least15.\nConsequently EVERY original global cut holds strictly along this path.\n\n### 5.3 The complete last-pair static witness\n\nFor17/20<=t<=1 define\n\n P=1+sum_(i<5)h_i=6+(19/4)t,\n B=h_5-1=(3/2)t, C=h_6-1=(4/5)t,\n U_tail=B+C+2=A-P,\n H_def=BC[1+B(P+A)/P^2]-1.                              (22)\n\nIn this interval P>10, P<11, P+A>=2403/100>24, B>=51/40>5/4, and\nBC>=867/1000. Therefore\n\n H_def>(867/1000)(1+30/121)-1\n       =9917/121000>2/25.                              (23)\n\nFirst suppose BC<1. Set\n\n kappa=B^2 C+U_tail(1-BC)>0,\n tau_tri=B^2 C/kappa in(0,1),\n u=(1,0), w=(tau_tri,sqrt(tau_tri(1-tau_tri))),\n v=-(B u+w)/(1+C),  K=conv{u,v,w}.                     (24)\n\nThe positive relation B u+(1+C)v+w=0 and independence of u,w put0\nstrictly inside the triangle. Its static function(8) has unique minimizer0.\nStart at x_0=P u. All original prefix queries0,...,5 have gradient u and\npositions x_i=(1+sum_(k=i)^4 h_k)u. The remaining actual data are\n\n x_6=-B u, g_6=v;    x_7=w, g_7=w.                    (25)\n\nThe last update holds because -B u-(1+C)v=w. The prefix includes the\noriginal subunit step h_2; none of its queries is omitted. The true initial\ndistance is P. Give every query its actual value x_i dot g_i-||g_i||^2/2.\n\nPrefix normals are nonnegative multiples of u, and compare negatively\nwith w-u and v-u because <u,w>=tau_tri<1 and\n<u,v>=-(B+tau_tri)/(1+C)<0. At6, y_6=(-BC u+w)/(1+C). Direct expansion\nusing <u,w>=||w||^2=tau_tri gives\n\n <y_6,w-v>=(-B^2 C+kappa*tau_tri)/(1+C)^2=0,\n <y_6,u-v>=-BC(1-tau_tri)/(1+C)<0.                     (26)\n\nThe coefficient of tau_tri in the first numerator is\nB+1+C+1-BC(1+C+1)=U_tail-BC(C+2)=kappa;\nthe equality to kappa follows also by expanding(24). The terminal normal\nis zero. Thus these are projections onto ONE static K. Equation(9) supplies\nALL56 directed finite residuals and all optimum comparisons with their\nactual nonpositive signs.\n\nUsing(10), the exact primitive energy is\n\n V_h=P^2-A^2 tau_tri=-U_tail*P^2*H_def/kappa.            (27)\n\nFor an algebra check,\nA^2 B^2 C-P^2 kappa\n=U_tail{BC[P^2+B(P+A)]-P^2}.\nFurthermore kappa=U_tail-BC(C+2)<U_tail, so(23) and P>10 imply\n\n V_h<-P^2 H_def<-8.                                    (28)\n\nIf BC>=1, including the singular boundary BC=1, instead use the scalar\nstatic function F(x)=x^2/2 for x<=1 and F(x)=x-1/2 for x>=1.\nIts derivative min(x,1) is continuous, nondecreasing and1-Lipschitz;\nits unique minimizer is0. Start at x_0=P. The original prefix through5\nhas gradient1 and query5 equals1. Query6 is-B with gradient-B, and\nquery7 is BC>=1 with gradient1. Every query is an actual query of this\nONE function. Their values again equal x_i g_i-g_i^2/2. Formula(9) is\nvalid by expansion: the only nonzero normals are nonnegative scalars\nat gradients1, which support every actual gradient and the optimum0.\nThus all ordered residuals, including terminal and optimum ones, have\nthe correct sign. Equation(10) now gives\n\n V_h=P^2-[P+(1+B)(1+C)]^2<-21<-8,                      (29)\n\nbecause P>10 and(1+B)(1+C)>1. The true initial distance remains P.\nNo degenerate triangle is used at BC=1, and no formal coordinate is\nremoved or identified at that boundary.\n\nTogether(28)--(29) give an exact strict original-cone obstruction for\nEVERY17/20<=t<=1. Before that interval the capacity is at most\n\n A(17/20)=5597/400<14<lambda^3,\n\nwhereas A(1)=301/20>lambda^3. Therefore every point on the WHOLE selected\npath(19) with capacity above lambda^3 lies in the excluded interval.\nThe full-rank initialization and the passing screens do not reach a seed.\n\n## 6. What these two obstructions leave open\n\nThe first construction genuinely crosses from chi=0 into chi>0, while\nincreasing physical capacity above lambda^3. The second starts with the\nexplicit full-rank original certificate(20), permits a subunit physical\nstep, is asymmetric, and reaches capacity301/20. No prescribed rank-one rate mechanism was reused.\n\nThese are restrictions on TWO declared physical families only. They do not\nprove full seven-step impossibility, a maximum-capacity theorem, or the\noriginal all-h/all-n asymptotic profile. {"id": "f34441b76f1ece11", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T05:51:49.985892+00:00", "author": "max4", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "UNVERIFIED same-cycle shortcut counterexamples: neither the initial quadratic eigenmode, a sum-only/order-free diffusion rule, nor a small-gap lower contraction bound transports the critical cycle's midpoint energy through its original suffix.", "evidence": "All notation and proofs are reproduced jointly in81c766c8e8e55ca5, not used as accepted premises. Direct construction: for m>=8 set beta=1-1/m,c=(1+sqrt2)(1+1/m),a=beta/c,d=1+beta*c^(m-2),N=ceil(pi sqrt(d/6)),mu=sin^2(pi/(2N)),z=cos(pi/(2N)),v_i=cos(pi*(i+1/2)/N). On the one static cycle set (Dx)_i=x_i-x_(i+1 mod N), F=||(Dx)_+||^2/8, T_t=x-(t/4)D^T(Dx)_+, and L_i=(e_i-e_(i+1))(e_i-e_(i+1))^T. The exact original first child is a positive multiple of v because every child factor1-mu*t_j>0, while5/4<mu*d<3/2 makes the central state a negative multiple of v. At X=-v only closing edge N-1 is positive. Hence gradF(X) is supported at endpoints0,N-1 and is not -mu*v, refuting scalar eigenmode continuation at the first suffix query. Let s=t_0=1+a and t=t_1=1+beta. Both are below2 and above8mu, so either first step from X has exactly active edges N-1,0,N-2. Put H0=L_(N-1)/4 and H1=(L_(N-1)+L_0+L_(N-2))/4. Direct expansion gives T_t T_s X-T_s T_t X=(t-s)(H0-H1)X. This is nonzero: t>s, and both previously inactive edge differences are -4mu*z, so (H0-H1)X=mu*z*(e_0-e_1+e_(N-2)-e_(N-1))!=0. Thus equal total steps do not imply equal actual states. Also (H0 H1-H1 H0)e_1=(e_(N-1)-e_0)/16!=0. Finally on this SAME graph take w=e_(N-1)-e_0. Its only positive difference is the closing one, of value2; consequently gradF(w)=w/2. The ACTUAL level1 step t=2-1/m gives T_t(w)=w/(2m) and F(T_t(w))=F(w)/(4m^2), despite the exact radial gap mu tending to zero. This last point tests a purported gap-only lower estimate; it does not claim that the selected suffix equals w. These tests introduce no second graph/weight/start proposal. None refutes the actual missing claim liminf_m N||[Psi_(m-1)(-Dv)]_+||^2>0.", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"proof_attempt_id": "81c766c8e8e55ca5", "plan_id": "26941c066a841f3f", "target_claim": "Continue the low-mode product; replace nonlinear dynamics by total step mass; infer slow damping from the graph gap alone.", "status": "refuted", "actual_terminal_lower_claim_status": "not_refuted"}, "glossary": {"X": "The exact amplitude-normalized post-central state -v.", "H0": "Single-closing-edge sector Hessian L_(N-1)/4.", "H1": "Next strict sector Hessian (L_(N-1)+L_0+L_(N-2))/4.", "w": "Mean-zero edge-localized test vector e_(N-1)-e_0 on the same selected graph.", "s,t": "The actual level0 and level1 steps t_0,t_1, compared in two orders only to test a proof shortcut."}}

{"id": "2a2831acc4633001", "timestamp_utc": "2026-10-01T06:27:00.692787+00:00", "author": "max", "kind": "counterexample", "claim": "max57 scoped array counterexample: for the legal nonbasic input h=(3/2,3/2) with its explicit full positive M=0 certificate, every convex four-corner lift using the same block weights at sources and targets and nonnegative child scales fails full PSD. This is NOT a counterexample to the physical inflated word.", "evidence": "UNVERIFIED joint derivation, part of 9be1a10ee154498a. No formal gate is authorized.\n\n## 1. Original coordinates, counts, and the fixed child\n\nNormalize smoothness to1. For any word w=(w_0,...,w_(n-1)), write\nS_w=sum w_i, A_w=1+S_w, and retain all n+1 formal gradients g_i and values\nf_i. Put x_i=x_0-sum_(t<i)w_t g_t and\n\n Q_ij=f_i-f_j+<g_i,x_j-x_i>+||g_i-g_j||^2/2,\n V_w=2sum_(i<n)w_i(f_i-f_n)+sum_(i<n)w_i(1-w_i)||g_i||^2\n                                      -S_w(S_w+1)||g_n||^2.\n\nLet e be the all-ones column, T_ii=1, T_it=w_t for t<i, T_it=0 for t>i,\nomega=(-2w_0,...,-2w_(n-1),2S_w)^T, and\n\n D=diag(w_0(2-w_0),...,w_(n-1)(2-w_(n-1)),-S_w(S_w+2)).\n\nA full certificate consists of Lambda_ii=0, Lambda_ij>=0 for every\nordered i!=j, L=diag(Lambda e)-Lambda, L^T e=omega, and\n\n M=D+(LT+T^T L^T)/2 positive semidefinite.                    (1)\n\nHere sym(B)=(B+B^T)/2; Gram is the matrix of pairwise gradient inner\nproducts, and the matrix pairing in (2) is trace(M^T Gram).\n\nThe accepted original-coordinate identity is\n\n V_w+sum_(i!=j)Lambda_ij Q_ij=<M,Gram(g_0,...,g_n)>.          (2)\n\nIt holds as a polynomial in every formal gradient and value, without\nidentifying coordinates. The signs Q_ij<=0 hold on an actual trajectory\nof a single convex differentiable1-smooth function. Consequently (1)\nis a sufficient primitive certificate on all such trajectories.\n\nIn this manuscript only, set a=sqrt(2), C=1+a, d=a-1=1/C. The fixed child\nis q=(a). Its two-node certificate is\n\n Lambda^q=[[0,2],[2C,0]], M^q=0.                             (3)\n\nIndeed its flow is (-2a,2a). The diagonal entries of D+sym(LT) are\na(2-a)+2(1-a)=2-a^2=0 and -a(a+2)+2C=0. Twice the off-diagonal is\n-2+2C(a-1)=0. Thus every entry of the full matrix is checked.\n\nThe arbitrary input h has length r>=1, h_i>=1, capacity A=1+sum h_i,\nand its given full certificate (Lambda^h,M^h). Define\n\n beta_i=1+C(h_i-1)=C h_i-a,\n H=(a,beta_0,a,beta_1,...,a,beta_(r-1),a).                   (4)\n\nBlock i has physical node2i (its start) and node2i+1 (its endpoint),\nfor i=0,...,r. All 2r+2 gradients remain independent formal coordinates.\nThere are r+1 child steps and r bridges, so the physical length is\nNphys=2r+1 and there are (2r+2)(2r+1) ordered multiplier slots. Since beta_i>=1,\nevery step is positive. Direct summation, using C-a=1, gives\n\n 1+sum H=1+(r+1)a+C sum h_i-r a=C A.                       (5)\n\nThis is a fixed-child statement only. Neither arbitrary-child closure\nnor an exponent-based restriction on arbitrary seed capacity follows.\n\n## 2. A nonbasic legal input, with its complete certificate\n\nTake h=(3/2,3/2), A=4, and\n\n Lambda^h=[[0,1,1/2],[7/2,0,17/2],[1,14,0]].                (6)\n\nIts six ordered entries are positive and its flow is (-3,-3,6).\nWith T=[[1,0,0],[3/2,1,0],[3/2,3/2,1]] and D=diag(3/4,3/4,-15),\nthe three diagonal entries of LT are (-3/4,-3/4,15). The sums of its\nopposite off-diagonal entries are\n\n (LT)_01+(LT)_10=(-1-3/4)+7/4=0,\n (LT)_02+(LT)_20=-1/2+1/2=0,\n (LT)_12+(LT)_21=-17/2+(3/2+7)=0.\n\nHence the full M^h is zero. A length2 primitive binary tree built from\nempty leaves contains a length1 empty-leaf join, whose step is a by the\naccepted join equation. Neither step in (6) equals a. Thus this is a\nnonbasic input for the present test, with all its certificate data checked.\n\nIts physical inflated word is\n\n H=(a,b,a,b,a), b=(3+a)/2, capacity4C.                       (7)\n\nThe unit-quadratic trajectory from x_0=1 has scalar gradient column\n\n v=(1,-d,1/2,-d/2,1/4,-d/4)^T.                            (8)\n\nIn particular its primitive energy is zero: (4C)^2 v_5^2=1.\nEquation(8) will test candidate remainder matrices; changing a coordinate\nof (8) does NOT automatically give another actual function trajectory.\n\n## 3. Mechanism1: every cross-block ordered pair is retained\n\nFor arbitrary input h choose t_i in[0,1], i=0,...,r, and let the two\nweights in block i be pi_i=(t_i,1-t_i). For every i!=j and a0,b0 in{0,1},\nset the cross-block output coefficient to\n\n Lambda^mix_(2i+a0,2j+b0)=C Lambda^h_ij pi_(i,a0) pi_(j,b0). (9)\n\nInside block i use mu_i Lambda^q, where, with S=A-1,\n\n mu_i=1-C h_i t_i/a for i<r,\n mu_r=1+C S t_r/a.                                        (10)\n\nRequire mu_i>=0. These are exact formulas on the original physical\nindices. They neither replace a block by an oracle nor assume its\ngradients equal. If all original off-diagonal entries and all weights\nare positive and mu_i>0, every physical ordered coefficient is positive.\n\nThe cross-block flow at node2i is C t_i omega^h_i and at node2i+1 is\nC(1-t_i)omega^h_i. The child adds (-2a mu_i,2a mu_i). Thus (10) gives\nthe required start flow -2a. For i<r the endpoint flow is\n-2C h_i+2a=-2beta_i; for i=r it is 2CS+2a=2(CA-1).\nTherefore (9)--(10) have the exact original physical flow. Nonnegativity\nand flow alone leave the full matrix\n\n Mmix=D_H+sym(Lmix T_H)                                    (11)\n\nto be tested on the entire physical space.\n\n### 3.1 The entire convex block-distribution family fails on (6)\n\nFor the input (6), admissibility gives\n\n 0<=t_0,t_1<=2a/(3C), 0<=t_2<=1.                           (12)\n\nLet s_0=1,s_1=1/2,s_2=1/4 and let m_i be the pi_i-weighted average of\nthe two entries of (8) in block i. This is an algebraic average used\nonly to evaluate the candidate matrix, not an interpolating virtual jet:\n\n m_i=s_i(a t_i-d).\n\nThe bounds (12) imply\n\n -d<=m_0<=d/3,  -d/2<=m_1<=d/6.                           (13)\n\nFor any exact-flow array on the word(7), v^T M v=0, by (2) evaluated\non the actual unit quadratic(8). If M is PSD, it must therefore satisfy\nMv=0. Since T_H v=e and L e=0, this forces\n\n L^T v=-2 T_H^(-T) D_H v=:\n tquad=(4,-7,8+3a,-(25+6a)/2,(32+15a)/2,-(17+15a)/2)^T.   (14)\n\nFor a direct check, D_H has entries\n(2d,(1-2a)/4,2d,(1-2a)/4,2d,1-16C^2).\nStarting at the last coordinate, (T_H^T tquad)_i equals\ntquad_i+H_i sum_(j>i)tquad_j for i<5, and tquad_5 for i=5;\nthese are precisely -2(D_H)_ii v_i. The successive suffix sums are\n0,-4,3,-5-3a,15/2,-(17+15a)/2, read from index0 to index5.\n\nIn (9), summing the two coordinates of (Lmix^T v) in block i cancels\nthe internal child contribution and gives C[(L^h)^T m]_i. Summing (14)\nin the same blocks gives (-3,-9/2,15/2). Hence PSD would require\n\n (3/2)m_0-(7/2)m_1-m_2=-3d,\n -m_0+12m_1-14m_2=-(9/2)d.                               (15)\n\nEliminate m_2 from these two explicit equalities to obtain\n\n -22m_0+61m_1=(75/2)d.                                   (16)\n\nBut (13) bounds its left side by\n\n 22d+(61/6)d=(193/6)d < (225/6)d=(75/2)d.                  (17)\n\nThe shortfall is at least (16/3)d>0. This is a collective block-total\nkernel obstruction, independent of t_2. Thus EVERY array of the form\n(9)--(10), not merely a particular set of weights, fails PSD on the\nvalid nonbasic input(6). This is a theorem about the declared array\nfamily only; it does not restrict arbitrary physical arrays.\n\n### 3.2 A fully positive instance with an exact negative remainder\n\nSet all t_i=1/4 in (9). Then\n\n mu_0=mu_1=(10-3a)/16>0,  mu_2=(14+3a)/8>0.                (18)\n\nEquations(6),(9),(18) specify every one of the30 ordered output entries,\nand all are strictly positive. For an explicit full-space failure, let\ne0=(1,0,0,0,0,0)^T and use the formal scalar gradient column v-e0.\n\nThe total outgoing coefficient at node0 is\n\n 2mu_0+C(1/4)(3/2)=13/8,\n\nso (Mmix)_00=2d+(1-a)(13/8)=3d/8.\nFor this choice the averages m_i=s_i(1-3a/4) satisfy\nsum_j Lambda^h_(j,0)m_j=2(1-3a/4). Hence\n\n (Lmix^T v)_0\n =4mu_0+(C/4)[3/2-2(1-3a/4)]=(25-4a)/8.\n\nBecause sum_i(Lmix^T v)_i=0 and the first step is a,\n(T_H^T Lmix^T v)_0=(1-a)(Lmix^T v)_0. Therefore\n\n (Mmix v)_0=2d-d(25-4a)/16=(1+3a)/16.\n\nUsing v^T Mmix v=0 now gives the exact value\n\n (v-e0)^T Mmix(v-e0)=3d/8-2(1+3a)/16=-1/2.                (19)\n\nThe changed formal vector is not asserted to interpolate a smooth convex\nfunction. Equation(19) refutes this full positive-flow multiplier array,\nnot physical feasibility of(7). In particular it is not outcome(b).\n\n", "verifiable": true, "status": "unverified", "fact_id": null, "links": {"joint_finding": "9be1a10ee154498a", "plan_id": "a11141038bb37133", "branch_id":
